Both now and ever...": and the second Dismissal Troparion: **"Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God, we have been granted immortal life by Thy Resurrection..."** (*Συνταφέντες σοι διὰ τοῦ Βαπτίσματος*) once, then the Dismissal.

64.    On Sunday at Matins, after Psalm 50, the Litē, the Trisagion Prayers, the...[^437]

---

35. On this Saturday, as well as on Palm Sunday and Saint Thomas Sunday, memorial services with boiled wheat [kolyvo] are forbidden. The names of the departed may be commemorated, if so desired, at the end of the Divine Liturgy, as we chant only the four mortuary Troparia: "With the souls of the righteous departed...", etc.
[******] According to the ancient order presented in the Triodion, the aforementioned 5 Stichera are each doubled and therefore sung for 10.
[*******] Similarly, according to the ancient order in the Triodion, the Doxastikon "Today the grace" is chanted twice: once after "Glory..." and again after "Both now and ever...", both at "Lord, I have cried" and at the Aposticha.
[********] See the preceding note.

# Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Matins (§ 64)

## Κυριακὴ τῶν Βαΐων: Ὄρθρος (§ 64)

64.    Τῇ Κυριακῇ εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον, μετὰ τὸν Ν' ψαλμόν, ἡ Λιτὴ τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον *Συνταφέντες σοι* καὶ ὁ Ἑξάψαλμος. Εἰς τὸ *Θεὸς Κύριος*, τὰ Ἀπολυτίκια ὡς εἰς τὸν Ἑσπερινόν, εἶτα τὸ Ψαλτήριον καὶ ὁ Πολυέλεος, τὰ Καθίσματα κατὰ σειράν, τὸ δὲ τελευταῖον δὶς τὸ α' Ἀντίφωνον τῶν Ἀναβαθμῶν τοῦ δ' ἤχου, τὸ Προκείμενον *Ἐκ στόματος νηπίων*, τὸ *Πᾶσα πνοὴ* καὶ τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον τοῦ Ὄρθρου· *Ἀνάστασιν Χριστοῦ* οὐ λέγομεν, ἀλλ' εὐθὺς ὁ Ν' ψαλμὸς χῦμα, Δόξα, *Σήμερον ὁ Χριστὸς εἰσέρχεται [ἐν πόλει Βηθανίᾳ]*, Καὶ νῦν, τὸ αὐτό, ὁ Στίχος *Ἐλέησόν με ὁ Θεός* καὶ τὸ *Σήμερον ἡ χάρις*, ὁ Κανὼν εἰς ς' ἄνευ Στίχων, εἰμὴ *Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν*, εἰς τὰ τελευταῖα δύο Τροπάρια· ἀπὸ γ' Ὠδῆς, ἡ Ὑπακοὴ *Μετὰ κλάδων ὑμνήσαντες* χῦμα· ἀφ' ς' τὸ Κοντάκιον [*Τῷ θρόνῳ ἐν Οὐρανῷ*], ὁ Οἶκος [*Ἐπειδὴ ᾍδην ἔδησας*], τὸ Μηνολόγιον τῶν Ἁγίων τῆς ἡμέρας καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τοῦ Τριῳδίου, αἱ Καταβασίαι *Ὤφθησαν αἱ πηγαὶ τῆς ἀβύσσου*, καὶ ψάλλεται ἡ θ' Ὠδή, εἶτα τὸ *Ἅγιος Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν* ἐκ γ' ὡς Ἐξαποστειλάριον καὶ ἀναγινώσκεται μετ' αὐτὸ ὑπὸ τοῦ Προεστῶτος ἡ Εὐχὴ τῶν Βαΐων, καὶ εὐθὺς ὁ α' Χορὸς τὸ *Πᾶσα πνοὴ* καὶ τὰ Στιχηρὰ τῶν Αἴνων³⁶ εἰς δ', Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Πρὸ ἓξ ἡμερῶν τοῦ Πάσχα*, Δοξολογία Μεγάλη καὶ τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον *Συνταφέντες σοι*.

---

36. Ἐν τῇ Μ. Ἐκκλησίᾳ, ψαλλομένων τῶν Αἴνων, οἱ χοροστατοῦντες Ἀρχιερεῖς ἀσπάζονται πρῶτον τὴν Εἰκόνα τῶν Βαΐων, εἶτα προσερχόμενοι λαμβάνουσι παρὰ τοῦ Πατριάρχου τὰ βαΐα τῶν φοινίκων, ἀσπαζόμενοι τὴν δεξιὰν αὐτοῦ, ὡς καὶ πάντες οἱ ὀφφικιάλιοι.

# Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Matins (§ 64)

## Palm Sunday: Matins (§ 64)

... Dismissal Troparion: **"Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God..."** (*Συνταφέντες σοι*), and the Six Psalms. At **"God is the Lord"** (*Θεὸς Κύριος*): the Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers [**"In confirming the common Resurrection..."** twice, "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Buried with Thee through Baptism..."** once]. Then the Psalter [Kathisma 2, Pss. 9–16 LXX, and Kathisma 3, Pss. 17–23 LXX] and the Polyeleos [Kathisma 19, Pss. 134–135 LXX]. The Sessional Hymns [Kathismata] in order from the Triodion: after the first Kathisma: **"In spirit carrying branches, with cleansed souls let us praise Christ in faith like children..."** (*Μετὰ κλάδων νοητῶς*) and another Sessional Hymn: **"Thou didst raise Lazarus from the tomb after four days, O Lord..."** (*Τετραήμερον ἤγειρας*); after the second Kathisma: **"O Christ our Savior, Thou didst shed tears in secret over Thy friend Lazarus lying dead..."** (*Δάκρυα προέχεας, Σωτήρ*) and **"O ye peoples and nations, rejoice together!..."** (*Λαοὶ καὶ τὰ ἔθνη συνευφράνθητε*). After the Polyeleos: the festal Sessional Hymn: **"He Who sits upon the throne of the Cherubim, for our sake sits upon a foal..."** (*Ὁ ἐπὶ θρόνου Χερουβίμ*) twice. The First Antiphon of the Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of Tone 4: **"From my youth up many passions have warred against me..."** (*Ἐκ νεότητός μου*). The Prokimenon: **"Out of the mouths of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise"** (*Ἐκ στόματος νηπίων καὶ θηλαζόντων κατηρτίσω αἶνον*, Ps. 8:2 [LXX]); verse: **"O Lord, our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth!"** (*Κύριε ὁ Κύριος ἡμῶν, ὡς θαυμαστὸν τὸ ὄνομά σου*, Ps. 8:1 [LXX]). **"Let every breath praise the Lord"** (*Πᾶσα πνοή*), and the Matins Gospel: Matthew 21:1–11, 15–17 (The Triumphal Entry into Jerusalem and the Children in the Temple). We do not chant **"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"**, but immediately Psalm 50 is read plain [chyma]. "Glory...": **"Today Christ entereth the city of Bethany..."** (*Σήμερον ὁ Χριστὸς εἰσέρχεται ἐν πόλει Βηθανίᾳ*); "Both now and ever...": the same; then the verse: **"Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy..."** (Ps. 50:1 [LXX]), and the Idiomelon: **"Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together..."** (*Σήμερον ἡ χάρις τοῦ Ἁγίου Πνεύματος*). The Canon: **"The springs of the deep appeared dry..."** (*Ὤφθησαν αἱ πηγαὶ τῆς ἀβύσσου*) in six without verses, except "Glory... Both now and ever..." at the last two Troparia. After the Third Ode, the Hypakoe: **"Praising with branches, first the peoples cried aloud with hymns of praise..."** (*Μετὰ κλάδων ὑμνήσαντες*) read plain. After the Sixth Ode, the Kontakion: **"Upborne upon the throne in heaven and upon a foal on earth, O Christ God..."** (*Τῷ θρόνῳ ἐν οὐρανῷ*), the Oikos: **"Since Thou didst bind Hades, O Immortal One..."** (*Ἐπειδὴ ᾍδην ἔδησας*), the Menologion of the Saints of the day from the Menaion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion. The Katavasiai: **"The springs of the deep appeared dry..."** (*Ὤφθησαν αἱ πηγαὶ τῆς ἀβύσσου*). In place of **"More honorable than the Cherubim"**, the Ninth Ode of the Canon is chanted in its entirety with its Megalynaria. Then: **"Holy is the Lord our God"** (*Ἅγιος Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν*) three times in place of the Exaposteilarion. Immediately after this, the Prayer over the Palms [and Branches] is read by the Superior [Proestos]. Directly the First Choir chants: **"Let every breath praise the Lord"** (*Πᾶσα πνοή*) and the appointed verses of the Praises [Lauds]³⁶ with the four Idiomela: [**"O Lord, great multitudes spread their...**[^438]

---

36. In the Great Church, while the Praises are being chanted, the presiding Hierarchs venerate first the Icon of the Palms, and then approaching the Patriarch they kiss his right hand and receive from him the palms and olive branches, as do all the officiating clergy and dignitaries.

# Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Divine Liturgy and Sunday Evening Vespers with Compline (§§ 65, 66)

## Κυριακὴ τῶν Βαΐων: Θεία Λειτουργία καὶ Ἑσπερινὸς μετὰ Ἀποδείπνου (§§ 65, 66)

65.    Εἰς τὴν Λειτουργίαν, τὰ Ἀντίφωνα τῆς ἑορτῆς καὶ τὸ Εἰσοδικὸν *Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι Κυρίου, Θεὸς Κύριος καὶ ἐπέφανεν ἡμῖν· Σῶσον ἡμᾶς... ὁ ἐπὶ πώλου ὄνου* κτλ. τὰ Ἀπολυτίκια *Τὴν κοινὴν Ἀνάστασιν*, καὶ *Συνταφέντες σοι*, εἶτα τὸ Κοντάκιον *Τῷ θρόνῳ ἐν οὐρανῷ*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, Ἀπόστολος καὶ Εὐαγγέλιον τῆς ἑορτῆς. Εἰς τὸ Ἐξαιρέτως, ὁ Εἱρμὸς τῆς θ' *Θεὸς Κύριος*, Κοινωνικὸν *Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι Κυρίου, Ἀλληλούϊα*. *Τὴν κοινὴν Ἀνάστασιν*, *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, καὶ Ἀπόλυσις.

66.    Τὸ ἑσπέρας [μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακόν], εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* ψάλλομεν τὰ γ' Ἀπόστιχα τῆς χθὲς Ἰδιόμελα *Χαῖρε καὶ εὐφραίνου*, δευτεροῦντες αὐτά, Δόξα, τὸ αὐτό, Καὶ νῦν *Ὁ τοῖς Χερουβὶμ ἐποχούμενος*, Εἴσοδος, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, τὸ Προκείμενον τῆς ἡμέρας, τὰ γ' τῆς σειρᾶς Ἀπόστιχα, Δόξα, *Ἐκ βαΐων καὶ κλάδων*, Καὶ νῦν, *Φοβερὸν τὸ ἐμπεσεῖν*. Μετὰ τὸ *Νῦν ἀπολύεις*, εὐθὺς ψάλλεται ὁ Κανὼν τοῦ Ἀποδείπνου *Ἰωσὴφ τὴν σωφροσύνην* μετὰ Στίχου *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν δόξα σοι*· ἀπ' θ' ὁ Εἱρμὸς *Ἀλλότριον τῶν μητέρων*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ *Θεοτόκε Παρθένε* κτλ. ὡς ἐν ταῖς λοιπαῖς Κυριακαῖς

# Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Divine Liturgy and Sunday Evening Vespers with Compline (§§ 65, 66)

## Palm Sunday: Divine Liturgy and Sunday Evening Vespers with Compline (§§ 65, 66)

... garments in the way..."** (*Πλεῖστος ὄχλος, Κύριε, ἐστρώννυον ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ τὰ ἱμάτια αὐτῶν*), **"When Thou wast about to enter the Holy City, O Lord..."** (*Μέλλοντός σου εἰσιέναι, Κύριε, εἰς τὴν ἁγίαν Πόλιν*), **"Come forth, ye peoples, come forth, ye nations..."** (*Ἐξέλθετε ἔθνη, ἐξέλθετε καὶ λαοί*), **"Before Thy voluntary Passion, O Christ God, intending to assure all of the universal Resurrection..."** (*Πρὸ ἓξ ἡμερῶν τοῦ ἑκουσίου Πάθους σου*)]; "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Six days before the Passover, Jesus came unto Bethany..."** (*Πρὸ ἓξ ἡμερῶν τοῦ Πάσχα*). The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal Troparion: **"Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God..."** (*Συνταφέντες σοι*).

65.    At the Divine Liturgy, the Festal Antiphons of Palm Sunday [First Antiphon: Ps. 114 LXX with refrain "Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us"; Second Antiphon: Ps. 115 LXX with refrain "Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon the foal of an ass, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!"; Third Antiphon: Ps. 117 LXX with the Dismissal Troparion: "In confirming the common Resurrection..."]. The Entrance Hymn [Isodikon]: **"Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord! God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us: Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon the foal of an ass, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!"** (*Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι Κυρίου...*, Ps. 117:26–27 [LXX]). After the Little Entrance, the Dismissal Troparia: **"In confirming the common Resurrection before Thy Passion..."** (*Τὴν κοινὴν Ἀνάστασιν*) and **"Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God..."** (*Συνταφέντες σοι*). Then the Kontakion: **"Upborne upon the throne in heaven and upon a foal on earth, O Christ God..."** (*Τῷ θρόνῳ ἐν οὐρανῷ*). The Trisagion Hymn: **"Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us"** (*Ἅγιος ὁ Θεός*). The Epistle [Apostolos]: Philippians 4:4–9 ("Rejoice in the Lord always; again I will say, rejoice!..."), and the Gospel: John 12:1–18 (The Anointing at Bethany and the Triumphal Entry). At "Especially [among the all-holy]...", the Heirmos of the Ninth Ode: **"God the Lord hath appeared unto us; let us appoint a festival, and rejoicing come, let us magnify Christ with palms and branches, crying aloud with voices of praise: Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord!..."** (*Θεὸς Κύριος καὶ ἐπέφανεν ἡμῖν*). The Communion Hymn [Koinōnikon]: **"Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord. Alleluia!"** (*Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι Κυρίου. Ἀλληλούϊα*, Ps. 117:26 [LXX]). In place of **"We have seen the true Light"**, we chant the Dismissal Troparion: **"In confirming the common Resurrection..."** (*Τὴν κοινὴν Ἀνάστασιν*); then: **"Blessed be the name of the Lord"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal.

66.    On Sunday evening, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 [LXX]), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*, Ps. 140 [LXX]), we chant the three Aposticha Idiomela of yesterday: **"Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion!..."** (*Χαῖρε καὶ εὐφραίνου*) twice, **"Today the Savior cometh unto Jerusalem..."** (*Σήμερον ὁ Σωτὴρ εἰς Ἱερουσαλὴμ ἔρχεται*) twice, and **"O Thou Who sittest upon the Cherubim and art praised by the Seraphim!..."** (*Ὁ τοῖς Χερουβὶμ ἐποχούμενος*) twice. "Glory...": **"Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion!..."**; "Both now and ever...": **"O Thou Who sittest upon the Cherubim..."**. Little Entrance with the censer, **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Great Prokimenon of the day: **"Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; hear me speedily: attend unto my soul, and deliver it!"** (*Μὴ ἀποστρέψῃς τὸ πρόσωπόν σου ἀπὸ τοῦ παιδός σου*, Ps. 68:18–19 [LXX]). Then the three appointed Aposticha: **"Passing from one divine feast to another divine feast, let us hasten, O believers..."** (*Ἐκ βαΐων εἰς βάϊα πιστοί*); verse: **"Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in heaven..."** (Ps. 122:1 [LXX]); **"It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God..."** (*Φοβερὸν τὸ ἐμπεσεῖν εἰς χεῖρας Θεοῦ ζῶντος*, Heb. 10:31); verse: **"Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us..."** (Ps. 122:3 [LXX]); **"O evil and adulterous generation, that hath not kept faith with its husband!..."** (*Γενεὰ πονηρὰ καὶ μοιχαλίς*); "Glory...": **"Passing from one divine feast to another divine feast..."**; "Both now and ever...": **"It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God..."**. After **"Lord, now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace"** (*Νῦν ἀπολύεις*, Luke 2:29), we immediately chant the Canon of Compline: **"Emulating the chaste wisdom of Joseph..."** (*Ἰωσὴφ τὴν σωφροσύνην ζηλώσαντες*) with the verse: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!"**. After the Ninth Ode, the Heirmos: **"Childbirth without corruption is alien to mothers, and virginity is strange to childbearing women..."** (*Ἀλλότριον τῶν μητέρων ἡ παρθενία*), the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion: **"Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos, Mary full of grace, the Lord is with thee..."** (*Θεοτόκε Παρθένε*), and the rest of Small Compline as on other Sundays of Great Lent.[^439]

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday (Matins Opening and Rubrics) (§§ 66 Concluded, 67 Opening)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Δευτέρα εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον (§§ 66 Τέλος, 67 Ἀρχή)

τῆς Μεγάλης Τεσσαρακοστῆς. Εἰς τὴν Συγχώρησιν ψάλλεται τὸ *Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον*, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς . . . ἐρχόμενος ὁ Κύριος ἐπὶ τὸ ἑκούσιον πάθος διὰ τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν* ³⁷, καὶ τὸ *Δι' εὐχῶν*.

**ΜΕΓΑΛΗ ΔΕΥΤΕΡΑ ΕΙΣ ΤΟΝ ΟΡΘΡΟΝ**

67.    Ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεός*, ὁ Προεστὼς *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*, τὸ *Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης τὸ Τρισάγιον, ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Ὅτι σοῦ ἐστιν*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης *Κύριε ἐλέησον* ιβ', *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν* γ' καὶ τοὺς ψαλμοὺς *Ἐπακούσαι σου Κύριος*, κτλ. (ἀναγινωσκομένων τούτων βραδέως, ὁ Ἱερεὺς θυμιᾷ τὸν λαὸν διὰ κατζίου)· μετὰ τὸ Τρισάγιον, *Ὅτι σοῦ ἐστιν*, *Σῶσον Κύριε* κτλ. καὶ ὁ Προϊστάμενος λέγει τὸν Ἑξάψαλμον. Μετὰ τὴν Συναπτήν, ψάλλεται μετὰ τῶν ὡρισμένων αὐτοῦ Στίχων *Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει*, τὸ Μέγα Ἀλληλουϊάριον δίς, καὶ δὶς τὸ μικρὸν καὶ τὸ Τροπάριον *Ἰδοὺ ὁ νυμφίος ἔρχεται* ἐκ τρίτου δίς, μὲν ἀργῶς, ἅπαξ δὲ συντόμως· εἰς τὸ τέλος τοῦ πρώτου μὲν λέγομεν *Προστασίαις τῶν Ἀσωμάτων σῶσον ἡμᾶς* ³⁸, τοῦ δευτέρου δὲ συνάπτομεν τὸ χαρακτηριστικὸν τοῦ Ἁγίου τοῦ Ναοῦ, λέγοντες *Πρεσβείαις τοῦ Ἁγίου ἢ τῶν Ἁγίων σῶσον ἡμᾶς*, καὶ εἰς τὸ τρίτον *Διὰ τῆς Θεοτόκου ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς*· εἶτα τὸ Ψαλτήριον³⁹ καὶ ἀκολούθως ψάλλονται τὰ γ' κατὰ σειρὰν Καθίσματα ἀργῶς, τὰ δύο ἀνὰ μίαν καὶ τὸ γ' δίς, καὶ εὐθὺς ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Καὶ ὑπὲρ τοῦ καταξιωθῆναι ἡμᾶς*, καὶ

---

37. Ἡ αὐτὴ Ἀπόλυσις τηρεῖται μέχρι τῆς Μεγάλης Τετάρτης ἐν τῷ Ὄρθρῳ καὶ ἐν τῇ Λειτουργίᾳ.
38. Τῇ Μ. Τρίτῃ *Πρεσβείαις τοῦ Προδρόμου σῶσον ἡμᾶς*· τῇ Μ. Τετάρτῃ *Δυνάμει τοῦ Σταυροῦ σου σῶσον ἡμᾶς*.
39. Συνήθως παραλείπεται τὸ Ψαλτήριον τῆς ἡμέρας ἐν ταῖς πλείσταις τῶν ἱερῶν Ἐκκλησιῶν κατὰ τοὺς ὄρθρους τούτους, εἴτε χάριν τῆς συντομίας εἴτε καὶ διότι εἰσὶ πέντε Καθίσματα ὡρισμένα καθ' ἑκάστην τῶν τριῶν πρώτων ἡμερῶν τῆς Μ. Ἑβδομάδος, καὶ ἀναγινώσκεται ἰδιαιτέρως τὸ πρωΐ ὑπὸ τοῦ Ἱερέως· ἀλλ' εὐχῆς ἔργον ἐστὶν ἵνα ἀναγινώσκηται μέρος τοὐλάχιστον τῶν πέντε Καθισμάτων εἰς ἀκρόασιν καὶ τοῦ λαοῦ, καθ' ὅσον μάλιστα τὸ Ψαλτήριον ἐθεωρήθη ἀνέκαθεν ἡ βάσις τῶν ἐκκλησιαστικῶν ἡμῶν προσευχῶν καὶ ἡ ἐπιούσιος πνευματικὴ τροφὴ ἑκάστου χριστιανοῦ, ὡς ἐσημειώθη ἐν τῇ Προθεωρίᾳ §3 ὑποσημ. 3.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday (Matins Opening and Rubrics) (§§ 66 Concluded, 67 Opening)

## Great and Holy Monday: Matins Opening and Rubrics (§§ 66 Concluded, 67 Opening)

... previous Sundays of Great Lent. At the rite of Mutual Forgiveness, the Stavrotheotokion is chanted: **"When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ..."** (*Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον*); the Dismissal: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee... May the Lord, coming unto His voluntary Passion for our salvation, Christ our true God..."** (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς . . . ἐρχόμενος ὁ Κύριος ἐπὶ τὸ ἑκούσιον πάθος διὰ τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν*),³⁷ and: **"Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."** (*Δι' εὐχῶν*).

**GREAT AND HOLY MONDAY AT MATINS**

67.    The Priest: **"Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages."** (*Εὐλογητὸς ὁ Θεός*); the Superior [Proestos]: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King, Comforter, the Spirit of truth..."** (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι. Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*); the Reader: the Trisagion Prayers; the Priest: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."** (*Ὅτι σοῦ ἐστιν*); the Reader: **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times); **"Glory... Both now and ever..."**, **"O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God..."** (3 times) and the Royal Psalms: Psalm 19 [LXX]: **"The Lord hear thee in the day of trouble..."** (*Ἐπακούσαι σου Κύριος*), and Psalm 20 [LXX]: **"The king shall rejoice in Thy strength, O Lord..."** (While these Psalms are being read slowly, the Priest censes the people with the hand-censer [*katzion*]). After the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."**; then the Troparia: **"Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."** (*Σῶσον Κύριε*); **"Glory..."**: **"Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ God..."**; **"Both now and ever..."**: **"O awesome and unashamed protection, despise not our petitions, O Good One..."** (*Προστασία φοβερὰ καὶ ἀκαταίσχυντε*). The Dean or presiding Reader [Proistamenos] recites the Six Psalms. After the Great Litany, the Great Alleluia is chanted with its appointed verses: **"From night until morning my spirit seeks Thee early, O God, for Thy commandments are a light upon the earth"** (*Ἐκ νυκτὸς ὀρθρίζει*, Isa. 26:9 [LXX]), twice in slow melody and twice in brief melody. Then the Dismissal Troparion: **"Behold, the Bridegroom comes in the middle of the night, and blessed is the servant whom He finds watching..."** (*Ἰδοὺ ὁ Νυμφίος ἔρχεται*) three times (twice slowly and once rapidly). At the end of the first time we say: **"...through the protection of the Bodiless Hosts, save us!"** (*Προστασίαις τῶν Ἀσωμάτων σῶσον ἡμᾶς*);³⁸ at the second time we join the commemoration of the Patron Saint of the Church, saying: **"...through the intercessions of Saint [N.] (or: the Saints), save us!"** (*Πρεσβείαις τοῦ Ἁγίου ἢ τῶν Ἁγίων σῶσον ἡμᾶς*); and at the third time: **"...through the Theotokos, have mercy on us!"** (*Διὰ τῆς Θεοτόκου ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς*). Then the Kathismata of the Psalter,³⁹ and next the three appointed Sessional Hymns [Kathismata] of the Triodion are chanted slowly in sequence: the first two once each, and the third twice. Immediately the Priest exclaims: **"And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel..."**, and...[^440]

---

37. This same Dismissal is maintained until Great and Holy Wednesday at Matins and at the Divine Liturgy.
38. On Great and Holy Tuesday: "...through the intercessions of the Forerunner, save us"; on Great and Holy Wednesday: "...by the power of Thy Cross, save us."
39. The Psalter of the day is customarily omitted in most holy churches during these Matins services, either for the sake of brevity or because five Kathismata are appointed for each of the first three days of Holy Week, and it is read privately in the morning by the Priest; nevertheless it is devoutly to be wished that at least a portion of the five Kathismata be read in the hearing of the people also, inasmuch as the Psalter has ever been regarded as the foundation of our ecclesiastical prayers and the daily spiritual sustenance of every Christian, as was noted in the Introductory Chapter [Protheoria] § 3, footnote 3.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday (Matins Concluded) (§ 67 Concluded)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Δευτέρα εἰς τὸν Ὄρθρον (Τέλος) (§ 67 Τέλος)

ἀναγινώσκει τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον, μεθ' ὃ ὁ Ν' ψαλμὸς καὶ τὰ Τριῴδια ἀνὰ ς' ἐν ἑκάστῃ Ὠδῇ, καὶ αὖθις ὁ Εἱρμὸς ἀργῶς. ἡ Αἴτησις, τὸ Κοντάκιον καὶ ὁ Οἶκος, τὸ Μηνολόγιον καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τοῦ Τριῳδίου· εἶτα ἡ η' Ὠδὴ μετὰ τοῦ Εἱρμοῦ, καὶ ἀντὶ μὲν τοῦ *Δόξα*, λέγομεν *Εὐλογοῦμεν Πατέρα, Υἱὸν καὶ Ἅγιον Πνεῦμα τὸν Κύριον*, εἰς δὲ τὸν Εἱρμὸν *Αἰνοῦμεν, εὐλογοῦμεν καὶ προσκυνοῦμεν τὸν Κύριον*· εἶτα *Τὴν θεοτόκον καὶ μητέρα τοῦ φωτὸς* καὶ ψάλλεται ἡ θ' Ὠδὴ μετὰ τοῦ Εἱρμοῦ αὖθις· εἰς τὸ τέλος ἡ Αἴτησις καὶ τὸ Ἐξαποστειλάριον ἐκ γ', καὶ ψάλλομεν τὰ β' τῶν Αἴνων Ἰδιόμελα εἰς δ', Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Κύριε ἐρχόμενος*. Εἰς τοὺς Αἴνους⁴⁰, ἱστῶμεν Στίχους δ', καὶ ψάλλομεν τὰ β' τῶν Αἴνων Ἰδιόμελα εἰς δ'. Δόξα καὶ νῦν, *Κύριε ἐρχόμενος*, τὸ *Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει* χῦμα καὶ μετὰ τὸ *Πληρώσωμεν τὴν ἑωθινὴν δέησιν*, ἄρχεται τῶν Ἀποστίχων ὁ α' Χορός, εἶτα *Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, Κοντάκιον *Ὁ Ἰακὼβ ὠδύρετο*, *Κύριε ἐλέησον* ιβ' ⁴¹, *Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητός*, *Ἐπουράνιε βασιλεῦ*, τὰς τρεῖς Μ. μετανοίας, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις *Ἐρχόμενος ὁ Κύριος ἐπὶ τὸ ἑκούσιον πάθος* καὶ τὸ *Δι' εὐχῶν*⁴².

---

40. Εἰς τὸ *Αἰνεῖτε* [τῶν Αἴνων] ὁ Πατριάρχης ἢ Ὁ Ἀρχιερεὺς ἀσπάζεται τὰς ἁγίας εἰκόνας τῶν παθῶν καὶ εὐλογήσας τὸν λαόν, τῶν Χορῶν ψαλλόντων τὸ *Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη* ἀνέρχεται αὖθις εἰς τὸ παραθρόνιον.
41. Ὅρα σημείωσιν 31.
42. Ἡ διάταξις αὕτη τῆς Ἀκολουθίας τηρεῖται καὶ ἐν ταῖς δυσὶν ἀκολούθοις ἡμέραις, τῇ Μ. Τρίτῃ καὶ τῇ Μ. Τετάρτῃ, εἴτε τὴν πρωΐαν, εἴτε ἀφ' ἑσπέρας γίνεται ὁ Ὄρθρος τοῦ Νυμφίου. Ἐν τῇ τελευταίᾳ περιπτώσει ὅμως συνήθως τὸ Ψαλτήριον ἀναγινώσκεται τὸ πρωΐ.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday (Matins Concluded) (§ 67 Concluded)

## Great and Holy Monday: Matins Concluded (§ 67 Concluded)

... reads the Gospel [Matthew 21:18–43: The Cursing of the Barren Fig Tree and the Parable of the Wicked Vinedressers]. After the Gospel, Psalm 50 is read; then the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] in six Troparia in each Ode, and again the Heirmos slowly as Katavasia. The Small Litany, the Kontakion: **"Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph..."** (*Ὁ Ἰακὼβ ὠδύρετο*), the Ikos, the Menologion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion. Then the Eighth Ode with its Heirmos; and instead of **"Glory..."**, we say: **"We bless the Lord: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit"** (*Εὐλογοῦμεν Πατέρα, Υἱὸν καὶ Ἅγιον Πνεῦμα τὸν Κύριον*). Before the Heirmos is repeated as Katavasia, we sing: **"We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord"** (*Αἰνοῦμεν, εὐλογοῦμεν καὶ προσκυνοῦμεν τὸν Κύριον*). Then the Deacon exclaims: **"The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!"** (*Τὴν Θεοτόκον καὶ μητέρα τοῦ φωτός*), and the Ninth Ode is chanted with its Heirmos again. At the conclusion, the Small Litany and the Exaposteilarion: **"I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the robe of my soul, O Giver of Light, and save me!"** (*Τὸν νυμφῶνά σου βλέπω, Σωτήρ μου, κεκοσμημένον*) three times. At the Praises:⁴⁰ we set 4 verses, and we chant the two Idiomela of the Praises (twice each): [**"O Lord, coming unto Thy voluntary Passion..."** (*Κύριε, ἐρχόμενος πρὸς τὸ πάθος*) and **"O Lord, coming unto Thy Passion, Thou didst establish the hearts of Thy Disciples..."**]. "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"O Lord, coming unto Thy Passion, Thou didst say unto Thy Disciples: Watch and pray..."** (*Κύριε, ἐρχόμενος πρὸς τὸ πάθος, τοὺς ἰδίους ἐστήριξας Μαθητάς*). The Small Doxology: **"To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God..."** (*Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*) read plain. After the litany: **"Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord"** (*Πληρώσωμεν τὴν ἑωθινὴν δέησιν*), the First Choir begins the Aposticha. Then: **"It is good to give praise unto the Lord, and to sing unto Thy name, O Most High"** (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: **"Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph..."**, **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times);⁴¹ the Priest: **"Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages"** (*Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητός*); the Reader: **"Confirm, O God, the holy Orthodox faith..."**; then the Prayer: **"O Heavenly King, strengthen our faithful civil authorities..."** (*Ἐπουράνιε βασιλεῦ*); the three Great Prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem the Syrian, and the Dismissal: **"May the Lord, coming unto His voluntary Passion for our salvation, Christ our true God..."** (*Ἐρχόμενος ὁ Κύριος ἐπὶ τὸ ἑκούσιον πάθος*), and: **"Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."** (*Δι' εὐχῶν*).⁴²[^441]

---

40. At the "Praise ye" (*Αἰνεῖτε*) [of the Praises], the Patriarch or the presiding Hierarch venerates the holy Icons of the Passion and, having blessed the people while the choirs chant "Many years to you, Master!" (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*), he ascends again to the small side-throne [*parathronion*].
41. See footnote 31.
42. This order of the service is observed on the following two days, namely on Great and Holy Tuesday and Great and Holy Wednesday, whether the Matins of the Bridegroom takes place in the morning or in the evening. In the latter case, however, the Psalter is customarily read in the morning.

# Holy Week: Great Monday (Hours, Presanctified, Compline) and Great Tuesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified) (§§ 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 73)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Δευτέρα καὶ Μεγάλη Τρίτη (§§ 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 73)

68.    Τὸ πρωῒ ἀναγινώσκονται πέντε Καθίσματα τοῦ Ψαλτηρίου, εἶτα αἱ Ὧραι λιταὶ μετὰ τοῦ Ἀπολυτικίου *Ἰδοὺ ὁ Νυμφίος ἔρχεται* καὶ τοῦ Κοντακίου *Ὁ Ἰακὼβ ὠδύρετο*· εἰς τὴν θ' Ὥραν ψάλλονται οἱ Μακαρισμοί, μετὰ δὲ τὴν Εὐχὴν *Παναγία Τριάς*, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις, καὶ ἄρχεται ὁ Ἑσπερινός.

69.    Ἐν τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακὸν καὶ τὰ Πρὸς Κύριον, εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* ψάλλομεν τὰ ς' Ἰδιόμελα τῶν Αἴνων καὶ τῶν Ἀποστίχων, Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Δευτέραν Εὔαν*, Εἴσοδος μετὰ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, καὶ τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα τῆς ἡμέρας, τὸ *Κατευθυνθήτω* καὶ εὐθὺς μετ' αὐτὸ *Σοφία, ὀρθοί, ἀκούσωμεν...* καὶ λέγεται τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον καὶ καθεξῆς ἡ Λειτουργία τῶν Προηγιασμένων.

70.    Τὸ ἑσπέρας ἀναγινώσκεται τὸ Μέγα Ἀπόδειπνον, ὡς εἴθισται, μετὰ δὲ τὸ *Δόξα ἐν Ὑψίστοις Θεῷ*, ἄρχεται τοῦ Τριῳδίου ὁ β' Χορός, ἀπ' θ' ὁ Εἱρμὸς *Τὸν προδηλωθέντα ἐν ὄρει*, τὸ Τρισάγιον καὶ ἀκολούθως τὸ *Κύριε τῶν δυνάμεων* κτλ. τοῦ Μ. Ἀποδείπνου· εἰς τὴν Συγχώρησιν ψάλλεται τὸ *Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον Χριστέ*.

**ΜΕΓΑΛΗ ΤΡΙΤΗ**

71.    Ἡ Ἀκολουθία τοῦ Ὄρθρου τῆς Μ. Τρίτης ψάλλεται ἀπαραλλάκτως κατὰ τὴν ἐν § 67 προεκτεθεῖσαν διάταξιν. (Ὅρα τῇ Μεγάλῃ Δευτέρᾳ).

72.    Τὸ πρωῒ πρὸ τοῦ Εὐλογητοῦ, ἀναγινώσκονται ἕτερα πέντε Καθίσματα τοῦ Ψαλτηρίου καὶ αἱ Ὧραι λιταὶ ὡς χθές· μετὰ τὴν Εὐχὴν *Παναγία Τριάς*, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις, καὶ ἄρχεται ὁ Ἑσπερινός.

73.    Ἐν τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακὸν καὶ τὰ Πρὸς Κύριον, εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* ψάλλομεν τὰ ς' Ἰδιόμελα τῶν Αἴνων καὶ τῶν Ἀποστίχων, ἅπερ ἐψάλησαν ἐν τῷ Ὄρθρῳ, Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Ἰδού σοι τὸ τάλαντον*, Εἴσοδος μετὰ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου, *Φῶς ἱλαρὸν* καὶ τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα τῆς ἡμέρας, τὸ *Κατευθυνθήτω*, τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον τῆς ἡμέρας, καὶ καθεξῆς ἡ λοιπὴ Ἀκολουθία τῶν Προηγιασμένων.

# Holy Week: Great Monday (Hours, Presanctified, Compline) and Great Tuesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 73, 74 Opening)

## Great Monday (Hours, Presanctified, Compline) and Great Tuesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 73, 74 Opening)

68.    In the morning, the five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 4, 5, 6, 7, and 8]; then the plain Hours with the Dismissal Troparion: **"Behold, the Bridegroom comes..."** (*Ἰδοὺ ὁ Νυμφίος ἔρχεται*) and the Kontakion: **"Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph..."** (*Ὁ Ἰακὼβ ὠδύρετο*). At the Ninth Hour, the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] are chanted; and after the Prayer: **"All-Holy Trinity, Consubstantial Might..."** (*Παναγία Τριάς*), the Dismissal is given, and Vespers begins.

69.    At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 [LXX]) and the Eighteenth Kathisma: **"Unto the Lord"** (*Πρὸς Κύριον*, Pss. 119–133 [LXX]), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*, Ps. 140 [LXX]), we chant the six Idiomela of the Praises and the Aposticha. "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Finding the Egyptian woman another Eve, the serpent hastened through speech to trip up Joseph..."** (*Δευτέραν Εὔαν τὴν Αἰγυπτίαν εὑρὼν ὁ δράκων*). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), and the Old Testament Readings of the day: Exodus 1:1–20 [LXX] and Job 1:1–12 [LXX]. Then **"Let my prayer arise"** (*Κατευθυνθήτω*), and immediately after it: **"Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel"**, and the Gospel is read [Matthew 24:3–35: The Discourse on the Mount of Olives], and in sequence the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts.

70.    In the evening, Great Compline is read, as is customary. After the Doxology: **"Glory to God in the highest..."** (*Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις Θεῷ*), the Second Choir begins the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion]; after the Heirmos of the Ninth Ode: **"Him that was prefigured upon the mountain..."** (*Τὸν προδηλωθέντα ἐν ὄρει*), the Trisagion Prayers, and following this: **"O Lord of hosts, be with us..."** (*Κύριε τῶν δυνάμεων*), and the rest of Great Compline. At the rite of Mutual Forgiveness, we chant: **"When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ, she lamented: Woe is me, my Child!..."** (*Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον, Χριστέ*).

**GREAT AND HOLY TUESDAY**

71.    The service of Matins of Great and Holy Tuesday is chanted unalterably according to the order set forth in Paragraph 67. (See the order for Great and Holy Monday).

72.    In the morning, before the exclamation: **"Blessed is our God..."**, the next five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 9, 10, 11, 12, and 13], and the plain Hours as yesterday with the Dismissal Troparion: **"Behold, the Bridegroom comes..."** and the Kontakion: **"Having in mind the hour of the end, O my soul..."** (*Τὴν ὥραν, ψυχή, τοῦ τέλους ἐννοήσασα*). At the Ninth Hour the Beatitudes are chanted; after the Prayer: **"All-Holy Trinity..."**, the Dismissal is given, and Vespers begins.

73.    At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 [LXX]) and the Eighteenth Kathisma: **"Unto the Lord"** (*Πρὸς Κύριον*, Pss. 119–133 [LXX]), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the six Idiomela of the Praises and the Aposticha which were chanted at Matins. "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Behold, the Master entrusts thee with the talent, O my soul..."** (*Ἰδού σοι τὸ τάλαντον ὁ Δεσπότης ἐγχειρίζει, ψυχή μου*). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), and the Old Testament Readings of the day: Exodus 2:5–10 [LXX] and Job 1:13–22 [LXX]. Then **"Let my prayer arise"** (*Κατευθυνθήτω*), the Gospel of the day [Matthew 24:36–26:2: The Parables of the Ten Virgins and the Talents], and in sequence the remaining service of the Presanctified Gifts.

74.    In the evening, Great Compline is read; after the Doxology: **"Glory to God in the...**[^442]

# Holy Week: Great Tuesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Wednesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 74 Concluded, 75, 76, 77 Opening)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Τρίτη καὶ Μεγάλη Τετάρτη (§§ 74 Τέλος, 75, 76, 77 Ἀρχή)

74.    Τὸ ἑσπέρας, τὸ Μ. Ἀπόδειπνον· μετὰ τὸ *Δόξα ἐν Ὑψίστοις Θεῷ*, ἄρχεται τοῦ Τριῳδίου ὁ α' Χορός· ἀπ' θ' ὁ Εἱρμὸς *Τὴν ὑπερφυῶς σαρκί*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, *Κύριε τῶν Δυνάμεων* κτλ. τοῦ Μ. Ἀποδείπνου· εἰς τὴν Συγχώρησιν, τὸ *Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον*, καὶ οὕτω λήγει τὸ Μέγα Ἀπόδειπνον καὶ τὸ *Κύριε τῶν Δυνάμεων*.

**ΜΕΓΑΛΗ ΤΕΤΑΡΤΗ**

75.    Ἡ Ἀκολουθία τοῦ Ὄρθρου καὶ τῆς ἡμέρας ταύτης ψάλλεται ἀπαραλλάκτως⁴³ ὡς διετάχθη ἐν § 67.
       Τὸ δὲ πρωῒ ἀναγινώσκονται ἕτερα ε' Καθίσματα τοῦ Ψαλτηρίου, καὶ οὕτω πληροῦται ὅλον τὸ Ψαλτήριον, καταλιμπανομένων τῶν β' Καθισμάτων, ΙΖ' καὶ ΙΗ', τοῦ μὲν ὡς ἐν τῷ Μ. Σαββάτῳ ἀναγνωσθησομένου, τοῦ δὲ ὡς συχνάκις ἐν ἑκάστῃ Προηγιασμένῃ, καὶ δὴ καὶ ἐν ταῖς τρισὶ τῆς ἑβδομάδος ταύτης ἀναγινωσκομένου, ἐπίσης ἀναγινώσκονται καὶ αἱ Ὧραι λιταί, ὡς κατὰ τὰς δύο προηγουμένας ἡμέρας, καὶ μετὰ τὴν Εὐχὴν *Παναγία Τριὰς* ἡ Ἀπόλυσις, καὶ ἄρχεται ὁ Ἑσπερινός.

76.    Ἐν τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακὸν καὶ τὰ Πρὸς Κύριον, εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* ἱστῶμεν Στίχους ι' καὶ ψάλλομεν πάντα τὰ Στιχηρά, ἅπερ ὡς Αἶνοι καὶ Ἀπόστιχα ἐψάλησαν ἐν τῷ Ὄρθρῳ, Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Κύριε ἡ ἐν πολλαῖς ἁμαρτίαις*, Εἴσοδος μετὰ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου, *Φῶς ἱλαρόν*, τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα τῆς ἡμέρας, τὸ *Κατευθυνθήτω*, καὶ τὸ ὡρισμένον Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ καθεξῆς ἡ τῶν Προηγιασμένων Λειτουργία, ἥτις ἐστὶ καὶ ἡ τελευταία τῆς Μεγάλης Τεσσαρακοστῆς.

77.    Τὸ ἑσπέρας ἀναγινώσκεται τὸ μικρὸν Ἀπόδειπνον· μετὰ τὸ *Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*, ψάλλονται τὰ Τριῴδια *Ἀνώγεων ἐστρωμένον* μετὰ Στίχου *Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*· ἀπ' θ' ὁ Εἱρμὸς *Ἀσπόρου συλλήψεως*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Κοντάκιον *Ὑπὲρ τὴν πόρνην Ἀγαθέ*, τὸ *Κύριε ἐλέησον* μ', *Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ*, τὸ

---

43. Τῇ νυκτὶ ταύτῃ εἰς τὰ Ἀπόστιχα ψάλλεται ὑπὸ τοῦ β' Χοροῦ ἀργῶς καὶ μετὰ μέλους *Κύριε ἡ ἐν πολλαῖς ἁμαρτίαις*, ὅπερ θεωρεῖται μὲν ποίημα Κασσιανῆς μοναχῆς, ἀλλ' ἐν χειρογράφῳ Ἱεροσολυμιτικῷ φέρεται ὡς ποίημα Φωτίου πατριάρχου.

# Holy Week: Great Tuesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Wednesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 74 Concluded, 75, 76, 77 Opening)

## Great Tuesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Wednesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 74 Concluded, 75, 76, 77 Opening)

... highest..."** (*Δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις Θεῷ*), the First Choir begins the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion: Odes 3, 8, and 9]; after the Heirmos of the Ninth Ode: **"With faith let us magnify her that supernaturally conceived in the flesh..."** (*Τὴν ὑπερφυῶς σαρκί*), the Trisagion Prayers, **"O Lord of hosts, be with us..."** (*Κύριε τῶν δυνάμεων*), and the rest of Great Compline. At the rite of Mutual Forgiveness, we chant: **"When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ, she lamented: Woe is me, my Child!..."** (*Σφαγήν σου τὴν ἄδικον*), and in this manner Great Compline and **"O Lord of hosts"** come to an end.

**GREAT AND HOLY WEDNESDAY**

75.    The service of Matins of this day also is chanted unalterably⁴³ as prescribed in Paragraph 67.
       In the morning, another five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 14, 15, 16, 19, and 20], and thus the entire Psalter is completed, omitting the two Kathismata (17 and 18): the former because it is to be read on Great Saturday, and the latter because it is frequently read at every Presanctified Liturgy, and indeed was read on the three preceding days of this week. Likewise the plain Hours are read, as on the two preceding days; and after the Prayer: **"All-Holy Trinity..."** (*Παναγία Τριάς*), the Dismissal is given, and Vespers begins.

76.    At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 [LXX]) and the Eighteenth Kathisma: **"Unto the Lord"** (*Πρὸς Κύριον*, Pss. 119–133 [LXX]), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we set 10 verses, and we chant all the Stichera which were chanted as Praises and Aposticha at Matins. "Glory... Both now and ever...": the Doxastikon: **"O Lord, the woman who had fallen into many sins, having recognized Thy divinity, O Lord, taking upon herself the office of a myrrh-bearer..."** (*Κύριε, ἡ ἐν πολλαῖς ἁμαρτίαις*). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), the Old Testament Readings of the day: Exodus 2:11–23 [LXX] and Job 2:1–10 [LXX]. Then **"Let my prayer arise"** (*Κατευθυνθήτω*), and the appointed Gospel [Matthew 26:6–16: The Anointing at Bethany and the Betrayal of Judas], and in sequence the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts, which is also the final one of Great Lent.

77.    In the evening, Small Compline is read. After **"It is truly meet"** (*Ἄξιόν ἐστιν*), the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] is chanted: **"A guest-room furnished and ready..."** (*Ἀνώγεων ἐστρωμένον*) with the verse: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!"** (*Δόξα σοι, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι*). After the Ninth Ode, the Heirmos: **"The childbirth of a conception without seed..."** (*Ἀσπόρου συλλήψεως*), the...[^443]

---

43. On this night at the Aposticha, the Second Choir sings slowly and with solemn melody the renowned hymn: "O Lord, the woman who had fallen into a multitude of sins...", which is commonly held to be the composition of the Nun Kassiani; nevertheless, in a Jerusalem manuscript it is recorded as the composition of the Patriarch Photios.

# Holy Week: Great Wednesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Thursday (Matins, Vespers Opening) (§§ 77 Concluded, 78, 79 Opening)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Τετάρτη καὶ Μεγάλη Πέμπτη (§§ 77 Τέλος, 78, 79 Ἀρχή)

*Ἄσπιλε ἀμόλυντε*, κτλ. καὶ Ἀπόλυσις.

**ΜΕΓΑΛΗ ΠΕΜΠΤΗ ΕΙΣ ΤΟΝ ΟΡΘΡΟΝ**

78.    Μετὰ τὸν Ἑξάψαλμον καὶ τὴν Μ. Συναπτήν, ψάλλομεν μετὰ Στίχων τὸ *Ἀλληλούϊα* εἰς ἦχον πλ. δ' τετράκις, καὶ τὸ Τροπάριον *Ὅτε οἱ ἔνδοξοι Μαθηταὶ* ἐκ γ' καὶ εὐθὺς τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον τοῦ Ὄρθρου τῆς ἡμέρας, εἶτα ὁ Ν' ψαλμός, καὶ ὁ Κανὼν ἄνευ Στίχων μετὰ τῆς Καταβασίας ἐν τέλει ἑκάστης Ὠδῆς. Ἀπὸ γ' Ὠδῆς τὰ Καθίσματα· ἀφ' ς' τὸ Κοντάκιον, ὁ Οἶκος, τὸ Μηνολόγιον καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τοῦ Τριῳδίου. Μετὰ τὸν Εἱρμὸν τῆς η' Ὠδῆς, γίνεται ἡ Ἐκφώνησις *Τὴν θεοτόκον* καὶ ψάλλεται ἡ θ' Ὠδή, τὸ Ἐξαποστειλάριον ἐκ γ', οἱ Αἶνοι εἰς δ', Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Ὃν ἐκήρυξεν Ἀμνόν*, τὸ *Σοὶ Δόξα πρέπει* χῦμα, *Πληρώσωμεν τὴν Ἑωθινήν*, καὶ τὰ δ' Ἀπόστιχα, Δόξα, *Ὁ τρόπος σου δολιότης*, Καὶ νῦν, *Μυσταγωγῶν σου Κύριε*, *Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Τροπάριον τῆς Προφητείας, καὶ ἡ Προφητεία. Ἐκτενής, καὶ Ἀπόλυσις.

79.    Ἐν τῷ Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακόν, εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* τὰ ε' Ἰδιόμελα τῶν Αἴνων δευτεροῦντες τὸ α', Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, *Γέννημα ἐχιδνῶν*, Εἴσοδος μετὰ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου, *Φῶς...*

# Holy Week: Great Wednesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Thursday (Matins, Vespers Opening) (§§ 77 Concluded, 78, 79 Opening)

## Great Wednesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Thursday (Matins, Vespers Opening) (§§ 77 Concluded, 78, 79 Opening)

... Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: **"Having transgressed more than the harlot, O Good One, I have in no wise brought forth a flood of tears for Thee; but falling down in silence before Thee, I kiss Thy most pure feet with love..."** (*Ὑπὲρ τὴν πόρνην, Ἀγαθέ*); **"Lord, have mercy"** (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: **"Thou Who at all times and at every hour..."** (*Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ*), the nocturnal prayer: **"O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Virgin, Lady Bride of God..."** (*Ἄσπιλε, ἀμόλυντε*), etc., and the Dismissal.

**GREAT AND HOLY THURSDAY AT MATINS**

78.    After the Six Psalms and the Great Litany, we chant the Alleluia with its verses in Tone 8 four times, and the Troparion: **"When the glorious Disciples were enlightened at the Supper during the washing of the feet, then the ungodly Judas was darkened by the disease of avarice; and to lawless judges he delivered Thee, the righteous Judge. Behold his goods, O lover of money, who for the sake thereof hanged himself! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Lord, Who art good towards all, glory to Thee!"** (*Ὅτε οἱ ἔνδοξοι Μαθηταὶ*) three times. Immediately the Matins Gospel of the day is read: Luke 22:1–39 [LXX: The Institution of the Holy Eucharist and the Discourse at the Last Supper]. Then Psalm 50 is read, and the Canon is chanted without verses, with its Katavasia at the end of each Ode. After the Third Ode, the Sessional Hymns [Kathismata]: **"He Who created the lakes, the springs and the seas, desiring to teach us the highest humility, girded Himself with a towel and washed the feet of the Disciples..."**; "Glory...": **"Having humbled Thyself through compassion, Thou didst wash the feet of Thy Disciples..."**; "Both now and ever...": **"Eating, O Master, with Thy Disciples, Thou didst mystically reveal Thy holy slaughter..."**. After the Sixth Ode, the Kontakion: **"Having taken the Bread into his hands, the traitor extends them secretly to receive the price of Him Who fashioned man with His own hands; and Judas remained incorrigible, having dipped his hand into the dish with Christ..."**, the Ikos, the Menologion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion on the Mystical Supper, the Washing of the Disciples' Feet, the Agony in the Garden, and the Betrayal of Judas. After the Heirmos of the Eighth Ode is chanted as Katavasia, the Deacon exclaims: **"The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!"** (*Τὴν Θεοτόκον καὶ μητέρα τοῦ φωτός*), and the Ninth Ode is chanted. Then the Exaposteilarion: **"I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior..."** (*Τὸν νυμφῶνά σου βλέπω*) three times. At the Praises [Lauds], we chant the four Stichera: [**"The assembly of the Jews hastens together to deliver the Creator and Fashioner of all unto Pilate..."**, **"Judas the transgressor, having dipped his hand into the dish with Thee at supper..."**, **"Judas, being a treacherous servant, deceitfully devised betrayal..."**, and **"Judas, servant and deceiver, disciple and plotter, friend and devil, was made manifest from his deeds..."**]. "Glory... Both now and ever...": the Doxastikon: **"The Lamb whom Isaiah proclaimed comes of His own will to the slaughter..."** (*Ὃν ἐκήρυξεν Ἀμνόν*). The Small Doxology: **"To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God..."** (*Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*) read plain. The litany: **"Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord"** (*Πληρώσωμεν τὴν Ἑωθινήν*), and the four Aposticha with verses from Psalm 40 LXX: [**"Today the evil Sanhedrin has assembled together against Christ..."**; verse: **"He that eats of my bread has lifted up his heel against me"** (Ps. 40:9 [LXX]); **"Today Judas hides his treachery under the pretense of love for the poor..."**; verse: **"When he went abroad, he spoke of the same"** (Ps. 40:6 [LXX]); **"Thy deceitful manner is revealed unto the end..."**; verse: **"An evil word have they poured out against me"** (Ps. 40:8 [LXX]); and **"O believers, let none of us who are uninitiated draw near to the Master's table..."**]. "Glory...": **"Thy deceitful manner..."**; "Both now and ever...": **"Initiating Thy Disciples, O Lord, Thou didst teach them, saying: O my friends, see that no fear separate you from Me..."** (*Μυσταγωγῶν σου, Κύριε*). Then: **"It is good to give praise unto the Lord..."** (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*), the Trisagion Prayers, the Troparion of the Prophecy: **"Thou Who didst endure slaps for the human race, O Christ God, deliver us from corruption and save our souls"**, and the Old Testament Prophecy: Jeremiah 11:18–12:5, 9–11, 14–15 [LXX: The Prophecy of the Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter]. The Augmented Litany [Ektenis], and the Dismissal.

79.    At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 [LXX]), at **"Lord, I have cried"** (*Κύριε ἐκέκραξα*), we chant the five Idiomela of the Praises, repeating the first one for six. "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Judas is truly the offspring of vipers, who ate the manna in the wilderness and murmured against the Nourisher..."** (*Γέννημα ἐχιδνῶν*). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, **"O Gladsome Light"** (*Φῶς ἱλαρόν*), and the Old Testament Readings: Exodus 19:10–19 [LXX], Job...[^444]

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday (Liturgy of Saint Basil Concluded) and Service of the Holy Passions Opening (§§ 79 Concluded, 80 Opening)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Μεγάλη Πέμπτη (Θεία Λειτουργία Τέλος) καὶ Ἀκολουθία τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν (§§ 79 Τέλος, 80 Ἀρχή)

ἱλαρὸν καὶ τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα· εἶτα ἡ μικρὰ Συναπτή, τὸ Τρισάγιον, ὁ Ἀπόστολος, τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ καθεξῆς ἡ Λειτουργία τοῦ Μεγάλου Βασιλείου, Χερουβικὸν καὶ Κοινωνικὸν *Τοῦ δείπνου σου τοῦ μυστικοῦ*, τὸ αὐτὸ καὶ ἀντὶ τοῦ *Εἴδομεν τὸ φῶς*, ἔπειτα *Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς . . . ὁ δι' ὑπερβάλλουσαν ἀγαθότητα ὁδὸν ἀρίστην τὴν ταπείνωσιν ὑποδείξας ἐν τῷ νίψαι τοὺς πόδας τῶν Μαθητῶν καὶ μέχρι Σταυροῦ καὶ ταφῆς συγκαταβὰς ἡμῖν, Χριστὸς ὁ ἀληθινὸς* κτλ. καὶ τὸ *Δι' εὐχῶν*.

**ΑΚΟΛΟΥΘΙΑ ΤΩΝ ΑΓΙΩΝ ΠΑΘΩΝ** ⁴⁴

80.    Τῇ Ἁγίᾳ καὶ Μ. Πέμπτῃ ἑσπέρας, περὶ ὥραν α΄ τῆς νυκτός, εὐλογήσαντος τοῦ Ἱερέως, ὁ Προεστὼς λέγει *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν δόξα σοι*, τὸ *Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης τὸ Τρισάγιον, ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Ὅτι σοῦ ἐστιν*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης *Κύριε ἐλέησον* ιβ΄, *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν* γ΄ καὶ τοὺς ψαλμοὺς *Ἐπακούσαι σου Κύριος* (βραδέως ἕως οὗ θυμιάσῃ ὁ Ἱερεύς), εἶτα τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὰ Τροπάρια *Σῶσον Κύριε* κτλ. καὶ ὁ Προεστὼς τὸν Ἑξάψαλμον, ἡ Μ. Συναπτή, καὶ ψάλλεται τὸ σύντομον Ἀλληλουϊάριον ἐκ δ΄ καὶ τὸ Τροπάριον *Ὅτε οἱ ἔνδοξοι μαθηταὶ* δὶς ἀργῶς καὶ ἅπαξ συντόμως, καὶ ἀναγινώσκεται ὑπὸ τοῦ Ἀρχιερέως τὸ [α΄.] Εὐαγγέλιον τῆς Διαθήκης· εἰς τὸ τέλος εὐλογήσας τὸν λαὸν διὰ τρικηρίου, τῶν Χορῶν ψαλλόντων τὸ *Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*, ἀνέρχεται εἰς τὸν Θρόνον καὶ οἱ Χοροὶ ψάλλουσι τὰ Ἀντίφωνα *Ἄρχοντες λαῶν* μέχρι τοῦ

---

44. Ὅρα ἰδιαιτέραν κατ' ἔκτασιν Ἀκολουθίαν τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν, ὡς γίνεται ἐν τοῖς Πατριαρχείοις εἰς τὸ Παράρτημα.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday (Liturgy of Saint Basil Concluded) and Service of the Holy Passions Opening (§§ 79 Concluded, 80 Opening)

## Great and Holy Thursday: Liturgy of Saint Basil Concluded and Service of the Holy Passions Opening (§§ 79 Concluded, 80 Opening)

... Job 38:1–21; 42:1–5 [LXX]; and Isaiah 50:4–11 [LXX]. Then the Small Litany, the Trisagion Hymn, the Epistle (1 Corinthians 11:23–32: The Institution of the Holy Eucharist), and the Gospel (Matthew 26:2–20; John 13:3–17; Matthew 26:21–39; Luke 22:43–45; Matthew 26:40–27:2: The Composite Passion Gospel of the Mystical Supper), and in sequence the rest of the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great. In place of the Cherubic Hymn, the Communion Hymn, and **"We have seen the true light"**, is chanted: **"Of Thy Mystical Supper, O Son of God, receive me today as a communicant; for I will not speak of the Mystery to Thine enemies, neither will I give Thee a kiss as did Judas, but like the thief do I confess Thee: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom!"** (*Τοῦ δείπνου σου τοῦ μυστικοῦ*). Then: **"Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth and forevermore"** (*Εἴη τὸ ὄνομα Κυρίου*), and the Dismissal: **"Glory to Thee, our God, our hope, glory to Thee. May He Who out of exceeding goodness showed humility to be the most excellent way when He washed the feet of the Disciples, and condescended unto the Cross and burial for our sake, Christ our true God..."** (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς . . . ὁ δι' ὑπερβάλλουσαν ἀγαθότητα*), and: **"Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."** (*Δι' εὐχῶν*).

**THE SERVICE OF THE HOLY PASSIONS** ⁴⁴

80.    On Holy and Great Thursday evening, around the First Hour of the night [7:00 p.m.], the Priest having given the blessing, the Superior [Proestos] says: **"Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King, Comforter, the Spirit of truth..."** (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, δόξα σοι. Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*); the Reader: the Trisagion Prayers; the Priest: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."**; the Reader: **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times), **"Glory... Both now and ever..."**, **"O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God..."** (3 times) and the Royal Psalms: Psalm 19 [LXX]: **"The Lord hear Thee in the day of trouble..."** (*Ἐπακούσαι σου Κύριος*), and Psalm 20 [LXX]: **"The king shall rejoice in Thy strength, O Lord..."** (these are read slowly until the Priest finishes censing with the hand-censer [*katzion*]); then the Trisagion Prayers, the Troparia: **"Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..."** (*Σῶσον Κύριε*); **"Glory..."**: **"Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ God..."**; **"Both now and ever..."**: **"O awesome and unashamed protection, despise not our petitions, O Good One..."**; and the Superior [Proestos] reads the Six Psalms. The Great Litany; then the brief Alleluia is chanted four times with its verses, and the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8: **"When the glorious Disciples were enlightened at the Supper during the washing of the feet, then the ungodly Judas was darkened by the disease of avarice; and to lawless judges he delivered Thee, the righteous Judge. Behold his goods, O lover of money, who for the sake thereof hanged himself! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Lord, Who art good towards all, glory to Thee!"** (*Ὅτε οἱ ἔνδοξοι μαθηταὶ*) twice slowly and once rapidly. Then the First Gospel of the Testament [John 13:31–18:1: The Farewell Discourse of Christ to the Disciples] is read by the Archbishop [or the Priest]; at the conclusion, having blessed the people with the three-stemmed candlestick [*trikirion*], while the choirs chant: **"Many years to you, Master!"** (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*), he ascends the throne, and the choirs chant the Antiphons, beginning with: **"The rulers of the peoples took counsel together against the Lord and against His Anointed"** (*Ἄρχοντες λαῶν*) until the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"Sustaining the disciples at the supper, Thou didst say: One of you will betray Me..."** (*Ἐν τῷ δείπνῳ τοὺς μαθητάς*).[^445]

---

44. See Appendix [Parartima] IV for the detailed order of the Service of the Holy Passions as celebrated in the Patriarchate of Constantinople.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday Evening (Service of the Holy Passions Gospels II–IX, Antiphons IV–XV, and Canon) (§ 80 Continued)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Ἀκολουθία τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν (Συνέχεια) (§ 80 Συνέχεια)

Καθίσματος *Ἐν τῷ δείπνῳ τοὺς μαθητάς*, μεθ' ὃ ἀναγινώσκεται τὸ β΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· εἰς τὸ τέλος ὁ β΄ Χορὸς ψάλλει *Δόξα τῇ μακροθυμίᾳ σου Κύριε δόξα σοι* (ὅπερ καὶ εἰς πάντα τὰ λοιπὰ Εὐαγγέλια ἐπαναλαμβάνεται), καὶ ἄρχεται τοῦ δ΄ Ἀντιφώνου μέχρι τοῦ Καθίσματος *Ποῖός σε τρόπος Ἰούδα* καὶ τὸ γ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· εἶτα ὁ Χορὸς τὸ ζ΄ Ἀντίφωνον μέχρι τοῦ Καθίσματος *Ὢ πῶς Ἰούδας ὁ ποτέ σου μαθητής*, καὶ τὸ δ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· εἶτα ὁ β΄ Χορὸς τὸ ι΄ Ἀντίφωνον μέχρι τοῦ Καθίσματος *Ὅτε παρέστης τῷ Καϊάφᾳ ὁ Θεός*, καὶ τὸ ε΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· μετὰ τοῦτο ὁ α΄ Χορὸς τὸ ιγ΄ Ἀντίφωνον μέχρι τοῦ ιε΄, ὅτε καὶ γίνεται ἡ ἔξοδος τοῦ Σταυροῦ, ὡς συνειθίσθη ἤδη σχεδὸν πανταχοῦ, εἶτα ἐξακολουθοῦσι τὰ Ἀντίφωνα μέχρι τοῦ Καθίσματος *Ἐξηγόρασας ἡμᾶς*, καὶ τότε λέγεται τὸ ϛ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· εἶτα ἄρχεται ὁ α΄ Χορὸς τοὺς Μακαρισμούς· τούτων συμπληρωθέντων, ψάλλεται τὸ Προκείμενον *Διεμερίσαντο τὰ ἱμάτιά μου ἑαυτοῖς* ἐκ γ΄ καὶ τὸ ζ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον, ὁ Ν΄ ψαλμὸς καὶ τὸ η΄ Εὐαγγέλιον· εἶτα ὁ α΄ Χορὸς ἄρχεται τοῦ Κανόνος *Πρὸς σὲ ὀρθρίζω*, ἀνὰ δ΄ Τροπάρια ἀφ' ἑκάστης Ὠδῆς ἄνευ Στίχων καὶ εἰς τὸ τέλος ἑκάστης Ὠδῆς ὁ Εἱρμὸς ἀργῶς, ἡ Αἴτησις, τὸ Κοντάκιον, ὁ Οἶκος, τὸ Μηνολόγιον καὶ τὸ Ὑπόμνημα τοῦ Τριῳδίου, ἡ Ἐκφώνησις *Τὴν θεοτόκον*, καὶ ψάλλεται ἡ θ΄, τὸ Ἐξαποστειλάριον ἐκ γ΄ καὶ τὸ θ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον. Εἰς τοὺς Αἴνους, τὰ γ΄ Ἰδιόμελα δευτεροῦντες τὸ α΄ μετὰ τὸ

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday Evening (Service of the Holy Passions Gospels II–IX, Antiphons IV–XV, Procession of the Cross, and Canon) (§ 80 Continued)

## Great and Holy Thursday Evening: Service of the Holy Passions Continued (§ 80 Continued)

... Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"Sustaining the disciples at the supper, Thou didst say: One of you will betray Me; but Judas the transgressor did not wish to understand"** (*Ἐν τῷ δείπνῳ τοὺς μαθητάς*). After this, the Second Gospel [John 18:1–28: The Betrayal and Arrest in Gethsemane, and Jesus before Annas] is proclaimed; at the conclusion, the Second Choir chants: **"Glory to Thy forbearance, O Lord, glory to Thee!"** (*Δόξα τῇ μακροθυμίᾳ σου Κύριε δόξα σοι*) (which is repeated at the end of each of the remaining Gospels). Then begins the Fourth Antiphon until the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"What reason has made thee, O Judas, a traitor of the Savior? Did He cast thee off from the choir of the Apostles? Did He deprive thee of the gift of healings?..."** (*Ποῖός σε τρόπος Ἰούδα*). Then follows the Third Gospel [Matthew 26:57–75: Jesus before Caiaphas and the Denial of Peter]. Then the choir chants the Seventh Antiphon until the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"O how did Judas, who was once Thy disciple, devise betrayal against Thee?..."** (*Ὢ πῶς Ἰούδας ὁ ποτέ σου μαθητής*). Then the Fourth Gospel [John 18:28–19:16: Jesus before Pilate and the Condemnation to Death] is read; then the Second Choir begins the Tenth Antiphon until the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"When Thou, O God, didst stand before Caiaphas, and wast delivered unto Pilate, the Judge of all: the powers of heaven shook with fear..."** (*Ὅτε παρέστης τῷ Καϊάφᾳ ὁ Θεός*). Then the Fifth Gospel [Matthew 27:3–32: The Despair of Judas, Pilate Washes His Hands, and Simon of Cyrene Bears the Cross] is proclaimed; after this, the First Choir begins Antiphon Thirteen: **"The assembly of the Jews requested Pilate to crucify Thee, O Lord..."** (*Ὁ δῆμος τῶν Ἰουδαίων*), continuing through Antiphon Fourteen. At this point takes place the Procession of the Holy Cross, as has now become customary almost everywhere: the Priest, carrying the Holy Crucifix and preceded by candle-bearers and the hand-censer [*katzion*], exits from the Sanctuary and proceeds in solemn procession around the temple, chanting in a recitative melody: **"Today is hung upon the Tree, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters"** (*Σήμερον κρεμᾶται ἐπὶ ξύλου, ὁ ἐν ὕδασι τὴν γῆν κρεμάσας*) three times, which is repeated by the choir. The Antiphons continue until the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: **"Thou hast ransomed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; having been nailed to the Cross and pierced with the spear, Thou didst pour forth immortality upon mankind. O our Savior, glory to Thee!"** (*Ἐξηγόρασας ἡμᾶς ἐκ τῆς κατάρας τοῦ νόμου*); then the Sixth Gospel [Mark 15:16–32: The Mocking by the Soldiers and the Crucifixion at Golgotha] is read. Then the First Choir begins the Beatitudes [Makarismoi], at the conclusion of which is chanted the Prokimenon in Tone 4 three times: **"They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots"** (*Διεμερίσαντο τὰ ἱμάτιά μου ἑαυτοῖς*, Ps. 21:18 [LXX]), and the Seventh Gospel [Matthew 27:33–54: The Crucifixion, the Darkness, and the Death of Jesus] is read. Then Psalm 50 [LXX], and the Eighth Gospel [Luke 23:32–49: The Penitent Thief and the Death of Christ] is read; then the First Choir begins the Canon of the Triodion: **"Early in the morning my soul rises unto Thee, O Word of God..."** (*Πρὸς σὲ ὀρθρίζω*) by Saint Cosmas the Melodist, with four Troparia at each Ode, without biblical verses, and at the end of each Ode the Heirmos is chanted slowly as Katavasia. After Ode 5 is sung, the Small Litany, the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou dost endure the Cross, Thou art my Son and my God!"** (*Τὸν δι' ἡμᾶς σταυρωθέντα*), the Ikos, the Menologion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion. Then Ode 8 of the Canon, at the end of which the Deacon exclaims: **"The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!"** (*Τὴν Θεοτόκον καὶ μητέρα τοῦ φωτός*), and the Ninth Ode is sung. The Small Litany, the Exaposteilarion in Tone 3: **"The wise thief in a single hour Thou didst account worthy of Paradise, O Lord; enlighten me also by the wood of the Cross, and save me!"** (*Τὸν λῃστὴν αὐθημερὸν*) three times. The Ninth Gospel [John 19:25–37: Jesus Commends His Mother to John, "It Is Finished", and the Piercing of His Side] is then read. At the Praises: the three Idiomela with their verses are sung, repeating the first: Verse 1: **"Praise Him for His mighty acts, praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness!"** (*Αἰνεῖτε αὐτὸν ἐπὶ ταῖς δυναστείαις αὐτοῦ*, Ps. 150:2 [LXX]); Idiomelon 1: **"Two evil deeds has My first-born son Israel committed: he has forsaken Me, the fountain of living water, and has dug for himself a broken cistern..."** (*Δύο καὶ πονηρὰ ἐποίησεν*). Verse 2: **"Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet, praise Him with lute and harp!"** (*Αἰνεῖτε αὐτὸν ἐν ἤχῳ σάλπιγγος*, Ps. 150:3 [LXX]); Idiomelon 1 repeated. Verse 3: **"Praise Him with timbrel and dance, praise Him with strings and pipes!"** (*Αἰνεῖτε αὐτὸν ἐν τυμπάνῳ καὶ χορῷ*, Ps. 150:4 [LXX]); Idiomelon 2: **"Every member of Thy holy flesh endured dishonor for our sake: the head with thorns, the face with spittings, the cheeks with buffetings, the mouth with vinegar mingled with gall, the ears with impious blasphemies, the back with scourgings, the hand with the reed, the whole body with extension upon the Cross, the limbs with nails, and the side with the spear. O Thou Who didst suffer for us and didst free us from passions, Who didst condescend to us in Thy love for mankind and didst raise us up: O Almighty Savior, have mercy on us!"** (*Ἕκαστον μέλος τῆς ἁγίας σου σαρκός*)...[^446]

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday Evening (Service of the Holy Passions Concluded: Gospels X–XII, Praises Concluded, Aposticha, and Dismissal) (§ 80 Concluded)

## Μεγάλη Ἑβδομάς: Ἀκολουθία τῶν Ἁγίων Παθῶν (Τέλος) (§ 80 Τέλος)

Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, τὸ ι΄ Εὐαγγέλιον καὶ εὐθὺς *Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*, *Πληρώσωμεν*, καὶ τὸ ια΄ Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ εὐθὺς τὰ Ἀπόστιχα, μεθ' ἃ τὸ ιβ΄ Εὐαγγέλιον ὑπὸ τοῦ Διακόνου ἀπὸ τοῦ ἄμβωνος, τὸ *Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*, τὸ *Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι τῷ Κυρίῳ*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Ἀπολυτίκιον *Ἐξηγόρασας ἡμᾶς* ἐκ γ΄, εἶτα *Ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς ὁ Θεός . . . ὑπὲρ τῶν εὐσεβῶν καὶ ὀρθοδόξων χριστιανῶν . . . ὑπὲρ τοῦ Ἀρχιεπισκόπου ἡμῶν . . . Ὅτι ἐλεήμων καὶ φιλάνθρωπος*, *Σοφία*, *Ὁ ὢν Εὐλογητός*, τὸ *Στερεώσαι Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς* ὑπὸ τοῦ Ἀρχιερέως ἢ τοῦ Προεστῶτος, ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡ ἐλπὶς ἡμῶν δόξα σοι*, *ὁ ἐμπτυσμοὺς καὶ μάστιγας καὶ κολαφισμοὺς καὶ σταυρὸν καὶ θάνατον ὑπομείνας διὰ τὴν τοῦ κόσμου σωτηρίαν Χριστὸς ὁ ἀληθινὸς . . .* κτλ. καὶ τὸ *Δι' εὐχῶν*.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Thursday Evening (Service of the Holy Passions Concluded: Gospels X–XII, Praises Concluded, Aposticha, and Dismissal) (§ 80 Concluded)

## Great and Holy Thursday Evening: Service of the Holy Passions Concluded (§ 80 Concluded)

... Verse 4: **"Praise Him with sounding cymbals, praise Him with loud clashing cymbals! Let everything that has breath praise the Lord!"** (*Αἰνεῖτε αὐτὸν ἐν κυμβάλοις εὐήχοις*, Ps. 150:5–6 [LXX]); Idiomelon 3: **"Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, all creation trembled; the foundations of the earth shook with fear of Thy might; for when Thou wast lifted up today, the Hebrew race perished..."** (*Σταυρωθέντα σε θεωροῦσα*). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: **"They stripped Me of My garments and clothed Me in a scarlet robe; they placed upon My head a crown of thorns and gave a reed into My right hand, that I might smash them like a potter's vessels"** (*Ἐξέδυσάν με τὰ ἱμάτιά μου*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: **"I gave My back to scourgings, and My cheeks to blows, and I turned not My face from the shame of spittings; I stood before the tribunal of Pilate and endured the Cross for the salvation of the world"** (*Τὸν νῶτόν μου ἔδωκα εἰς μάστιγας*). Then the Tenth Gospel [Mark 15:43–47: Joseph of Arimathea Obtains the Body of Jesus and Lays It in a Tomb] is read, and immediately: **"To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God, and to Thee we send up glory: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** (*Σοὶ δόξα πρέπει*) read plain. The Deacon: **"Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord"** (*Πληρώσωμεν τὴν ἑωθινὴν δέησιν*), and the Eleventh Gospel [John 19:38–42: The Burial of Jesus by Joseph and Nicodemus] is proclaimed. Then, immediately, the Aposticha: 1. **"All creation was altered with fear when it beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ; the sun was darkened, and the foundations of the earth were shaken..."** (*Πᾶσα ἡ κτίσις ἠλλοιοῦτο φόβῳ*); the Verse: **"They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots"** (*Διεμερίσαντο τὰ ἱμάτιά μου*, Ps. 21:18 [LXX]); 2. **"Why does the impious and lawless people imagine vain things? Why did they condemn to death the Life of all?..."** (*Λαὸς δυσσεβὴς καὶ παράνομος*); the Verse: **"They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink"** (*Ἔδωκαν εἰς τὸ βρῶμά μου χολὴν*, Ps. 68:21 [LXX]); 3. **"Today, seeing Thee, O Word, hanging upon the Cross, the blameless Virgin wept with maternal sorrow..."** (*Σήμερον σὲ θεωροῦσα ἡ ἄμεμπτος Παρθένος*); the Verse: **"God is our King before the ages; He has worked salvation in the midst of the earth"** (*Ὁ δὲ Θεὸς βασιλεὺς ἡμῶν πρὸ αἰώνων*, Ps. 73:12 [LXX]); 4. **"Beholding Thee, the Creator and God of all, lifted up upon the Tree, all creation trembled..."** (*Ἐπὶ ξύλου βλέπουσα κρεμάμενον*). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: **"O Lord, when Thou didst ascend the Cross, fear and trembling fell upon creation..."** (*Κύριε, ἀναβαίνοντός σου ἐν τῷ Σταυρῷ*); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 8: **"The reed of denial has already been dipped by the unjust judges..."** (*Ἤδη βάπτεται κάλαμος ἀποφάσεως*). The Twelfth Gospel [Matthew 27:62–66: The Guard Set at the Tomb] is read by the Deacon from the ambo. At the conclusion of the reading, the Archbishop blesses him, and the choir chants: **"Many years to you, Master!"** (*Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*). The Priest: **"It is good to give praise unto the Lord, and to sing unto Thy name, O Most High"** (*Ἀγαθὸν τὸ ἐξομολογεῖσθαι*, Ps. 91:1 [LXX]). The Reader: the Trisagion Prayers. The Chanter: the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: **"Thou hast ransomed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; having been nailed to the Cross and pierced with the spear, Thou didst pour forth immortality upon mankind. O our Savior, glory to Thee!"** (*Ἐξηγόρασας ἡμᾶς*) three times. The Deacon intones the Augmented Litany: **"Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee: hear and have mercy"**; **"Again we pray for all pious and Orthodox Christians"**; **"Again we pray for our Archbishop [N.]"**. The Priest gives the exclamation: **"For Thou art a merciful and loving God, and unto Thee we send up glory: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** The Deacon: **"Wisdom!"** The Priest: **"Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages"** (*Ὁ ὢν εὐλογητός*). The Archbishop [or Superior / Proestos]: **"Confirm, O Lord God, the holy Orthodox faith of pious and Orthodox Christians unto ages of ages"** (*Στερεώσαι Κύριος ὁ Θεός*). The Priest: **"Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee. May Christ our true God, Who endured spittings, and scourgings, buffetings, and the Cross and Death for the salvation of the world, through the intercessions of His all-pure and blameless holy Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind"** (*Δόξα σοι ὁ Θεὸς ἡ ἐλπὶς ἡμῶν δόξα σοι· ὁ ἐμπτυσμοὺς καὶ μάστιγας...*), and: **"Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen."** (*Δι' εὐχῶν*).[^447]

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (The Royal Hours: First and Third Hours) (§ 81)

## Μεγάλη Παρασκευή: Αἱ Βασιλικαὶ Ὧραι (Ὧραι Α΄ καὶ Γ΄) (§ 81)

**ΜΕΓΑΛΗ ΠΑΡΑΣΚΕΥΗ**

**ΑΙ ΩΡΑΙ**

81.    Περὶ τὴν α΄ πρωϊνὴν ὥραν τῆς ἡμέρας εὐλογήσαντος τοῦ Ἱερέως, ὁ Προεστὼς τὸ *Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης τὸ Τρισάγιον, ὁ Ἱερεὺς *Ὅτι σοῦ ἐστιν*, ὁ Ἀναγνώστης *Κύριε ἐλέησον* ιβ΄, *Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν*, *Δεῦτε προσκυνήσωμεν* γ΄ καὶ ἀναγινώσκει τοὺς ψαλμοὺς τῆς α΄ Ὥρας, εἶτα *Δόξα, Σταυρωθέντος σου Χριστέ, Καὶ νῦν, Τί σε καλέσωμεν* καὶ ἄρχεται ὁ α΄ Χορὸς ψάλλων τὸ α΄ Ἰδιόμελον, ὁ β΄ μετὰ τοῦ Στίχου *Ἵνα τί ἐφρύαξαν ἔθνη*, καὶ ψάλλει τὸ β΄, καὶ αὖθις ὁ α΄ *Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Τοῖς συλλαβοῦσί σε παρανόμοις*· ἀκολούθως ἡ Προφητεία, ὁ Ἀπόστολος, καὶ τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον, καὶ εὐθὺς *Τὰ διαβήματά μου*, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Κοντάκιον *Τὸν δι' ἡμᾶς σταυρωθέντα*, *Κύριε ἐλέησον* μ΄, *Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ* καὶ ὁ Προεστὼς τὴν Εὐχήν· *Χριστὲ τὸ φῶς*, καὶ ἄρχεται ἡ γ΄ Ὥρα ⁴⁵.

Εἰς τὴν γ΄ Ὥραν ἄρχεται τοῦ Ἰδιομέλου ὁ β΄ Χορός· ὁ α΄ μετὰ τοῦ Στίχου *Τὰ ῥήματά μου ἐνώτισαι Κύριε* τὸ β΄, καὶ πάλιν ὁ β΄ *Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Ἑλκόμενος ἐπὶ Σταυροῦ*, ἡ Προφητεία, ὁ Ἀπόστολος, τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ ὡς ἄνωθεν, ἡ Εὐχὴ *Δέσποτα Θεέ*, καὶ ἄρχεται ἡ ϛ΄ Ὥρα.

---

45. Κατ' αὐτήν, ὡς καὶ εἰς τὰς δύο ἐφεξῆς Ὥρας θυμιᾷ διὰ κατζίου ἀνὰ εἷς Διάκονος κατὰ τὴν τάξιν αὐτῶν ἐνδυόμενος τὴν συνήθη μανδύαν.

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (The Royal Hours: First and Third Hours) (§ 81)

## Great and Holy Friday: The Royal Hours (First and Third Hours) (§ 81)

**GREAT AND HOLY FRIDAY**

**THE ROYAL HOURS**

81.    Around the First Hour of the morning [7:00 a.m.], the Priest having given the blessing, the Superior [Proestos] says: **"O Heavenly King, Comforter, the Spirit of truth..."** (*Βασιλεῦ οὐράνιε*); the Reader: the Trisagion Prayers; the Priest: **"For Thine is the kingdom..."**; the Reader: **"Lord, have mercy"** (12 times), **"Glory... Both now and ever..."**, **"O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God..."** (3 times), and he reads the Psalms of the First Hour: Psalm 5 [LXX]: **"Give ear to my words, O Lord..."**; Psalm 2 [LXX]: **"Why do the nations rage..."**; and Psalm 21 [LXX]: **"O God, my God, attend to me; why hast Thou forsaken me?..."**. Then "Glory...": **"When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, the tyranny was destroyed, and the power of the enemy was trampled underfoot; for neither an angel nor a man, but the Lord Himself saved us. Glory to Thee!"** (*Σταυρωθέντος σου Χριστέ*); "Both now and ever...": **"What shall we call thee, O full of grace?..."** (*Τί σε καλέσωμεν*). The First Choir begins chanting the first Idiomelon in Tone 8: **"Today the veil of the temple is rent in twain for the rebuke of the lawless, and the sun hides its rays beholding the Master crucified"** (*Σήμερον τοῦ ναοῦ τὸ καταπέτασμα*); the Second Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 8: **"As a sheep led to the slaughter wast Thou brought, O Christ King, and as an innocent lamb nailed to the Cross by lawless men on account of our sins, O Lover of mankind"** (*Ὡς πρόβατον ἐπὶ σφαγὴν*), preceded by the verse: **"Why do the nations rage, and the peoples plot in vain?"** (*Ἵνα τί ἐφρύαξαν ἔθνη*, Ps. 2:1 [LXX]); and again the First Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Unto the transgressors who seized Thee, O Christ, Thou didst cry aloud: What harm did I do unto you, or wherein did I provoke you?..."** (*Τοῖς συλλαβοῦσί σε παρανόμοις*). Next, the Prophecy: Zechariah 11:10–13 [LXX] (The Thirty Pieces of Silver Cast into the Furnace in the House of the Lord); the Epistle: Galatians 6:14–18 (Glorying only in the Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ); and the Gospel: Matthew 27:1–56 (The Trial before Pilate, the Mocking, the Crucifixion, and the Death of Jesus). Directly the Reader says: **"Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me..."** (*Τὰ διαβήματά μου*, Ps. 118:133 [LXX]), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion in Tone 8: **"Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou dost endure the Cross, Thou art my Son and my God!"** (*Τὸν δι' ἡμᾶς σταυρωθέντα*), **"Lord, have mercy"** (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: **"Thou Who at all times and at every hour, in heaven and on earth, art worshipped and glorified, O Christ God..."** (*Ὁ ἐν παντὶ καιρῷ*), and the Superior [Proestos] says the concluding prayer: **"O Christ, the True Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that cometh into the world..."** (*Χριστὲ τὸ φῶς*), and the Third Hour begins.⁴⁵

At the Third Hour, the service begins as at the First Hour, then Psalms 34, 108, and 50 [LXX]. "Glory...": **"O Lord, the Jews condemned Thee to death, the Life of all..."** (*Κύριε, κατέκριναν σε Ἰουδαῖοι θανάτῳ*); "Both now and ever...": **"O Theotokos, thou art the true vine that blossomed for us the fruit of life..."** (*Θεοτόκε, σὺ εἶ ἡ ἄμπελος*). The Second Choir begins the first Idiomelon in Tone 6: **"Through fear of the Jews, Peter Thy friend and companion denied Thee, O Lord..."** (*Διὰ τὸν φόβον τῶν Ἰουδαίων*); then the First Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 6: **"Before Thy precious Cross, when the soldiers mocked Thee, O Lord, the angelic hosts were struck with dread..."** (*Πρὸ τοῦ τιμίου σου Σταυροῦ*), preceded by the verse: **"Give ear to my words, O Lord; understand my cry"** (*Τὰ ῥήματά μου ἐνώτισαι Κύριε*, Ps. 5:1 [LXX]). Then the Second Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Drawn upon the Cross, Thou didst cry aloud, O Lord: For which deed do the Jews wish to stone Me? Because I healed their paralytics?..."** (*Ἑλκόμενος ἐπὶ Σταυροῦ*). The Prophecy: Isaiah 50:4–11 [LXX] (The Obedient Servant Gives His Back to Scourges); the Epistle: Romans 5:6–10 (While We Were Still Sinners, Christ Died for Us); then the Gospel: Mark 15:16–41 (The Soldiers Mock Christ, the Crucifixion, and His Death on the Cross). The Reader: **"Blessed is the Lord God, blessed is the Lord day by day..."** (*Εὐλογητὸς Κύριος ὁ Θεός*), and the rest as above: the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: **"Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us..."**, **"Lord, have mercy"** (40 times), **"Thou Who at all times..."**, the Prayer of Saint Mardarios: **"O Master God, Father Almighty, Lord, Only-Begotten Son Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit, one Divinity, one Power, have mercy on me a sinner..."** (*Δέσποτα Θεέ*), and the Sixth Hour begins.[^448]

---

45. At this Hour, as also at the two following Hours, one Deacon in turn according to the order of seniority, vested in the customary mandya, censes with the hand-censer [*katzion*].

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (The Royal Hours: Sixth and Ninth Hours) (§§ 82, 83)

## Μεγάλη Παρασκευή: Αἱ Βασιλικαὶ Ὧραι (Ὧραι Ϛ΄ καὶ Θ΄) (§§ 82, 83)

82.    Εἰς τὴν ϛ΄ Ὥραν, ὁ α΄ Χορὸς ψάλλει τὸ α΄ Ἰδιόμελον, ὁ β΄ μετὰ τοῦ Στίχου *Ἔδωκαν εἰς τὸ βρῶμά μου χολὴν* τὸ β΄, *Δόξα, Καὶ νῦν, Δεῦτε χριστοφόροι λαοί*, ἡ Προφητεία, ὁ Ἀπόστολος, τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον κτλ. ὡς ἄνωθεν, ἡ Εὐχὴ *Θεὲ καὶ Κύριε τῶν δυνάμεων*, καὶ ἄρχεται ἡ θ΄ Ὥρα.

83.    Εἰς τὴν θ΄ Ὥραν, ὁ β΄ Χορὸς ἄρχεται τοῦ α΄ Ἰδιομέλου, τὸ β΄ λέγει ὁ α΄ μετὰ τοῦ Στίχου *Διεμερίσαντο τὰ ἱμάτιά μου ἑαυτοῖς*, τὸ δὲ τέλος ἀπὸ τοῦ *Σὺν Πατρὶ καὶ Ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι* λαμβάνει ὁ β΄ Χορός· ὁ δὲ α΄ ἄρχεται *Δόξα Πατρί*, ὁ β΄ *Καὶ νῦν καὶ ἀεί*, καὶ ὁ Κανονάρχης ἐρχόμενος εἰς τὸ μέσον τοῦ Ναοῦ ἀναγινώσκει κατὰ τὸ ὕφος τοῦ Ἀποστόλου τὸ Στιχηρὸν *Σήμερον κρεμᾶται ἐπὶ ξύλου, ὁ ἐν ὕδασι τὴν γῆν κρεμάσας* ἐκ γ΄, καὶ τὸ τέλος *Προσκυνοῦμέν σου τὰ πάθη Χριστὲ* ὡσαύτως ἐκ γ΄, εἶτα ψάλλεται τὸ αὐτὸ Στιχηρὸν ὑπ' ἀμφοτέρων τῶν Χορῶν κατὰ Στίχον, ἡ Προφητεία, ὁ Ἀπόστολος, τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον, τὸ Τρισάγιον, τὸ Κοντάκιον κτλ. ἡ Εὐχὴ *Δέσποτα Κύριε Ἰησοῦ Χριστέ*, ἡ Ἀπόλυσις καὶ ἄρχεται

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (The Royal Hours: Sixth and Ninth Hours) (§§ 82, 83)

## Great and Holy Friday: The Royal Hours (Sixth and Ninth Hours) (§§ 82, 83)

... the prayer: **"O God and Master, Father Almighty, Lord, Only-Begotten Son Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit..."** (*Δέσποτα Θεέ*), and the Sixth Hour begins.

82.    The Sixth Hour begins as the previous two Hours, with Psalms 53, 139, and 90 [LXX]. "Glory...": **"Thou hast worked salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God; Thou didst stretch out Thy most pure hands upon the Cross; Thou didst gather all the nations that cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, glory to Thee!"** (*Εἰργάσω σωτηρίαν ἐν μέσῳ τῆς γῆς*); "Both now and ever...": **"Seeing that we have no boldness because of our manifold sins, do thou entreat Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin Theotokos..."** (*Ὅτι οὐκ ἔχομεν παρρησίαν*). The First Choir chants the first Idiomelon in Tone 8: **"Thus saith the Lord unto the Jews: My people, what have I done unto you, or wherein have I grieved you? Your blind have I given sight, your lepers have I cleansed, the man lying on his pallet have I restored. My people, what have I done unto you, and wherewith have you repaid Me? Instead of manna, gall; instead of water, vinegar; instead of loving Me, you have nailed Me to the Cross!..."** (*Τάδε λέγει Κύριος πρὸς Ἰουδαίους*); the Second Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 8: **"O lawgivers of Israel, Jews and Pharisees, the choir of the Apostles cries aloud unto you: Behold the Temple which you destroyed; behold the Lamb Whom you crucified and delivered unto the tomb!..."** (*Οἱ νομοθέται Ἰσραήλ*), preceded by the verse: **"They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink"** (*Ἔδωκαν εἰς τὸ βρῶμά μου χολὴν*, Ps. 68:21 [LXX]). Then the First Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": **"Come, O Christ-bearing peoples, let us behold what counsel Judas the traitor took with the lawless priests against our Savior..."** (*Δεῦτε χριστοφόροι λαοί*). Next, the Prophecy: Isaiah 52:13–54:1 [LXX] (The Suffering Servant: He Was Wounded for Our Transgressions); the Epistle: Hebrews 2:11–18 (He Who Sanctifies and Those Who Are Sanctified Are All from One); and the Gospel: Luke 23:32–49 (The Crucifixion, the Penitent Thief, the Veil Rent, and the Death of Jesus). The Reader: **"Let Thy tender mercies speedily prevent us, O Lord, for we are brought very low..."** (*Ταχὺ προκαταλαβέτωσαν ἡμᾶς οἱ οἰκτιρμοί σου*, Ps. 78:8 [LXX]), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: **"Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us..."**, **"Lord, have mercy"** (40 times), **"Thou Who at all times..."**, the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: **"O God and Lord of powers, and Maker of all creation, Who through the compassion of Thine inscrutable mercy didst send down Thine Only-Begotten Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, for the salvation of our race..."** (*Θεὲ καὶ Κύριε τῶν δυνάμεων*), and the Ninth Hour begins.

83.    The Ninth Hour begins as the previous three Hours, with Psalms 68, 69, and 85 [LXX]. "Glory...": **"When the thief beheld the Author of life hanging upon the Cross, he said: If Thou wert not God incarnate Who art crucified with us, the sun would not have hidden its rays, nor would the earth have shaken with trembling. But O Thou Who endurest all things, remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom!"** (*Ὁρῶν ὁ λῃστὴς τὸν ἀρχηγὸν τῆς ζωῆς*); "Both now and ever...": **"O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst despoil death and didst manifest the Resurrection as God, despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thine own hand..."** (*Ὁ δι' ἡμᾶς γεννηθεὶς ἐκ Παρθένου*). The Second Choir begins the first Idiomelon in Tone 7: **"A marvelous and fearful wonder it was to behold today: the Creator of heaven and earth hangs upon the Tree; He Who suspended the earth upon the waters is transfixed with nails; the sun hides its rays at the sight of the Master dishonored..."** (*Θάμβος ἦν κατιδεῖν τὸν οὐρανοῦ καὶ γῆς Ποιητήν*); then the First Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 2: **"When the transgressors nailed the Lord of glory upon the Cross, they mocked Him and said: If Thou art the Son of God, save Thyself and come down from the Cross!..."** (*Ὅτε σε σταυρῷ προσήλωσαν παράνομοι*), preceded by the verse: **"They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots"** (*Διεμερίσαντο τὰ ἱμάτιά μου ἑαυτοῖς*, Ps. 21:18 [LXX]). The Second Choir takes up the conclusion to this Idiomelon: **"...Together with the Father and the Holy Spirit!"** (*Σὺν Πατρὶ καὶ Ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι*). The First Choir chants: **"Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit"**; the Second Choir: **"Both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."** At this point the Canonarch, coming into the middle of the temple [before the Holy Cross], intones in the recitative manner of the Epistle the solemn Sticheron:
**"Today is hung upon the Tree, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters"** (3 times) (*Σήμερον κρεμᾶται ἐπὶ ξύλου, ὁ ἐν ὕδασι τὴν γῆν κρεμάσας*):
**"A crown of thorns crowns Him Who is the King of angels."**
**"He is wrapped in false purple, Who wrapped the heavens with clouds."**
**"He received buffetings, Who freed Adam in the Jordan."**
**"He was transfixed with nails, Who is the Bridegroom of the Church."**
**"He was pierced with a spear, Who is the Son of the Virgin."**
**"We worship Thy Passion, O Christ!"** (3 times) (*Προσκυνοῦμέν σου τὰ πάθη Χριστέ*):
**"Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!"** (*Δεῖξον ἡμῖν καὶ τὴν ἔνδοξόν σου Ἀνάστασιν*).
Then this same Sticheron is chanted by both choirs verse by verse. The Prophecy: Jeremiah 11:18–12:5, 9–11, 14–15 [LXX] (The Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter); the Epistle: Hebrews 10:19–31 (A New and Living Way Opened through the Veil of His Flesh); the Gospel: John 19:23–37 (The Crucifixion, the Parting of the Garments, "It Is Finished", and the Piercing of His Side). The Reader: the Trisagion Prayers; the Kontakion: **"Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us..."**; **"Lord, have mercy"** (40 times); **"Thou Who at all times..."**; the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: **"O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who hast patiently endured our transgressions, and hast brought us even unto this present hour, in which, hanging upon the life-creating Tree, Thou didst open the way into Paradise unto the penitent thief, and by death didst destroy death..."** (*Δέσποτα Κύριε Ἰησοῦ Χριστέ*); the Dismissal, and begins...[^449]

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (Vespers of the Taking Down from the Cross Opening) (§ 84 Opening)

## Μεγάλη Παρασκευή: Ὁ Ἑσπερινὸς (Ἡ Ἀποκαθήλωσις Ἀρχή) (§ 84 Ἀρχή)

**Ο ΕΣΠΕΡΙΝΟΣ**  
**[Η ΑΠΟΚΑΘΗΛΩΣΙΣ]**

84.    Ἐν τῷ Μ. Ἑσπερινῷ, μετὰ τὸν Προοιμιακόν, εἰς τὸ *Κύριε ἐκέκραξα* ψάλλομεν τὰ τῶν Ἀποστίχων ε΄ Ἰδιόμελα *Πᾶσα ἡ κτίσις* [Δὶς] κτλ. εἰς ϛ΄. *Δόξα, Ὢ πῶς ἡ παράνομος συναγωγή, Καὶ νῦν, Φοβερὸν καὶ παράδοξον μυστήριον*, Εἴσοδος μετὰ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου, τὸ *Φῶς ἱλαρὸν* καὶ εὐθὺς τὰ Ἀναγνώσματα, ὁ Ἀπόστολος καὶ ἀπ' ἄμβωνος τὸ Εὐαγγέλιον· τὸ *Εἴπωμεν πάντες*, τὸ *Καταξίωσον*, *Πληρώσωμεν τὴν ἑσπερινὴν δέησιν*, καὶ μετὰ τὸ *Εἴη τὸ κράτος*, οἱ Χοροὶ ἔρχονται πρὸς τὴν βορείαν Πύλην τοῦ ἁγίου Βήματος καὶ ἄρχονται ψάλλοντες τὰ Ἀπόστιχα, ἓξ δὲ Ἱερεῖς ἀσκεπεῖς ἐνδεδυμένοι φελώνια καὶ αἴροντες μετ' εὐλαβείας ἐπὶ κεφαλῆς τὸν ἐπιτάφιον, ἐξ ὧν ὁ Μέγας Ἀρχιμανδρίτης φέρει ἐν τῇ δεξιᾷ καὶ τὸ ἱερὸν Εὐαγγέλιον, ἐξέρχονται τοῦ ἱεροῦ Βήματος προπορευομένων τῶν λαμπάδων, τῶν ἑξαπτερύγων, τῶν Ψαλτῶν, τοῦ Πριμικηρίου μετὰ τοῦ Διβαμβούλου καὶ τῶν Διακόνων θυμιώντων ἔρχονται εἰς τὸ μέσον τοῦ Ναοῦ ὅπου ἵσταται ηὐτρεπισμένον τὸ Κουβούκλιον, καὶ οἱ μὲν Χοροὶ τῶν Ψαλτῶν ἵστανται κατὰ σειρὰν ψάλλοντες τὰ λοιπὰ τῶν Ἀποστίχων, οἱ δὲ Ἱερεῖς φέροντες ἐπὶ τῆς κεφαλῆς τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον, ποιοῦσι τρεῖς κύκλους περὶ τὸ Κουβούκλιον, εἶτα τιθέασιν αὐτὸν ἐντὸς τοῦ Κουβουκλίου, καὶ ὁ Μ. Ἐκκλησιάρχης ῥαίνει μὲ εὔοσμα ἄνθη αὐτό, ὁ δὲ Πατριάρχης κατερχόμενος τοῦ Θρόνου προσέρχεται, ἀσπάζεται τὸ ἱερὸν Εὐαγγέλιον καὶ τὸν Ἐπιτάφιον καὶ εὐλογήσας τὸν λαόν, τῶν Χορῶν ψαλλόντων τὸ *Εἰς πολλὰ ἔτη*, ἀνέρχεται εἰς τὸν Θρόνον, οἱ δὲ ἅγιοι Ἀρχιερεῖς προσερχόμενοι ἀνὰ δύο ἀσπάζονται ἐπίσης καὶ ἔπειτα λαμβάνουσι παρὰ τοῦ Πατριάρχου ἄνθη, ὡσαύτως καὶ πάντες

# Holy Week: Great and Holy Friday (Vespers of the Taking Down from the Cross Opening) (§ 84 Opening)

## Great and Holy Friday: Vespers of the Taking Down from the Cross Opening (§ 84 Opening)

... **"Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!"** (*Δεῖξον ἡμῖν καὶ τὴν ἔνδοξόν σου Ἀνάστασιν*).

This is repeated antiphonally by both choirs verse by verse. Then the Prophecy (Jeremiah 11:18–12:4, 9–10, and 14–15 [LXX]: The Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter); the Epistle (Hebrews 10:19–31: A New and Living Way Opened through the Veil of His Flesh); and the Gospel (John 19:23–37: The Parting of the Garments, "It Is Finished", and the Piercing of His Side). Then the Reader: **"Deliver us not up utterly, for Thy holy Name's sake, and make not Thy covenant void..."** (*Μὴ δὴ παραδῴης ἡμᾶς εἰς τέλος*).
