On Tuesday: Ode 1 of the Menaion; Ode 2 of the Triodion; Ode 3 of the Menaion and Heirmos of Ode 2; Small Litany; Sessional Hymns of the Menaion; Odes 4, 5, and 6 of the Menaion; Heirmos of the Menaion; Small Litany; Martyrikon; Menologion; Odes 7 and 8 of the Menaion and Triodia of Ode 8; Ode 9 of the Menaion and Triodia of Ode 9. This proportion is observed on all weekdays through Friday throughout Great Lent. On Wednesday and Friday of Cheesefare week, because the Triodion has Canons for all Odes, the Heirmos of Ode 6 is taken from the Triodion rather than the Menaion. At Ode 9, we chant "We praise, we bless..." and the Heirmos of the Second Canon of Ode 8 of the Triodion; "More honorable than the Cherubim"; the Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Second Canon of the Triodion, followed by "It is truly right to call thee blessed"; the Small Litany and Exclamation: "For all the powers of heaven praise Thee..."; the Photagogikon [Hymn of Light] of the Tone thrice; the Small Doxology: "To Thee belongs glory..."; and the Litany of Fervent Supplication [Ektenis]. Footnote 10 provides the exact daily endings for the Photagogika: On Monday: 1st Photagogikon: "Through the intercessions of the Bodiless Powers, and save me"; 2nd Photagogikon: "Through the intercessions of Thy Saint [of the Church], and save me"; 3rd Photagogikon: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, and save me". On Tuesday: "Through the intercessions of the Forerunner, and save me". On Wednesday and Friday: "By the power of Thy Cross, and save me". On Thursday: "Through the intercessions of the Apostles, and save me". On Saturday: the Exaposteilaria for the dead: "O Thou Who rulest over the dead and the living" and "In Thee do we boast"; or according to exact rubrics, the Photagogikon of the Tone twice with "Through the intercessions of Thy Saints" and "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos", followed by "O Thou Who rulest over the dead and the living".
[^409]: Great Lent: Weekday Aposticha Concl., Hours, Beatitudes / Typika at Ninth Hour, and Transition to Vespers (§ 19 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p699 (printed page 684) and facing English leaf p700 (printed page 685). Weekday Matins, Hours, and Typika Order: Paragraph 19 concludes weekday Matins: Following the Litany of Fervent Supplication and the Exclamation: "For Thine it is to have mercy on us and to save us...", the Idiomelon of the Matins Aposticha is chanted twice, with the Verses: "We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord..." [Ps. 89:14 LXX] and "And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us..." [Ps. 89:17 LXX]; then the Martyrikon once; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion on Wednesday and Friday); "It is good to give praise unto the Lord..." [Ps. 91:2 LXX]; the Trisagion Prayers; the Dismissal Troparion: "Standing in the temple of Thy glory, we seem to stand in heaven, O Theotokos..."; "Lord, have mercy" 40 times; "Glory... Both now and ever..."; "More honorable than the Cherubim"; "In the name of the Lord, bless, father"; the Priest: "He Who is blessed, Christ our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."; the Reader: "O Heavenly King, strengthen the faith..."; followed by three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephraim the Syrian. The Hours (First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth) are read according to the Triodion and Horologion. At the Ninth Hour, following the Kontakia ("When the thief beheld the Author of life...", etc.), "Lord, have mercy" 40 times, "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, bless, father", the Priest says "Through the prayers of our holy Fathers...", and three great prostrations are made. Immediately, the Beatitudes [Makarizmoi] are chanted by the two choirs: "In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom"; the Troparia: "The heavenly choir praiseth Thee..."; the Symbol of Faith: "I believe in one God..."; "Remit, forgive, pardon, O God, our offenses..."; the Lord's Prayer: "Our Father..."; and the daily Kontakia: "On the mountain wast Thou transfigured, O Christ God..."; the Kontakion of the day (Footnote 11 lists: Monday: "O Archistrategi of God"; Tuesday: "O Prophet of God and Forerunner of grace"; Wednesday and Friday: "O Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross"; Thursday: "The sure and God-proclaiming heralds" and St. Nicholas: "In Myra, O holy one..."); the Kontakion of the Patron Saint of the Church; the Martyrikon of the Tone (Seventh Ode Martyrikon from the end of the Triodion); "Glory...": "With the Saints give rest..."; "Both now and ever...": "Steadfast protectress of Christians..."; "Lord, have mercy" 40 times; the prayer: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour..."; "Lord, have mercy" thrice; "More honorable than the Cherubim"; "In the name of the Lord, bless, father". The Priest exclaims: "May God be merciful unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us" [Ps. 66:2 LXX]; three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephraim; and the Proestos immediately begins reading the Introductory Psalm: "Bless the Lord, O my soul" [Ps. 103 LXX], which opens Vespers.
[^410]: Great Lent: Order of Lenten Vespers and Presanctified Liturgy Opening (§§ 20–21 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p701 (printed page 686) and facing English leaf p702 (printed page 687). Great Lenten Vespers (Aliturgical Order): Paragraph 20 codifies the structure of Great Lenten Vespers on days when no Presanctified Liturgy is celebrated (observed on Monday, Tuesday, and Thursday evenings of Great Lent when not superseded by a feast): At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six Stichera are chanted: three Prosomia from the Triodion and three of the Saint of the following day from the Menaion; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion on Tuesday or Thursday evenings). The Entrance with the censer is omitted. "O Gladsome Light" is read in recitativo (or chanted simply). The Prokimena and two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis and Proverbs follow. Then "Vouchsafe, O Lord", the Evening Litany ("Let us complete our evening prayer"), and the priestly Exclamation: "Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the day is chanted twice, the Martyrikon of the Tone once, and "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion). Footnote 12 specifies the psalm verses for the Aposticha: before the second singing of the Idiomelon, the Verse: "Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou Who dwellest in the heavens! Behold, as the eyes of servants look unto the hands of their masters, and as the eyes of a maiden unto the hands of her mistress, so our eyes look unto the Lord our God, until He have mercy upon us" (Ps. 122:1–2 LXX); and before the Martyrikon, the Verse: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us; for we are utterly filled with contempt: our soul is exceedingly filled with the reproach of the prosperous, and with the contempt of the proud" (Ps. 122:3–4 LXX). Then "Now Thou dost dismiss Thy servant", the Trisagion Prayers, and the festive Troparia chanted with melody: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos, Mary full of grace, the Lord is with thee; blessed art thou among women, and blessed is the fruit of thy womb, for thou hast borne the Savior of our souls" with a great prostration; "O Baptizer of Christ, remember us all, that we may be delivered from our transgressions, for unto thee is given grace to intercede for us" with a prostration; "Glory...": "Intercede for us, holy Apostles and all Saints, that we may be delivered from perils and sorrows, for we have acquired you as fervent advocates before the Savior" with a prostration; "Both now and ever...": "Beneath thy tender compassion we take refuge, O Theotokos; despise not our supplications in adversity, but deliver us from perils, O only pure and blessed one", read rapidly in recitativo (chuma) without prostration. "Lord, have mercy" 40 times; "Glory... Both now and ever..."; "More honorable than the Cherubim"; "In the name of the Lord, bless, father"; Priest: "He Who is, Christ our God, is blessed..."; Reader: "O Heavenly King, strengthen our faithful civil authorities..."; followed by the three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephraim the Syrian. Then the Trisagion Prayers again, "Lord, have mercy" 12 times, the prayer: "All-Holy Trinity, one-natured Might, undivided Kingdom, Cause of all good, be well pleased with me also, the sinner; confirm, instruct my heart, and cleanse away all my defilement; enlighten my mind, that I may ever glorify, hymn, and worship Thee, saying: One is holy, One is the Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen."; "Blessed be the name of the Lord" thrice; "Glory... Both now and ever..."; Psalm 33 LXX ("I will bless the Lord at all times"), and the Dismissal. Concluding Rubric & Presanctified Transition: Paragraph 20 concludes by contrasting this aliturgical order with the days when the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated: on Presanctified days, after the prayer "All-Holy Trinity, one-natured Might...", the Priest pronounces the Dismissal of Matins and immediately intones the solemn opening of the Divine Liturgy: "Blessed is the kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." Paragraph 21 initiates the Presanctified Liturgy: the Deacon (if serving) exclaims "Bless, Master!", the Priest proclaims the Kingdom, and the Proestos reads the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX).
[^411]: Great Lent: Order of Presanctified Liturgy Continued (§ 21 Continued: Eighteenth Kathisma, "Lord, I have cried", Entrance, Old Testament Readings, "Let my prayer arise", and Great Entrance). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p703 (printed page 688) and facing English leaf p704 (printed page 689). Presanctified Liturgy Structure: Paragraph 21 continues the order of the Presanctified Liturgy: After the Introductory Psalm, the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress...", Pss. 119–133 LXX, the Gradual Psalms or Songs of Ascents) is appended. Footnote 13 provides the historical and liturgical background: the formal order of the Typikon requires "Unto the Lord" (Ta Pros Kyrion) to be chanted on every weekday of Great Lent; however, in recent centuries, general custom has restricted its chanting exclusively to days when the Presanctified Liturgy is served, though certain traditional priests continue to recite it on every Monday of Great Lent. At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera are chanted: the Idiomelon of the day twice, the Martyrikon once, three Prosomia in sequence from the Triodion, and four Prosomia from the Menaion for the Saint of the following day. Footnote 14 records the Friday evening variation: on Friday at Vespers (on the eve of the Saturday of the Dead), the Idiomelon is sung twice, followed by the four Martyrika of the Tone from the end of the Triodion, four Stichera for the Saint; "Glory...": Nekrosimon Doxastikon (Hymn for the Dead); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion of the Tone of the week. On other days: "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion (or Stavrotheotokion on Tuesday and Thursday evenings). The Entrance is made with the censer (or with the Gospel if a Gospel pericope is appointed for the Saint), followed by "O Gladsome Light" and the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis and Proverbs. Footnote 15 describes the venerable ceremonial of the Presanctified light: after the first Reading (and the intervening second Prokimenon), the Reader exclaims "Command!" (Keleuson); the Priest, holding the censer in his right hand and a lighted candle in his left, turns toward the holy doors and exclaims: "Wisdom! Let us attend!"; then, turning toward the people, he elevates the candle and censer cross-wise, proclaiming: "The light of Christ enlightens all!" The faithful perform a full prostration. After the second Reading, the Priest exclaims to the Reader: "Peace be unto thee, reader", and the Deacon adds: "Wisdom!" Immediately the Priest, holding the censer and censing lightly before the Holy Table, intones: "Let my prayer arise in Thy sight as incense, and the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice" (Ps. 140:2 LXX). The two Choirs alternate chanting the four verses four times, while the faithful remain kneeling, and finally the Priest censes the people while singing "Let my prayer arise" up to the words "in Thy sight as incense", which the Choir concludes. Following "Let my prayer arise" and the three great prostrations, the Great Litany of Fervent Supplication is intoned, along with the litanies for the Catechumens and for Those Preparing for Holy Illumination. After the Exclamation: "According to the gift of Thy Christ, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", instead of the Cherubic Hymn, the ancient entrance hymn is chanted: "Now the powers of heaven do invisibly minister with us; for lo, the King of glory enters. Behold, the mystical sacrifice, fully accomplished, is ushered in. With faith and love let us draw near, that we may become partakers of eternal life. Alleluia." Footnote 16 details the Great Entrance of the Presanctified: the chanter halts the melody mid-sentence; the Priest, preceded by the Deacon censing, exits the Sanctuary bearing the Presanctified Holy Gifts upon his head in complete silence and profound solemnity, omitting all audible commemorations, because the sacrifice is already perfected and the gifts carried are the very Body and Blood of our Lord Jesus Christ. The people prostrate themselves in reverent adoration and remain bent down until the Priest re-enters the Sanctuary and places the Holy Gifts upon the Holy Table; then the chanter resumes the hymn from "Behold, the mystical sacrifice...". The Litany of Completion follows: "Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord", the priestly Exclamation: "And make us worthy, Master...", and the Lord's Prayer ("Our Father..."). The Deacon exclaims: "Let us attend!", and the Priest proclaims: "The Presanctified Holy Things for the holy!"
[^412]: Great Lent: Presanctified Liturgy Conclusion, Feasts of Saints in Lent, and Great Compline Opening (§§ 21 Concluded, 22, and 23 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p705 (printed page 690) and facing English leaf p706 (printed page 691). Presanctified Liturgy Conclusion: Paragraph 21 concludes the Presanctified Liturgy: Following the elevation of the Presanctified Gifts, the Communion Hymn [Koinonikon] is chanted: "Taste and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia" (Ps. 33:9 LXX). The Deacon exclaims: "With the fear of God, faith, and love, draw near", and the post-communion hymns are sung: "I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall be ever in my mouth" and "Taste of the heavenly Bread and the Cup of Life, and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia." The Priest recites the Prayer behind the Ambo: "O Master Almighty, Who hast created all creation in wisdom..."; then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord, henceforth and forever" thrice. The Priest exclaims: "The blessing of the Lord and His mercy be upon you...", and gives the Dismissal (during which "The Master and Chief Priest of us all, O Lord, preserve for many years!" is chanted if a bishop presides). After the Dismissal, Psalm 33 LXX ("I will bless the Lord at all times") and Psalm 144 LXX ("I will extol Thee, my God, my King") are read during the distribution of the Antidoron, concluding with "Through the prayers of our holy Fathers...". Feast Days in Great Lent: Paragraph 22 codifies the rubrics for an officially celebrated Saint (Polyeleos or Doxology rank) falling on fasting weekdays: 1) If on Wednesday: at Vespers, ten Stichera at "Lord, I have cried": the day's Idiomelon twice, the Martyrikon once, three Prosomia from the Triodion sequence, and four Prosomia for the celebrated Saint (not the Saint of the following day); "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion in the same Tone as the Doxastikon. Entrance with the Gospel. After "Let my prayer arise", the Prokimenon, Epistle [Apostolos], and Gospel of the celebrated Saint are read, and the Koinonikon of the Saint is chanted: "The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance. Alleluia" (Ps. 111:6 LXX). 2) If on Friday: at "Lord, I have cried", the day's Idiomelon twice, the four Martyrika of the Tone, and four Stichera for the Saint; "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": the first Theotokion of the Tone of the week. Two exceptions are noted: a) On Friday of the First Week of Lent (commemoration of the Great Martyr Theodore the Tyro and the miracle of the Kolyva), after the Idiomelon twice, four Stichera of Saint Theodore and four of the celebrated Saint are chanted; b) On Friday of the Fifth Week of Lent (eve of the Akathist Saturday), after the Idiomelon twice, four Stichera of the Theotokos ("Counsel eternal", Boulen proaionion) and four of the celebrated Saint are chanted. 3) If on Monday, Tuesday, or Thursday: at "Lord, I have cried", the Idiomelon of the Matins Aposticha of the day is chanted twice, the Martyrikon once, three Prosomia of the sequence from the Triodion, and four Stichera of the celebrated Saint; "Glory...": of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion; and the remainder of the Presanctified Liturgy proceeds as above. Great Compline Opening: Paragraph 23 initiates Great Compline (To Mega Apodeipnon): On Monday evening of the First Week of the Fast, the Priest exclaims: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", "O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of Truth...".
[^413]: Great Lent: Order of Great Compline Continued (§ 23 Continued: Hexapsalmos of Compline, "God is with us", Creed, Penitential Verses, Prayer of Manasseh, Great Canon of St. Andrew, and "Lord of hosts"). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p707 (printed page 692) and facing English leaf p708 (printed page 693). Great Compline Structure: Paragraph 23 details the order of Great Compline: The Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest exclaims: "For Thine is the kingdom...", and the Reader recites "Lord, have mercy" 12 times, "Glory... Both now and ever...", "Come, let us worship" thrice, and begins the Six Psalms of Great Compline (Psalms 4, 6, 12, 24, 30, and 90 LXX). After Psalm 90, "Glory... Both now and ever... Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia, glory to Thee, O God" thrice, "Lord, have mercy" thrice, "Glory... Both now and ever...". Immediately the two Choirs chant the antiphonal verses of "God is with us: understand, O ye nations, and submit yourselves, for God is with us" (Isa. 8:9 LXX), the First Choir repeating the first verse in full at the end. The Reader recites the three evening Troparia in recitativo (chuma): "The day is past, I give Thee thanks, O Lord...", and the Second Choir chants: "The bodiless nature of the Cherubim glorifies Thee with unceasing hymns...", down to: "That escaping from the deceits of the evil one, we may sing the angelic hymn!". The Reader recites: "Holy, Holy, Holy, Lord God of Sabaoth, heaven and earth are full of Thy glory!", followed by the Nicene-Constantinopolitan Symbol of Faith: "I believe in one God...". The Second Choir chants the verses of supplication: "All-holy Lady, Theotokos, intercede for us sinners" (thrice), followed by the invocations of the Bodiless Powers, the Apostles, Martyrs, and Saints (twice), and concluding with: "O Lord, God, forgive us our sins" (thrice), "and have mercy on us". The Trisagion Prayers follow, after which the Reader recites the Prayer of Manasseh, King of Judah: "O Lord Almighty, God of our fathers Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob...". Following another Trisagion, the penitential Troparia are chanted in a subdued voice: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...". Then the Small Doxology ("Glory to God in the highest...") and the prayer "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this night without sin" (Kataxioson). The Great Penitential Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete is chanted: Heirmos "He is my helper and protector unto salvation" (Exod. 15:2 LXX), with the refrain before each troparion: "Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me". Footnote 17 notes that the Great Canon is chanted in four sections on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, and Thursday evenings of the First Week of Great Lent; on all subsequent weekdays of Great Lent until Thursday evening of the Sixth Week, Canons to the Theotokos from the Theotokarion are chanted in its place. After Ode 6 of the Canon, the Kontakion "My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou?" is chanted with melody; after Ode 9, the Heirmos "Without seed of man, by the Holy Spirit..." is chanted, followed by the Trisagion Prayers. Then the hymn "O Lord of hosts, be with us, for we have no other helper in distress but Thee; O Lord of hosts, have mercy on us!" is chanted antiphonally with its six scriptural verses (Ps. 150 LXX), the first choir repeating the refrain after "Praise God in His Saints" and the second choir after "Praise Him in the firmament of His power".
[^414]: Great Lent: Great Compline Conclusion, Gospel, Mutual Forgiveness, and General Rule (§ 23 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p709 (printed page 694) and facing English leaf p710 (printed page 695). Great Compline Conclusion: Paragraph 23 concludes the order of Great Compline: Following the verses of "O Lord of hosts", the Reader recites: "Glory...": "O Lord, if we had not Thy Saints as intercessors...", "Both now and ever...": "Great is the multitude of mine offenses, O Theotokos...". The First Choir chants: "All-holy Theotokos, forsake me not during the days of my life...", and the Second Choir chants: "All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God; keep me under thy protection". The Reader recites "Lord, have mercy" 40 times, the prayer "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", "Lord, have mercy" thrice, "Glory... Both now and ever...", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, bless, father". The Priest exclaims: "May God be merciful unto us, and bless us, and cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us" (Ps. 66:2 LXX). Three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephraim the Syrian, and "Lord, have mercy" 12 times are performed. The two Canonarchs alternate the concluding prayers: the right Canonarch intones the Prayer of Paul of Evergetis: "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, all-chaste, pure Lady, Bride of God..."; the left Canonarch intones the Prayer of Antiochus the Monk: "And grant unto us, O Master, as we depart to sleep, repose of body and soul..."; followed by the antiphonal invocations: "Most glorious ever-virgin Mary...", "The Father is my hope...", and "All my hope I place in thee...". Then the Priest exclaims: "And that we may be counted worthy to listen to the Holy Gospel, let us pray to the Lord our God", and reads the appointed Gospel pericope of the First Week. The Bishop (or Priest) says: "Peace be unto all", the Priest exclaims: "Let us bow our heads unto the Lord", and the Hierarch (or Priest) recites the prayer of bowing heads: "O Master abundant in mercy, Lord Jesus Christ our God...". The Priest intones the litany of supplication: "Let us pray for the peace of the world...", and all the brethren and faithful receive mutual forgiveness from the Hierarch or Superior [Proestos], kissing his right hand. While this veneration is taking place, the Second Choir chants the venerable Theotokion: "Thou protectest all, O good one, who with faith flee unto thy strong hand; for we sinners have no other advocate before God in dangers and afflictions, being weighed down with many sins: O Mother of God Most High, therefore we bow before thee: deliver thy servants from every distress!" (Panton prostateueis, agathe). Footnote 18 records that on Tuesday and Thursday evenings, the Stavrotheotokion "When the Virgin beheld Thine unjust slaughter, O Christ..." (Sphagen sou ten adikon, Christe) is chanted in its place. The service concludes with the final exclamation: "Through the prayers of our holy Fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us." Concluding Statutory Rubric: George Violakis establishes the general rule that this exact form and order of Great Compline is to be observed daily throughout all of Great Lent until Great and Holy Tuesday evening, with the sole exception that the Gospel readings are appointed strictly and exclusively for the First Week. On Great and Holy Monday and Great and Holy Tuesday evenings, instead of the Canon of the Theotokos from the Theotokarion, the proper Triodia of Compline appointed in the Triodion are chanted, concluding with the hymn "When the Virgin beheld Thine unjust slaughter, O Christ."
[^415]: Great Lent: Wednesday and Friday of First Week, Presanctified Liturgy, and First Stasis of Akathist Hymn (§§ 24, 25, 26 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p711 (printed page 696) and facing English leaf p712 (printed page 697). Wednesday of the First Week of Great Lent: Paragraph 24 codifies the order of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on the first Wednesday of the Great Fast: At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress...", Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six Stichera are chanted: the Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion ("Fasting, O brethren, bodily, let us also fast spiritually; let us loose every bond of iniquity...", Nēsteusantes adelphoi sōmatikōs) twice, the Martyrikon of the occurring Tone once, three Prosomia of the sequence from the Triodion, and four Prosomia from the Menaion for the Saint of the following day; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion from the Menaion. The Entrance is made with the censer, followed by "O Gladsome Light" and the two appointed Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 1:24–2:3 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 2:1–22 LXX). Immediately following the readings, "Let my prayer arise in Thy sight as incense" (Ps. 140:2 LXX) is chanted with its verses, followed by the Great Litany of Fervent Supplication, the Litanies of the Catechumens and for Those Preparing for Holy Illumination, and the prayer of the Presanctified Gifts. At the solemn Entrance of the Presanctified Gifts, instead of the Cherubic Hymn, the ancient hymn "Now the powers of heaven do invisibly minister with us..." (Nyn hai dynameis) is chanted, followed by the Litany of Completion, the Lord's Prayer ("Our Father..."), and the elevation: "The Presanctified Holy Things for the holy!" The Communion Hymn [Koinonikon] is chanted: "Taste and see that the Lord is good. Alleluia" (Ps. 33:9 LXX). The post-communion hymns follow: "I will bless the Lord at all times", "Blessed be the name of the Lord" thrice, the Prayer behind the Ambo, and the Dismissal, during which Psalm 33 LXX ("I will bless the Lord at all times") and Psalm 144 LXX ("I will extol Thee, my God, my King") are read, concluding with "Through the prayers of our holy Fathers...". Friday of the First Week of Great Lent: Paragraph 25 codifies the order of the Presanctified Liturgy on Friday of the First Week, which serves as the festal eve of the commemoration of the Great Martyr Theodore the Tyro (the miracle of the Kolyva): At Vespers of the Presanctified, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Eighteenth Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried", ten Stichera are chanted: the Idiomelon of the day ("Come, O faithful, let us work the works of God in the light...", Deute pistoi epergasōmetha) twice, the four Martyrika of the Tone from the end of the Triodion, and four Stichera for Saint Theodore; "Glory...": Doxastikon of Saint Theodore ("Having used the apostate ruler as an instrument...", Organō chrēsamenos); "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the occurring Tone of the week. Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 2:20–3:20 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 3:19–34 LXX), "Let my prayer arise", and the remainder of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts in regular sequence. Friday Evening Small Compline and the First Stasis of the Akathist: Paragraph 26 opens the venerable Constantinopolitan rubrical order for Friday evening of the First Week: In the evening, Small Compline (To mikron Apodeipnon) is served; after the hymn "It is truly meet" (Axion estin hōs alēthōs), the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos ("I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit...", Anoixō to stoma mou) is chanted with six Troparia in each Ode. At the conclusion of Ode 9 of the Canon, the festal Kontakion of the Akathist: "To Thee, the Champion Leader, do we, thy servants, dedicate a hymn of victory..." (Tē hypermachō stratēgō ta nikētēria) is chanted slowly (argōs) in Tone 8, during which the First Stasis of the Oikoi of the Most Holy Theotokos (Oikoi 1–6: "An archangel was sent from heaven...") is solemnly recited by the presiding Hierarch or Priest before the icon of the Mother of God. At the conclusion of the First Stasis, "To Thee, the Champion Leader" is chanted again at a rapid tempo (syntomon). Footnote 19 records the patriarchal ceremonial protocol: While "To Thee, the Champion Leader" is being sung, the Ecumenical Patriarch (or presiding Hierarch, if present) descends to venerate the holy icon of the Theotokos, and then blesses the two Choirs while they chant "For many years, Master" (Eis polla etē, Despota). The Patriarch stands not upon the patriarchal cathedra (throne), but rather at the Parathronion (the small side throne adjacent to the solea), vested without the patriarchal mandyas (mantle), holding merely the chazranion (the ebony pastoral walking stick). Following this ceremony, the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers and begins the Kontakion of Saint Theodore the Tyro: "Having received the faith of Christ into thy heart as a breastplate...".
[^416]: Great Lent: Akathist Conclusion, Friday Evening Dismissal Protocol, and Saturday of the First Week of Great Lent (Commemoration of the Great Martyr Theodore the Tyro and the Miracle of the Kolyva) (§§ 26 Concluded, 27 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p713 (printed page 698) and facing English leaf p714 (printed page 699). Akathist Conclusion and Gospel Pericope: Paragraph 26 concludes the Friday evening Akathist service: Following the chanting of the Kontakion of the Great Martyr Theodore ("Having received the faith of Christ into thy heart as a breastplate, and trampled down the hostile powers, O much-suffering champion, thou hast been crowned with an eternal heavenly crown", Pistin Christou hōsei thōraka), the Reader recites "Lord, have mercy" 40 times, the prayer "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", and immediately, omitting the penitential prostrations, the Canonarchs recite the evening prayers: "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, all-chaste, pure Lady..." and "And grant unto us, O Master, as we depart to sleep...". The Priest exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy to listen to the Holy Gospel, let us pray to the Lord our God", and reads the appointed Gospel pericope. Immediately following the reading of the Gospel, the Priest pronounces the Dismissal: "Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee", and the Patriarch or Hierarch blesses the congregation while the Choirs chant "Our Master and Hierarch, O Lord, preserve for many years!" (Ton Despotēn kai Archierea). The Priest recites the litany of supplication: "Let us pray for the peace of the world...", at the conclusion of which the Second Choir chants the venerable Troparion: "Gabriel was rapt in amazement as he beheld the beauty of thy purity and the exceeding radiance of thy virginity: what worthy praise can I offer unto thee? By what name shall I call thee? I am lost in wonder and dismay: wherefore as commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!" (Tēn hōraiotēta tēs parthenias sou). The service concludes with the final exclamation: "Through the prayers of our holy Fathers, O Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us." Footnote 20 establishes the general rule for the remaining Fridays of Great Lent: On all subsequent Friday evenings throughout Great Lent (unless a Saint of Polyeleos or Doxology rank falls on the following Saturday), after the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion for the Dead is recited: "As first-fruits of our nature, O Lord, the world offers unto Thee the God-bearing Martyrs..." (Hōs aparchas tēs physeōs). Footnote 21 codifies the liturgical calendar rule: This exact rubrical order of Friday Small Compline with the Stasis of the Akathist is observed throughout the first five weeks of Great Lent up to Friday evening of the Fifth Week; however, the reading of the Gospel pericope is appointed strictly and solely for the First Friday. Furthermore, in ecclesiastical jurisdictions where the Akathist Hymn is customarily chanted at Matins on Saturday mornings, Friday Small Compline is read together with the Canon of the Tone for the Departed (Nekrosimon), except on the First Friday of Lent, on which, in place of the Canon for the Departed, the proper Canon of Saint Theodore the Tyro from the Triodion is chanted: "Thee, Theodore, lover of God, with hymns do I praise..." (Theou se, Theodore erastēn). Saturday of the First Week (Saint Theodore the Tyro and the Miracle of the Kolyva): Paragraph 27 codifies the festal order for the first Saturday of Great Lent, celebrating the miraculous deliverance of the Christians of Constantinople from the defilement of Julian the Apostate through the intervention of Saint Theodore and the distribution of boiled wheat (kolyva): At Matins, following the Midnight Office, the Six Psalms are recited. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparion of the Great Martyr Theodore is chanted twice: "Great are the achievements of faith! In the fountain of flame, the holy Martyr Theodore rejoiced as if by streams of restful waters...", and the Theotokion once. The appointed Kathismata of the Psalter follow, with the Martyrika Kathismata of the occurring Tone; after the second Stichologia, the Kathismata of Saint Theodore are chanted; Psalm 50 is read in recitativo (chuma); the Canon of the Saint of the day from the Menaion is combined with the two Canons of Saint Theodore from the Triodion; after Ode 3, the Kathismata of Saint Theodore and the Theotokion; after Ode 6, the Kontakion and Oikos of Saint Theodore, the Menologion of the Menaion, and the Synaxarion / Memorial (Hypomnema) of the Triodion recounting the miracle of the Kolyva; the Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth"; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Exaposteilarion of Saint Theodore and the Theotokion; at the Praises (Lauds), the Stichera are chanted; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint ("The gift of holiness and divine wealth of life hast thou shown thyself to the world, O Theodore...", Hagiōsynēs dōrea); "Both now and ever...": "O Theotokos, thou art the true vine..."; the Great Doxology, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Theodore, and immediately the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated. At the Liturgy, the Typika and Beatitudes (Makarismoi) are sung, with Troparia taken from Odes 3 and 6 of the First Canon of Saint Theodore. After the Small Entrance, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Theodore, the Troparion of the Temple Patron Saint, and the Kontakion of the Theotokos: "O Protection of Christians that cannot be put to shame..." (Prostasia tōn Christianōn). The Epistle [Apostolos] of the Saint is read (2 Timothy 2:1–10: "My son Timothy, be strong in the grace that is in Christ Jesus..."), and the Gospel of the day (Mark 2:23–3:5 LXX: "At that time, Jesus went through the grainfields on the Sabbath..."). At the Anaphora, "Especially for our all-holy, immaculate, most blessed and glorious Lady, Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary", the Megalynarion "It is truly meet" (Axion estin) is chanted; the Communion Hymn [Koinonikon]: "The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance. Alleluia" (Ps. 111:6 LXX); "We have seen the true Light"...
[^417]: Great Lent: Blessing of Kolyva on First Saturday and Sunday of Orthodoxy Opening (Great Vespers and Festal Matins) (§§ 27 Concluded, 28, 29, 30 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p715 (printed page 700) and facing English leaf p716 (printed page 701). First Saturday Conclusion and Blessing of Kolyva: Paragraph 27 concludes the Divine Liturgy of the First Saturday: Following the post-communion hymns "We have seen the true Light" and "Blessed be the name of the Lord, henceforth and forever" (thrice), the solemn blessing of the memorial boiled wheat (kolyva) prepared in honor of the Great Martyr Theodore the Tyro takes place in the center of the church, accompanied by the chanting of the Troparion and Kontakion of Saint Theodore, after which the final Dismissal of the Liturgy is pronounced. Sunday of Orthodoxy (First Sunday of Great Lent, Feast of the Restoration of the Holy Icons): Paragraph 28 codifies the order of Great Vespers on Saturday evening: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera are chanted: six Resurrection Stichera of the Tone from the Octoechos and four Stichera from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Triodion ("The grace of truth has shone forth, and that which was shadowed forth of old is now openly accomplished: for behold, the Church is adorned with the icons of Christ, of His Mother, and of all the Saints...", Hē Charis epelampse); "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the occurring Tone from the Octoechos. At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichera of the Octoechos; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon of the Triodion ("Advancing from impiety unto true worship, and enlightened with the light of knowledge, let us clap our hands and offer praise and thanksgiving unto God...", Hoi ex aseveias eis euseveian provantes); the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion of the Tone, the Dismissal Troparion of the Holy Icons: "Thy pure icon do we venerate, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our offenses, O Christ God; for of Thine own good will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver from the bondage of the enemy those whom Thou hadst created. Wherefore we cry aloud unto Thee with thanksgiving: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who didst come to save the world!" (Tēn achranton eikona sou proskynoumen, Agathe); the festal Theotokion: "All beyond comprehension, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos: sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast acknowledged to be the true Mother who gavest birth unto the unlying God: Him do thou entreat that our souls may be saved" (Panta hyper ennoian); and the Dismissal. Footnote 22 records the patriarchal panikhida rubric: On this Saturday evening in the Patriarchal Church of Saint George at the Phanar (and in cathedral churches), trays of kolyva are placed before the solea; immediately following the Dismissal Troparia of Vespers, the solemn memorial Troparia for the Dead are chanted: "With the spirits of the righteous made perfect, give rest to the souls of Thy servants, O Savior..." (Meta pneumatōn dikaiōn). The Ecumenical Patriarch (or presiding Hierarch) solemnly commemorates by name all Orthodox Christian Emperors, Kings, Patriarchs, Hierarchs, clergy, and all pious Orthodox Christians from one end of the inhabited world unto the other who have fallen asleep in true faith throughout the centuries. Sunday of Orthodoxy Matins: Paragraph 29 details the festal order of Matins: Following Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon of the Tone is chanted; "Glory..." of the Litiya of the Triodion, "Both now and ever...": Theotokion; the Triadica "It is truly meet to glorify Thee, the Word of God...", the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion "Thy pure icon do we venerate...", and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparia are chanted in the same sequence as at Great Vespers; the appointed Kathisma of the Psalter and the Polyeleos (Psalm 134–135 LXX) or Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX); the Kathismata of the Octoechos, the Evlogitaria of the Resurrection ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Hypakoe, the Anavathmoi (Hymns of Ascent) of the occurring Tone, and the Resurrection Prokimenon. The Canons: the Resurrection Canon of the Octoechos and the Canon of the Triodion composed by Saint Theophanes the Branded, with the biblical verse before each troparion: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee". After Ode 3, the Resurrection Kontakion and Oikos, followed by the Kathisma of the Triodion and the Theotokion; after Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Triodion: "The uncircumscribed Word of the Father became circumscribed, taking flesh from thee, O Theotokos; and having restored the defiled image to its ancient dignity, He united it with divine beauty. Confessing this salvation, we depict it in word and deed!" and the Oikos of the Triodion, the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Synaxarion / Memorial (Hypomnema) of the Sunday of Orthodoxy, recounting the Seventh Ecumenical Council (Nicaea II, 787) and the final restoration of the Holy Icons under Empress Theodora and Patriarch Methodius in 843. The Katavasiai: "Israel in ancient times, walking across the Red Sea deep with dryshod feet, defeated the power of Amalek in the wilderness through the cross-wise outstretched hands of Moses" (Thalassēs to Erythraion pelagos); then the complete solemn order of the Resurrection Matins Gospel appointed for Sundays of the Triodion; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Exaposteilarion of the Resurrection, the Exaposteilarion of the Triodion, and the Theotokion. At the Praises (Lauds), eight Stichera: four Resurrection Stichera from the Octoechos and four Stichera from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Triodion ("Moses, during the season of abstinence, received the Law and drew the people unto him; Elijah, having fasted, shut the heavens; and the three youths of Abraham, through fasting, vanquished the lawless tyrant...", Mōsēs tō kairō tēs enkrateias); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."; the Great Doxology, and the Resurrection Troparion: "Today salvation is come unto the world: let us sing unto Him Who rose from the tomb, the Author of our life; for having destroyed death by death, He has given us victory and great mercy!" (Sēmeron sōtēria). Paragraph 30 opens the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great with its festal antiphonal structure.
[^418]: Great Lent: Sunday of Orthodoxy Divine Liturgy Festal Antiphons 1 and 2 (§ 30 Antiphons 1 & 2). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p717 (printed page 702) and facing English leaf p718 (printed page 703). Festal Antiphons of Orthodoxy: Paragraph 30 codifies the special festal Antiphons of the Divine Liturgy appointed for the Sunday of Orthodoxy, replacing the ordinary Sunday Typika and Beatitudes (Makarismoi) with psalm verses prophetically celebrating the divine kingship, redemption, and victory of Christ and the Church over heresy: First Antiphon: Chanted antiphonally by the two Choirs with the invariable refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us" (Tais presveiais tēs Theotokou, Sōtēr, sōson hēmas). Verse 1: "The Lord reigns; He is robed in majesty! The Lord is robed, He is girded with strength" (Ps. 92:1 LXX [Ps. 93:1]). Verse 2: "For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken" (Ps. 92:1b–2 LXX [Ps. 93:2]). Verse 3: "Who shall declare the mighty acts of the Lord, or cause all His praises to be heard?" (Ps. 105:2 LXX [Ps. 106:2]). Verse 4: "Let the redeemed of the Lord say so, whom He has redeemed from the hand of the enemy" (Ps. 106:2 LXX [Ps. 107:2]). Concluding Doxology: "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", followed by the final repeat of the Refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us." Second Antiphon: Chanted antiphonally with the festal refrain: "Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" (Sōson hēmas, Yie Theou, ho anastas ek nekrōn, psallontas soi, Allēlouia!). Verse 1: "Let them give thanks unto the Lord for His mercies, and for His wonderful works to the sons of men!" (Ps. 106:31 LXX [Ps. 107:31]). Verse 2: "Let them exalt Him in the assembly of the people, and praise Him in the seat of the elders" (Ps. 106:32 LXX [Ps. 107:32]). Verse 3: "Behold, the eyes of the Lord are upon them that fear Him, upon them that hope in His mercy" (Ps. 32:18 LXX [Ps. 33:18]). Verse 4: "To hear the groaning of the fettered, to loose the sons of the slain" (Ps. 101:21 LXX [Ps. 102:20]). Concluding Doxology: "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", followed by the theological hymn composed by Emperor Justinian: "Only-begotten Son and immortal Word of God, Who for our salvation didst will to be incarnate of the holy Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary, Who without change didst become man and wast crucified, O Christ God, trampling down death by death: Thou Who art one of the Holy Trinity, glorified together with the Father and the Holy Spirit, save us!" (Ho Monogenēs Yios).
[^419]: Great Lent: Sunday of Orthodoxy Antiphon 3, Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil Conclusion, Solemn Panikhida, and First Lenten Sunday Evening Vespers (§§ 30 Concluded, 31). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p719 (printed page 704) and facing English leaf p720 (printed page 705). Third Antiphon: Paragraph 30 continues with the Third Festal Antiphon of the Sunday of Orthodoxy, chanted with the Dismissal Troparion of the Holy Icons as its refrain: "Thy pure icon do we venerate, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our offenses, O Christ God; for of Thine own good will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver from the bondage of the enemy those whom Thou hadst created. Wherefore we cry aloud unto Thee with thanksgiving: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who didst come to save the world!" (Tēn achranton eikona sou proskynoumen, Agathe). Verse 1: "Let the heavens and the earth praise Him, the seas, and everything that creepeth in them!" (Ps. 68:35 LXX [Ps. 69:34]). Verse 2: "This is the day which the Lord has made; let us rejoice and be glad in it!" (Ps. 117:24 LXX [Ps. 118:24]). Verse 3: "O Lord my God, I will give thanks unto Thee forever!" (Ps. 29:13 LXX [Ps. 30:12]). Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great Conclusion: Following the Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, the Entrance Hymn is chanted: "Come, let us worship and fall down before Christ: O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, save us who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" Then the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion of the Tone, the Dismissal Troparion of the Holy Icons ("Thy pure icon do we venerate..."), the Dismissal Troparion of the Temple Patron Saint, and the Kontakion of the Akathist: "To Thee, the Champion Leader..." (Tē hypermachō). The Epistle [Apostolos] of the Sunday of Orthodoxy (Hebrews 11:24–26, 32–40: "Brethren, by faith Moses, when he was come to years, refused to be called the son of Pharaoh's daughter...") and the Gospel pericope (John 1:43–51: "At that time, Jesus determined to go into Galilee, and finding Philip He said unto him: Follow Me..."). The Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great proceeds in solemn order. Communion Hymn [Koinonikon]: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest. Alleluia" (Ps. 148:1 LXX); the post-communion hymn "We have seen the true Light", the Prayer behind the Ambo (opisthambonos euchē) recited by the Priest, and the Dismissal. Footnote 23 explains the textual history of the Prayer behind the Ambo: On all Sundays of Great Lent during the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great, the customary Prayer behind the Ambo from the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom ("O Lord Who blessest them that bless Thee and sanctifiest them that trust in Thee...", Ho eulogōn tous eulogountas se) is recited, because the ancient liturgical scrolls (eilitaria) do not preserve an authentic Prayer behind the Ambo for Saint Basil. On January 1 (the Feast of Saint Basil), however, as a celebrated exception, the prayer attributed to Saint Basil—the Uranophantor ("Revealer of Heavenly Mysteries", Ouranophantor)—is read on Mount Athos (and has gradually spread into broader liturgical usage). Solemn Patriarchal Memorial Service (Panikhida): Following the Dismissal of the Liturgy, the Ecumenical Patriarch exits the Sanctuary and ascends the throne in the nave; the concelebrating Metropolitans, Archbishops, and Priests take their corresponding seats around the solea. The Great Archdeacon proclaims: "Bless, Master!" (Eulogēson, Despota), and the Patriarch intones the opening doxology: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." The Archdeacon recites the Trisagion Prayers, the Patriarch pronounces the Exclamation: "For Thine is the kingdom...", and, along with the concelebrating Hierarchs, chants the funeral Troparia: "With the spirits of the righteous made perfect, give rest to the souls of Thy servants, O Savior...". The Patriarch solemnly commemorates all Orthodox Christians throughout the ages who have fallen asleep in the Lord—Emperors, Kings, Patriarchs, Hierarchs, clergy, and all departed Orthodox believers—and the concelebrating Hierarchs and Priests join in the commemorations. The Choir chants "Our Master and Hierarch", and the final Memorial Dismissal is pronounced. First Lenten Sunday Evening Vespers: Paragraph 31 codifies the first of the solemn Penitential Vespers services of Great Lent, celebrated on the evening of the Sunday of Orthodoxy: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera are chanted: four Penitential Stichera (Katanyktika) of the occurring Tone from the Octoechos, three Prosomia of the sequence from the Triodion, and three Prosomia from the Menaion; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion from the Menaion. Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", and the Great Prokimenon: "Thou hast given an inheritance to them that fear Thy name, O Lord" (Edōkas klēronomian tois phovoumenois to onoma sou, Kyrie, Ps. 60:6 LXX) with its four appointed verses. At the Aposticha, the Idiomelon of the Triodion: "Come, let us cleanse ourselves in the Spirit, casting away all filthiness of flesh and spirit, that we may celebrate the pure Fast..." (Deute ekkatharōmen) twice, and the Martyrikon of the Tone; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion ("The heavenly hosts praise thee, O full of grace..."). The Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers; after the priestly Exclamation: "For Thine is the kingdom...", the homily or catechetical instruction from the Ambo is delivered; and following the homily, the festive Lenten Troparia: "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos, Mary full of grace..." are chanted with great prostrations, followed by the Prayer of Saint Ephraim the Syrian with three great prostrations, the concluding prayers, and the Dismissal, exactly as set forth in Paragraph 17 (Cohort 70).
[^420]: Great Lent: Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday of the Second Week (§§ 32, 33, 34 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p721 (printed page 706) and facing English leaf p722 (printed page 707). Wednesday of the Second Week: Paragraph 32 codifies the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on the second Wednesday of Great Lent: At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress...", Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six Stichera are chanted: the Idiomelon of the day from the Triodion ("Fasting spiritually, O brethren, let us also fast bodily; let us loose every bond of iniquity...", Tēn pneumatikēn adelphoi) twice, the Martyrikon of the occurring Tone once, three Prosomia of the sequence from the Triodion, and four Prosomia from the Menaion for the Saint of the following day; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion from the Menaion. Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", and the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 4:16–26 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 5:15–6:3 LXX). Then "Let my prayer arise in Thy sight as incense" (Ps. 140:2 LXX), and the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts in regular order. Friday of the Second Week: Paragraph 33 codifies the order for Friday of the Second Week: At Vespers of the Presanctified, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried", ten Stichera are chanted: the Idiomelon of the day ("Now is the acceptable time, now is the day of salvation...", Nyn kairos euprosdektos) twice, the four Martyrika of the occurring Tone, and four Stichera from the Menaion; "Glory...": the Doxastikon of the Departed [Nekrosimon] of the occurring Tone; "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone of the week. Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 5:32–6:8 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 6:20–7:1 LXX), "Let my prayer arise", and the rest of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. Friday Evening Small Compline and Second Stasis of the Akathist: On Friday evening, Small Compline (To mikron Apodeipnon) is served; after the hymn "It is truly meet", the Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos ("I shall open my mouth...", Anoixō to stoma mou) is chanted with six Troparia in each Ode. At the conclusion of Ode 9 of the Canon, the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" is chanted slowly in Tone 8, during which the Second Stasis of the Oikoi of the Most Holy Theotokos (Oikoi 7–12: "The shepherds heard the angels hymning the incarnate presence of Christ...") is solemnly recited by the presiding Hierarch or Priest before the icon of the Mother of God, concluding as set forth in Paragraph 26. Saturday of the Second Week (Soul Saturday): Paragraph 34 opens the memorial order for the second Saturday of Great Lent: At Matins, following the Midnight Office, the Six Psalms are recited. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparia "Apostles, Martyrs, and Prophets..." (Apostoloi Martyres) are chanted, "Glory...": "Remember, O Lord, as Thou art good, Thy servants..." (Mnēsthēti Kyrie), "Both now and ever...": "Holy Mother of the ineffable Light..." (Mētēr hagia). The Sixteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("The Lord said unto my Lord...", Pss. 109–117 LXX); then the Martyrika Kathismata with the Kathisma of the Departed and the Theotokion of the Tone of the week. Then the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX, the Seventeenth Kathisma) in two Stases, followed by the Evlogitaria of the Departed ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes... The choir of the saints have found the fountain of life..."), during which the Priest solemnly commemorates all departed pious and Orthodox Christians, concluding with the Troparia "Give rest, our Savior, with the righteous unto Thy servants..." (Anapauson Sōtēr hēmōn) and "Both now and ever...": "Thou Who dawned upon the world from a Virgin, Christ God..." (Ho ek Parthenou anateilas). Psalm 50 is read; the Canon of the Saint from the Menaion alone until Ode 5; from Ode 6, the Triodia Canons begin until Ode 9, preceded by the Canon of the Saint. Heirmos of Ode 8: "The One beginningless from the Father..." (Ton ex anarchou tou Patros), followed by the Biblical Canticle verses with "More honorable than the Cherubim"; Heirmos of Ode 9: "Thee, the immortal fountain..." (Se tēn athanaton pēgēn); the Exaposteilarion of the Departed: "Thou Who rulest the dead and the living..." (Ho kai nekrōn kai zōntōn), and "In thee we boast, O Theotokos..." (Hēmeis en soi kauchōmetha, Theotoke). At the Praises (Lauds), the Martyrika of the occurring Tone of the week are chanted. Footnote Note [*]: Violakis records an important textual discordance in footnote [*]: In the General Introduction (Protheoria), § 19, Violakis specifies that at Matins on Lenten Saturdays, unless a celebrated Saint occurs, "Alleluia" is chanted instead of "God is the Lord", sung twice without verses and twice with the two memorial verses: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord" (Ps. 64:5 LXX) and "And their memorial is from generation to generation" (Ps. 101:13 LXX). The liturgical book Triodion prescribes this exact same order for the Saturday of the Second Week. In § 34, however, likely through an editorial oversight, Violakis reverts to the common order of "God is the Lord", which is typical of festal Matins. (See the commentary in Diptycha 1994, p. 71, and in the Imerologion of the Ecumenical Patriarchate 2010, p. 128, n. 36).
[^421]: Great Lent: Second Saturday Concluded, General Lenten Saturday Protocol, and Second Sunday of Great Lent (Saint Gregory Palamas) Opening (§§ 34 Concluded, 35, 36 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p723 (printed page 708) and facing English leaf p724 (printed page 709). Second Saturday Concluded and Divine Liturgy: Paragraph 34 concludes the Soul Saturday order: At the Praises, after the Martyrika, "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Departed, "Both now and ever...": Theotokion, and "To Thee belongs glory" (Soi doxa prepei). At the Aposticha of the Lauds, the Stichera of the Tone, "Glory... Both now and ever..." from the Triodion, "It is good to confess unto the Lord" (Ps. 91:2 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion "Apostles, Martyrs...", and the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom begins, at which the Antiphons of the Liturgy are the verses of "It is good to confess unto the Lord". After the Little Entrance: the Dismissal Troparion "Apostles, Martyrs...", the Troparion of the Temple Patron Saint, and the Kontakion: "As first-fruits of our nature, O Lord, the world offers unto Thee the God-bearing Martyrs..." (Hōs aparchas tēs physeōs). At "Especially": "It is truly meet" (Axion estin); the Communion Hymn [Koinonikon]: "Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord. Alleluia" (Ps. 64:5 LXX); "We have seen the true Light"; then the Priest again solemnly commemorates by name all the departed pious and Orthodox Christians, and the Dismissal is given. Universal Rule for Lenten Saturdays: Violakis establishes the general rubrical rule: "This exact order is to be observed on every Saturday until the Fourth Week of Lent." Thus, the Saturdays of the Second, Third, and Fourth Weeks of Great Lent share this identical memorial structure for the faithful departed. Second Sunday of Great Lent (Commemoration of Saint Gregory Palamas): Paragraph 35 codifies the order for Great Vespers on Saturday evening: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera are chanted: six Resurrection Stichera from the Octoechos and four Stichera for Saint Gregory Palamas from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon of Saint Gregory ("O thrice-blessed saint, holy Father, good shepherd and disciple of Christ the Chief Shepherd...", Trismakar hagie pater); "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the occurring Tone from the Octoechos. At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichera of the Tone; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint ("Thy tongue, awake with watchful care, sounding in the ears of our hearts, stirs our sluggish souls...", Hē grygoros sou glōssa); "Both now and ever...": "O unwedded Virgin, who didst conceive God ineffably in the flesh..." (Anympheute Parthene). The Resurrection Dismissal Troparion of the Tone, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Gregory Palamas: "O light of Orthodoxy, teacher and pillar of the Church, boast of monastics and invincible champion of theologians, Gregory the wonderworker, glory of Thessalonica and preacher of grace: pray unceasingly that our souls may be saved!" (Phōstēr tēs Orthodoxias), "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion; and the Dismissal. Second Sunday Matins Opening: Paragraph 36 details the order of Matins: After Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon of the Tone is chanted, followed by the Triadica "It is truly meet to glorify Thee, the Word of God...", the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Gregory, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparia are chanted in the same sequence as at Vespers; the appointed Kathismata of the Psalter and the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX); the Resurrection Kathismata, the Evlogitaria of the Resurrection ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes"), the Hypakoe, the Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of the occurring Tone, and the Resurrection Prokimenon. The Canons: the Resurrection Canon of the Octoechos and the Canon of Saint Gregory Palamas from the Triodion. After Ode 3, the Kontakion of Saint Gregory ("An organ of divine wisdom, a clear trumpet of theology, with one accord we hymn thee, O Gregory...", Tēs sophias to organon), followed by his Kathisma and its Theotokion; after Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Oikos of the Tone, the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Memorial/Synaxarion [Hypomnema] of the Triodion recounting the life, holy teachings, and defense of Orthodox hesychasm and the uncreated divine energies by Saint Gregory Palamas, Archbishop of Thessalonica. The Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth"; the complete solemn order of the Resurrection Matins Gospel appointed for Sundays of the Triodion; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Resurrection Exaposteilarion, and...
[^422]: Great Lent: Second Sunday Concluded, Weekly Cycle, and Third Sunday of Great Lent (Veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross) Opening (§§ 36 Concluded, 37, 38, 39 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p725 (printed page 710) and facing English leaf p726 (printed page 711). Second Sunday Matins Concluded and Divine Liturgy: Paragraph 36 concludes the Matins service for the Second Sunday: At the Praises (Lauds), eight Stichera are chanted: four Resurrection Stichera from the Octoechos and four Stichera for Saint Gregory Palamas from the Triodion. Footnote 24 specifies the psalm verses chanted before the final two Stichera of Saint Gregory: 1) "My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart shall be understanding" (Ps. 48:4 LXX [Ps. 49:3 MT]), and 2) "Let Thy priests, O Lord, be clothed with righteousness, and let Thy saints shout with joy" (Ps. 131:9 LXX [Ps. 132:9 MT]). "Glory...": Doxastikon of Saint Gregory: "To those who sit in the darkness of sin, thou hast shone forth like a sun, O Father Gregory..." (Tois en skotei hamartēmatōn); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."; the Great Doxology, and the Resurrection Troparion: "Today salvation is come unto the world" (Sēmeron sōtēria). Paragraph 37 codifies the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great: At the Liturgy, the Typika and the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] of the Tone for four, and four Troparia from Ode 6 of the Canon of Saint Gregory from the Triodion. After the Little Entrance: the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Gregory, that of the Temple Patron Saint, and the Kontakion: "To Thee, the Champion Leader..." (Tē hypermachō). The Epistle [Apostolos] of the Sunday (Hebrews 1:10–2:3: "In the beginning, Thou, Lord, didst lay the foundation of the earth...") and the Gospel pericope (Mark 2:1–12: Healing of the Paralytic at Capernaum). Communion Hymn [Koinonikon]: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest. Alleluia" (Ps. 148:1 LXX); "We have seen the true Light", and the Dismissal. Mid-Week Rubrics: In the evening, the Penitential Vespers is celebrated according to the rubrical order of Paragraph 31. The services of Wednesday, Friday, and Saturday of the Third Week follow the exact order of Paragraphs 32, 33, and 34. Third Sunday of Great Lent (Feast of the Veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross): Paragraph 38 codifies Great Vespers on Saturday evening: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera: six Resurrection Stichera from the Octoechos and four Stichera of the Precious Cross from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Cross: "O Christ our God, Who didst willingly accept crucifixion for our sake..." (Christe ho Theos hēmōn); "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the occurring Tone. At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichera; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon of the Cross: "O Lord Who didst fight alongside most gentle David to subdue the alien, fight alongside our faithful rulers and with the weapon of the Cross cast down our enemies..." (Ho symmachēsas, Kyrie); the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion, "Glory...": Dismissal Troparion of the Cross: "Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victory to the faithful over their adversaries, and by Thy Cross preserving Thy commonwealth" (Sōson Kyrie ton laon sou); "Both now and ever...": Dismissal Theotokion: "When Gabriel uttered the greeting to thee, O Virgin: Rejoice!..." (Tou Gabriēl phthenxamenou); and the Dismissal. Third Sunday Matins Opening: Paragraph 39 details the festal order of Matins: Following Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon of the Tone is chanted; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Sticheron of the Liti: "All creation seeing Thee hanging naked upon the Wood..." (Horōsa se hē ktisis hapasa); the Triadica "It is truly meet to glorify Thee, the Word of God..."; the Trisagion Prayers; the Dismissal Troparion: "Save, O Lord, Thy people..."; and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion, "Glory...": Dismissal Troparion of the Cross, "Both now and ever...": "When Gabriel uttered the greeting...". The Psalter, the Polyeleos (Pss. 134–135 LXX), the Resurrection Kathismata in order, and after the Second Stichologia...
[^423]: Great Lent: Third Sunday of Lent (Matins of the Precious Cross, Katavasiai, Melismatic Asmatikon, and Solemn Procession to the Tetrapod) (§ 39 Continued). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p727 (printed page 712) and facing English leaf p728 (printed page 713). Matins of the Cross Continued: Paragraph 39 details the festal Matins: After the Second Stichologia of the Psalter, the Kathisma of the Cross is chanted: "In paradise of old the wood stripped me bare, for by tasting thereof the enemy brought in death; but the Wood of the Cross, bearing the garment of life unto men, has been planted on earth, and the whole world is filled with all manner of joy..." (En paradeisō men to prin) twice. Footnote 25 establishes that the Resurrection Evlogitaria are omitted because of the solemn festal character of the Cross. The Hypakoe, the Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of the occurring Tone, and the Resurrection Prokimenon follow. The Canon of the Triodion is chanted for eight: first the Heirmos: "The most divine Moses prefigured of old the Cross..." (Ho theiotatos proetypōsen); before the first Troparion of each Ode, the refrain is: "Glory to Thy holy Resurrection, O Lord" (Doxa tē hagia Anastasei sou); before the remaining Troparia: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee" (Doxa soi ho Theos hēmōn, doxa soi); before the last two: "Glory...", "Both now and ever...". After Ode 3, the Kathismata of the Triodion in order; after Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Cross: "No longer does the fiery sword guard the gate of Eden, for an awesome bond has come upon it: the Wood of the Cross! The sting of death and the victory of Hades are trodden down..." (Ouketi phloginē romphaia) and the Oikos, the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Memorial/Synaxarion [Hypomnema] of the Triodion recounting how the Precious and Life-giving Cross is elevated at the midpoint of the Great Fast to refresh, comfort, and encourage the faithful amidst their ascetic labors. The Katavasiai: "The most divine Moses prefigured of old..."; the complete order of the Resurrection Matins Gospel; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Resurrection Exaposteilarion, the Exaposteilarion of the Cross, and the Theotokion. Praises and Patriarchal Custom: At the Praises (Lauds), eight Stichera: four Resurrection Stichera and four Prosomia of the Cross; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Cross: "The elevated high-aspiring disposition of the wicked Pharisees, the Lord and King of all things has exposed..." (Tēn hypsēlophrona gnōmēn); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."; the Great Doxology. Footnote 26 notes that the Patriarch (or presiding Hierarch) does not venerate the Holy Icons at this point according to the usual Sunday custom, because he will shortly venerate the Precious Cross itself. Solemn Procession of the Precious Cross to the Tetrapod: At the conclusion of the Great Doxology, the Choirs chant the slow, melismatic Asmatikon: "Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us" (Hagios ho Theos). The Choirs descend from their stalls and process to the North Door of the Sanctuary singing the Asmatikon. The Priest, fully vested in all his priestly vestments, censes the Holy Table and the Precious Cross, which is placed on the Holy Table upon a tray (diskos) adorned with flowers and three lighted candles. Having completed the censing, he raises the tray upon his head and exits through the North Door of the Sanctuary, preceded by candle-bearers, the six-winged fans [hexapteryga], two censers, and the Primikerios holding the Divamvoulon. When the Priest reaches the center of the nave, where the Tetrapod stands already adorned, he circumambulates it three times; then, facing Eastward, he proclaims: "Wisdom! Let us attend!" (Sophia, orthoi), and places the tray with the Cross upon the Tetrapod. He censes around the Tetrapod in a cross-wise manner while chanting the Dismissal Troparion: "Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..." (Sōson Kyrie ton laon sou), which is repeated in turn by each of the two Choirs. The Patriarch then descends from the throne, and standing before the Tetrapod, venerates the Precious Cross while chanting: "Thy Cross we worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify!" (Ton Stavron sou proskynoumen, Despota, kai tēn hagian sou Anastasin doxazomen) once; each of the Choirs repeats this chant once. Then the Patriarch blesses the people while the Choirs chant: "For many years, Master!" (Eis polla etē, Despota), after which he ascends again unto the throne.
[^424]: Great Lent: Third Sunday of Lent (Veneration of the Precious Cross Concluded, Flower Distribution, and Divine Liturgy Festal Antiphons 1 and 2) (§§ 39 Concluded, 40). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p729 (printed page 714) and facing English leaf p730 (printed page 715). Veneration Protocol and Flower Distribution: Paragraph 39 concludes the solemn ceremonial of the Veneration of the Cross: Following the Patriarch, the Hierarchs approach two by two in order of seniority to venerate the Precious Cross and receive blessed flowers from the hands of the Patriarch, followed by the Offikion holders (clergy and patriarchal lay dignitaries). During the veneration, the Second Choir chants the venerable Idiomelon from the Triodion: "Come, O believers, let us worship the life-giving Wood, whereon Christ the King of glory, stretching out His hands, raised us up unto our ancient blessedness..." (Deute pistoi to zōopoiōn xylon proskynēsōmen). After the veneration, the Dismissal Troparion "Save, O Lord, Thy people..." is chanted, and the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great begins. Footnote 27 records the pastoral rubric for the congregation: One of the Priests carries the Precious Cross among the congregation so that all the faithful, both men and women, may venerate it and receive flowers from his hands. In churches where multiple priests concelebrate, this duty is assigned to an assistant priest; in single-priest parishes, the celebrating priest himself performs this ministry, while the Choirs continue singing the appointed Idiomela of the Cross from the Triodion until the entire congregation has venerated. Festal Antiphons of the Precious Cross: Paragraph 40 codifies the special festal Antiphons of the Divine Liturgy appointed for the Third Sunday of Lent, replacing the ordinary Sunday Typika and Beatitudes (Makarismoi) with psalm verses prophetically celebrating the Cross, redemption, and victory: First Antiphon: Chanted antiphonally by the two Choirs with the invariable refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us" (Tais presveiais tēs Theotokou, Sōtēr, sōson hēmas). Verse 1: "The light of Thy countenance was marked upon us, O Lord!" (Ps. 4:7 LXX [Ps. 4:6 MT]). Verse 2: "Thou hast given a sign unto them that fear Thee, that they may flee from before the bow" (Ps. 59:6 LXX [Ps. 60:4 MT]). Verse 3: "Thou didst ascend on high, Thou didst lead captivity captive; Thou didst receive gifts among men" (Ps. 67:19 LXX [Ps. 68:18 MT]). Verse 4: "Thou hast given an inheritance to them that fear Thy name, O Lord" (Ps. 60:6 LXX [Ps. 61:5 MT]). Concluding Doxology: "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", followed by the final repeat of the Refrain: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, Savior, save us." Second Antiphon: Chanted antiphonally with the festal refrain: "Save us, O Son of God, Who art risen from the dead, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" (Sōson hēmas, Yie Theou, ho anastas ek nekrōn, psallontas soi, Allēlouia!). Verse 1: "All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God" (Ps. 97:3 LXX [Ps. 98:3 MT]). Verse 2: "Let us worship at the place where His feet stood" (Ps. 131:7 LXX [Ps. 132:7 MT]). Verse 3: "For God is our King from before the ages; He has worked salvation in the midst of the earth" (Ps. 73:12 LXX [Ps. 74:12 MT]). Verse 4: "I will be exalted among the nations; I will be exalted in the earth" (Ps. 45:11 LXX [Ps. 46:10 MT]). Concluding Doxology: "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", followed by the theological hymn composed by Emperor Justinian: "Only-begotten Son and immortal Word of God, Who for our salvation didst will to be incarnate of the holy Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary, Who without change didst become man and wast crucified, O Christ God, trampling down death by death: Thou Who art one of the Holy Trinity, glorified together with the Father and the Holy Spirit, save us!" (Ho Monogenēs Yios).
[^425]: Great Lent: Third Sunday of Lent (Liturgy Antiphon 3 Concluded, Basil Liturgy, Mid-Week Rubrics of Fourth Week) and Fourth Sunday of Lent (Saint John of the Ladder) Opening (§§ 40 Concluded, 41, 42 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p731 (printed page 716) and facing English leaf p732 (printed page 717). Liturgy of the Third Sunday (Veneration of the Cross): Paragraph 40 concludes the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great on the Third Sunday of Lent: The Third Antiphon is chanted with the Dismissal Troparion of the Cross ("Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance...", Sōson Kyrie ton laon sou) as the festal refrain to Psalm verses 98:5, 27:9a, and 27:9b (LXX). After the Little Entrance, the Dismissal Troparia are chanted: the Resurrection Troparion, "Save, O Lord, Thy people...", and the Patron Saint of the Church, followed by the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (Tē hypermachō). Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, the Choirs chant the ancient Cross hymn: "Thy Cross we worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify!" (Ton Stavron sou proskynoumen, Despota). Epistle pericope: Hebrews 4:14–5:6; Gospel pericope: Mark 8:34–9:1. Communion Hymn: "The light of Thy countenance was marked upon us, O Lord!" (Ps. 4:7 LXX [Ps. 4:6 MT]). Instead of "We have seen the true Light", "Save, O Lord, Thy people..." is sung, and the Dismissal is pronounced. Mid-Week Rubrics of the Fourth Week: On Sunday evening, Penitential Vespers follows Paragraph 31. On Monday, Wednesday, and Friday of the Fourth Week, at the First Hour, instead of "Order my steps in Thy word" (Ps. 118:133 LXX), the Choirs chant "Thy Cross we worship, O Master...", and the Kontakion of the Cross: "No longer does the fiery sword guard the gate of Eden..." (Ouketi phloginē romphaia) is recited. At the Ninth Hour, the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] are chanted as usual. In Wednesday Vespers (Mid-Fast / Mesonēstismos), at "Glory... Both now and ever...", the Idiomelon "Today He Who is unapproachable in essence..." (Sēmeron ho aprositos tē ousia) is chanted. Saturday of the Fourth Week follows Paragraph 34 (Soul Saturday memorial order). Fourth Sunday of Great Lent (Commemoration of Saint John of the Ladder): Paragraph 41 codifies Great Vespers on Saturday evening: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera: six Resurrection Stichera from the Octoechos and four Prosomia of Saint John from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone. At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Stichera; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint; "Both now and ever...": "O marvelous wonder transcending all the ancient miracles! For who has ever known a mother bear without a man..." (Ō thaumatos kainou). Dismissal Troparia: the Resurrection Troparion, the Dismissal Troparion of Saint John: "Dweller of the desert and angel in the body..." (Tais tōn dakryōn sou rhoais), the Dismissal Theotokion, and Dismissal. Paragraph 42 opens Sunday Matins: Following Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon and the Triadica ("It is truly meet to glorify Thee, the Word of God..."), the Dismissal Troparion of Saint John, and the Six Psalms. Note [*]: In what appears to have been an editorial oversight, Violakis indicates "Thy pure icon we venerate, O Good One..." (Tēn achranton eikona sou) as the Dismissal Troparion, whereas the correct Dismissal Troparion is "Save, O Lord, Thy people..." (Sōson Kyrie ton laon sou).
[^426]: Great Lent: Fourth Sunday of Lent (Saint John of the Ladder) Matins Concluded and Divine Liturgy, and Wednesday of the Fifth Week Opening (§§ 42 Concluded, 43, 44 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p733 (printed page 718) and facing English leaf p734 (printed page 719). Fourth Sunday Matins Concluded: Paragraph 42 concludes Matins: At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers; the appointed Kathismata of the Psalter and the Amomos (Kathisma 17, Psalm 118 LXX); the Resurrection Kathismata, Evlogitaria, Hypakoe, Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of the Tone, and Resurrection Prokimenon. Canons: the Resurrection Canon of the Octoechos, the Canon of the Triodion ("I have become like the prodigal son, O Christ...", Hōmoiōthēn Christe) with refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", and the Canon of Saint John with refrain "Holy father John, pray to God for us". After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Saint, followed by his Kathisma and Theotokion from the Triodion; after Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Oikos of the Tone, the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion for Saint John of the Ladder (author of The Ladder of Divine Ascent). The Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..."
