(Anoixō to stoma mou); the complete order of the Resurrection Matins Gospel for Sundays of the Triodion; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Resurrection Exaposteilarion, the Exaposteilarion of Saint John ("O venerable John of perpetual memory..."), and the Theotokion. At the Praises (Lauds), eight Stichera: four Resurrection Stichera and four Stichera of Saint John from Vespers, with Psalm verses 111:1 LXX ("Blessed is the man that feareth the Lord...") and 115:6 LXX ("Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints...") preceding the final two Stichera; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint: "Come, let us work in the mystical vineyard..." (Deute ergasōmetha en tō mystikō ampelōni); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."; Great Doxology, and "Today salvation is come unto the world" (Sēmeron sōtēria). Fourth Sunday Divine Liturgy: Paragraph 43 codifies the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great: Typika and the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] of the Tone for four, and four Troparia from Ode 6 of the Canon of Saint John from the Triodion. After the Little Entrance: the Resurrection Dismissal Troparion, that of Saint John, that of the Patron Saint of the Church, and the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader". Epistle pericope: Hebrews 6:13–20; Gospel pericope: Mark 9:17–31 (Healing of the Boy with a Mute Spirit). Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest. Alleluia" (Ps. 148:1 LXX); "We have seen the true Light", and Dismissal. Vespers on Sunday evening follows Paragraph 31. Wednesday of the Fifth Week Opening: Paragraph 44 opens Vespers of the Presanctified: After the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress I cried...", Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX)... Note [*]: If the Fourth Sunday of Lent falls on March 30 (the feast day of Saint John in the Menaion), the Memorial of the Triodion is omitted in favor of the Synaxarion of the Menaion.
[^427]: Great Lent: Wednesday of the Fifth Week (Presanctified Liturgy, Great Canon at Compline) and Thursday Matins Opening (§§ 44 Concluded, 45, 46 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p735 (printed page 720) and facing English leaf p736 (printed page 721). Wednesday Presanctified Liturgy: Paragraph 44 concludes the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on the fifth Wednesday of Lent: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), the Choirs begin chanting from verse 10: "The sinners shall fall into their own net, whilst that I withal escape" (Ps. 140:10 LXX [Ps. 141:10 MT]), singing the Idiomelon "Falling among thieves of my own lustful thoughts..." (Tois tōn emōn logismōn) twice, the Martyrikon once, three Prosomia from the Triodion, and all twenty-four alphabetical Stichera from the Great Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete. Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 17:1–9 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 17:17–18:5 LXX), "Let my prayer arise in Thy sight as incense" (Ps. 140:2 LXX), and the rest of the Presanctified Liturgy in regular order. The Great Canon with Compline: Paragraph 45 details the ancient monastic order of chanting the Great Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete at Small Compline on Wednesday evening: After "It is truly meet", the Great Canon is chanted with the penitential refrain: "Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me!" (Eleēson me ho Theos eleēson me). After Ode 6, the Kontakion "My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end is drawing near, and thou shalt be confounded..." (Psychē mou, psychē mou, anasta) is chanted slowly in Tone 6; subsequently Odes 7, 8, and 9 are sung, concluding with the Heirmos "Ineffable is the childbearing of a seedless conception..." (Asporou syllēpseōs). The Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion "My soul, my soul, arise!", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour..." (Ho en panti kairō), "More honorable than the Cherubim", the priestly blessing "May God be merciful unto us...", the Supplicatory Prayer to the Mother of God by Paul the Monk of Evergetis: "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Virgin..." (Aspile, amolynte), the Prayer of Antiochus the Monk to Christ: "And grant unto us, Master, as we depart unto sleep, rest of body and soul..." (Kai dos hēmin, Despota), "O most glorious Ever-Virgin...", "The Father is my hope...", "All my hope I place in thee...", the Dismissal, the Litany of Mutual Forgiveness ("Let us pray for the peace of the world..."), and "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". Thursday Matins Opening: Paragraph 46 opens the Matins service: Following the Midnight Office and the Six Psalms, "Alleluia" is chanted 12 times with verses in the Tone of the week, followed by the Triadika of the Tone from the Horologion. After the First Stichologia of the Psalter, the Penitential Kathismata of the Octoechos; after the Second Stichologia, the Kathismata found after Ode 3 of the Triodion Canon. Psalm 50, "Save, O God, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance...", and the Nine Biblical Odes are chanted verse by verse (see Paragraph 18). Then Odes 1 and 3 of the Canon of the Saint of the day from the Menaion with their Heirmos, the Small Litany, and the Kathisma of the Saint with its Theotokion...
[^428]: Great Lent: Thursday of the Fifth Week Matins Concluded, Thursday Vespers, and The Great Canon with Matins Opening (§§ 46 Concluded, 47, 48 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p737 (printed page 722) and facing English leaf p738 (printed page 723). Thursday Matins Concluded: Paragraph 46 concludes Thursday Matins: The Triodia of Ode 4 from the Triodion, Odes 5 and 6 of the Canon from the Menaion, the Heirmos and Small Litany, the Kontakion and Oikos of the Triodion, the Menologion and Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion; then the Troparia of the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] with the refrain: "Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me!". Subsequently, the Triodia of Odes 8 and 9, "Let us praise, bless, and worship the Lord", and the Heirmos "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify..." (Hon stratiai ouranōn doxazousi). After "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Heirmos "Ineffable is the childbearing of a seedless conception..." (Asporou syllēpseōs), "It is truly meet", the Exaposteilarion, the Small Doxology: "To Thee belongs glory" (Soi doxa prepei, read), "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord", the Idiomela of the Aposticha twice, the Martyrikon once, "Glory... Both now and ever...": Theotokion, "It is good to confess unto the Lord" (Ps. 91:2 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, "Standing in the temple of Thy glory...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God...", "O Heavenly King...", three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem the Syrian, and the Hours in regular order with the Kontakion "My soul, my soul, arise!" in each Hour. In the Ninth Hour, the Beatitudes are chanted; after the Trisagion Prayers, the Prayer "All-holy Trinity...", and Dismissal. Thursday Vespers and Presanctified Liturgy: Paragraph 47 codifies Thursday Vespers: Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX), Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter (Pss. 119–133 LXX); at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), the Idiomelon "Having willingly stripped myself of the divinely woven garment..." (Autexousiōs exedythēn) twice, the Martyrikon once, three Prosomia from the Triodion, and four from the Menaion; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Stavrotheotokion. Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 18:20–33 LXX) and Proverbs (Prov. 18:10–19:4 LXX), "Let my prayer arise", and the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated. The Great Canon with Matins Opening: Paragraph 48 details the solemn rubrical order for Matins when the Great Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete is chanted in its entirety: The Priest proclaims the blessing: "Blessed is our God..."; after "O Heavenly King", the Trisagion Prayers, "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "O come, let us worship...", and the Royal Psalms [Psalms 19 and 20 LXX]: "The Lord hear thee in the day of affliction..." (Ps. 19:2 LXX), during which the Priest solemnly censes around the entire church with the hand-censer or Katzion. Trisagion Prayers, "Save, O Lord, Thy people...", and the Six Psalms. "Alleluia" 12 times with verses in the Tone of the week, and the Triadika with their special concluding petitions: at the first verse: "By the intercessions of the Holy Apostles...", at the second verse: "By the intercessions of the Hierarch [Saint Nicholas]...", and at the third verse: "Through the intercessions of the Theotokos...". Footnote 29: In parish and cathedral practice, this solemn service of the Great Canon is often celebrated on Wednesday evening around the First Hour of the night (7 P.M.), or early Thursday morning, as is also done with the Akathist Hymn on the Fifth Saturday.
[^429]: Great Lent: Thursday of the Fifth Week: The Great Canon with Matins Concluded (§ 48 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p739 (printed page 724) and facing English leaf p740 (printed page 725). The Great Canon with Matins Concluded: Paragraph 48 concludes the solemn celebration of the Great Canon of Saint Andrew of Crete: The appointed Kathisma of the Psalter (which, on account of the great length of the service, is usually omitted and read in the morning before the Hours; see Footnote 30), the Small Litany, the Apostolic Kathismata of the Tone, Psalm 50, and the Great Canon begins, reciting before each Troparion (except the Heirmos) the refrain: "Have mercy on me, O God, have mercy on me!" (Eleēson me ho Theos eleēson me). After Ode 3, the Heirmos "Establish, O Lord, my unstable heart upon the rock of Thy commandments..." (Stereōson, Kyrie), the Small Litany, and three Kathismata from the Triodion; then the Second Choir begins the Triodia for ten with the refrain "Holy Apostles of Christ, intercede for us!" (Apostoloi tou Christou, presveusate hyper hēmōn). In continuation, Odes 4, 5, and 6 of the Great Canon, concluding with the Heirmos "I cried with my whole heart unto the merciful God, and He heard me from the lowest depths of Hades, and raised up my life from corruption!" (Evoēsa en holē tē kardia mou). The Small Litany, the Kontakion "My soul, my soul, arise! Why sleepest thou? The end is drawing near, and thou shalt be confounded; awake then, and be watchful, that Christ God may spare thee, Who is everywhere present and fillest all things" (Psychē mou, psychē mou, anasta), the Oikos, the Menologion of the day from the Menaion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion recounting how Saint Andrew, Archbishop of Crete, composed this monumental hymn of compunction, and how the life of Saint Mary of Egypt is set forth as an archetype of repentance. The Second Choir begins the Beatitudes [Makarismoi]: "In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom" with the penitential Troparia of the thief: "Thou didst make the thief a citizen of Paradise...". Odes 7, 8, and 9 of the Great Canon and the Triodia are chanted in sequence. At Ode 8, instead of "Glory...", "We bless Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, the Lord!" is sung, and after "Both now and ever...", "We praise, bless, and worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him unto all ages!". Heirmos of Ode 8: "Him Whom the heavenly hosts glorify...", Small Litany, and "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light, let us magnify in hymns!". Ode 9 concludes with the Heirmos "Ineffable is the childbearing of a seedless conception..." (Asporou syllēpseōs), Small Litany, and the Exaposteilarion is read. Small Doxology ("To Thee belongs glory", read), "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord", the Aposticha of the Praises (Lauds), "It is good to confess unto the Lord" (Ps. 91:2 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "O Heavenly King, strengthen our rulers...", three great prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem the Syrian, and the Dismissal is given. In the morning, following the designated Kathisma of the Psalter, the Hours are read in standard non-penitential form, followed by Vespers and the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. Footnote 31: While reciting "Lord, have mercy" 12 times has become customary practice (as noted in the Typikon of Constantine and for Holy Week Matins), the canonical order requires after the Trisagion Prayers: "Standing in the temple of Thy glory, we seem to stand in heaven...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "More honorable than the Cherubim", "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God...", and "O Heavenly King..." (see Paragraph 18).
[^430]: Great Lent: Friday of the Fifth Week (Presanctified Liturgy) and The Akathist Hymn with Compline Opening (§§ 49, 50 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p741 (printed page 726) and facing English leaf p742 (printed page 727). Friday Presanctified Liturgy: Paragraph 49 codifies the celebration of the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on Friday of the Fifth Week of Great Lent: At Vespers, after the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress I cried...", Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six verses are set: the Idiomelon "As the man who went down from Jerusalem fell among thieves..." (Hōs ex Hierousalēm) twice, the Martyrikon once, and the three Prosomia of the Forefeast of the Annunciation: "Revealing the pre-eternal counsel to thee, O Maiden..." (Boulēn proaiōnion) once each (repeated to total 6 Stichera); "Glory... Both now and ever...": the Doxastikon "The mystery hidden from all eternity is revealed today..." (To ap' aiōnos mystērion). Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light" (Phōs hilaron), the two Old Testament Readings of the day from Genesis (Gen. 22:1–18 LXX, the Sacrifice of Isaac) and Proverbs (Prov. 17:17–18:5 LXX), "Let my prayer arise" (Kateuthynthētō), and the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts is celebrated. The Akathist Hymn with Compline: Paragraph 50 details the monumental rubrics for the Friday evening celebration of the entire Akathist Hymn (Chairetismoi) integrated into Small Compline: After "It is truly meet" (Axion estin), the Troparion "When the Angel perceived what had secretly been commanded, he came with haste to Joseph's dwelling..." (To prostachthen mystikōs) is chanted in Tone 8: twice in a slow, ornate melody (argōs) and a third time in a concise, syllabic melody (syntomon). The Hierarch [or presiding Priest in epitrachelion and phelonion] stands before the icon of the Mother of God on the tetrapod and solemnly intones the First Stasis of the 24 Oikoi [Stanzas 1–6: Incipits Alpha to Zeta, beginning "An Angel and the chiefest among them was sent from heaven to say unto the Mother of God: Rejoice!..."], to which the choir refrains "Rejoice, Bride unwedded!" (Chaire, Nymphē anympheute). Following "For many years, O Master" (Eis polla etē), the Canon of the Akathist composed by Saint Joseph the Hymnographer: "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..." (Anoixō to stoma mou) in Tone 4 is chanted in six Troparia. After Ode 3, the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader..." (Tē hypermachō) is chanted again slowly, and the Hierarch reads the Second Stasis of the Oikoi [Stanzas 7–12: Incipits Eta to Mu, recounting the Adoration of the Shepherds and Magi, and the Flight into Egypt], refraining "Alleluia!". The Second Choir then chants the Fourth, Fifth, and Sixth Odes of the Canon, beginning with the Heirmos "He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the divine Word..." (Ho kathēmenos en doxē); at the conclusion of Ode 6, both choirs unite to chant "To Thee, the Champion Leader..." in a moderately slow tempo (argosyntomon dichoron), and the Third Stasis of the Oikoi [Stanzas 13–18: Incipits Nu to Sigma, theological contemplation of the Virgin Birth and divine condescension] is read. The First Choir chants the Seventh Ode: "The godly-minded youths worshipped not the creature rather than the Creator..." (Ouk elatrevsan) through the Ninth Ode, during which the Deacon performs a complete censing of the holy sanctuary, the tetrapod, and the entire church. Following Ode 9, both choirs unite once more to sing "To Thee, the Champion Leader..." in a moderately slow tempo, and the Fourth Stasis of the Oikoi [Stanzas 19–24: Incipits Tau to Omega, concluding with the climactic supplication "O all-praised Mother, who didst bear the Word..."] is solemnly recited. Footnote 32: Violakis cross-references Appendix II of the Typikon for the elaborate ceremonial protocol observed in the Ecumenical Patriarchate for the celebration of the Akathist Hymn.
[^431]: Great Lent: The Akathist Hymn with Compline Concluded, Saturday Matins and Divine Liturgy (§§ 50 Concluded, 51, 52 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p743 (printed page 728) and facing English leaf p744 (printed page 729). Akathist Compline Concluded: Paragraph 50 concludes the Friday evening service of the Akathist Hymn: Following the Fourth Stasis of the Oikoi, the Trisagion Prayers are recited, the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (Tē hypermachō) is read plain (chyma), "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), and the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour art worshipped and glorified in heaven and on earth, Christ our God..." (Ho en panti kairō). "Lord, have mercy" (3 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "More honorable than the Cherubim", "In the name of the Lord, Father, give the blessing!", the priestly blessing "May God be merciful unto us, and bless us...", and the nocturnal supplicatory prayers: the Supplicatory Prayer to the All-Holy Theotokos by Paul the Monk of the Monastery of the Benefactress (Evergetis): "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Virgin, Lady Bride of God..." (Aspile, amolynte), the Prayer of Antiochus the Monk to our Lord Jesus Christ: "And grant unto us, Master, as we depart unto sleep, rest of body and soul..." (Kai dos hēmin, Despota), "O most glorious Ever-Virgin, Mother of Christ our God...", "The Father is my hope, the Son is my refuge, the Holy Spirit is my protection: O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee!", and "All my hope I place in thee, O Mother of God: keep me under thy protection!". The Dismissal of Compline is pronounced, followed by the Litany of Mutual Forgiveness: "Let us pray for the peace of the world..." (Evxōmetha hyper tēs eirēnēs tou kosmou). At the close of the service, the Choirs chant slowly the ornate Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 3: "The beauty of thy virginity and the surpassing splendor of thy purity did Gabriel behold..." (Tēn hōraiotēta tēs parthenias sou), and the service concludes with the final priestly blessing: "Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.". Saturday Matins of the Akathist: Paragraph 51 codifies the Matins service on the Fifth Saturday of Great Lent (Saturday of the Akathist): Following the Midnight Office and the Six Psalms, at "God is the Lord", the festal Troparion "When the Angel perceived what had secretly been commanded..." (To prostachthen mystikōs) is chanted twice; then the designated Kathisma of the Psalter (Kathisma 16 or 17), followed by the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma]: "The great commander of the bodiless angels..." (Ho megas stratēgos) twice, Psalm 50 (read), and the Canon "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..." (Anoixō to stoma mou) in six Troparia. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn "The great commander" is chanted once. At Ode 6, the Triodia from the Triodion are appended; after Ode 6, the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" and the Oikos "An Archangel was sent from heaven to say unto the Mother of God: Rejoice!..." (Angelos prōtostatēs). The Menologion from the Menaion and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion recounting the miraculous deliverance of Constantinople from barbarian siege in 626 under Emperor Heraclius and Patriarch Sergius; then the remaining Odes of the Canon with the Triodia. Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth..."; after Ode 8, "More honorable than the Cherubim", and the Exaposteilarion: "The mystery hidden from all eternity is made known today..." (To ap' aiōnos mystērion) twice. At the Praises [Lauds], four Stichera: "The hidden mystery..." (To kekrymmenon mystērion) twice, "A glorious palace hath been prepared for Thee..." (Pastas evprepēs), and "Gabriel the Archangel came openly unto thee..." (Gabriēl ho Archangelos); "Glory... Both now and ever...": "The Theotokos heard a tongue that she knew not..." (Glōssan hēn ouk egnō), the Great Doxology, and the Troparion "When the Angel perceived...". Divine Liturgy of the Akathist: Paragraph 52 opens the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom: Typika and the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] with Troparia selected from Odes 3 and 6 of the Canon of the Theotokos. After the Little Entrance, the Troparion "When the Angel perceived..." and the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader". Epistle pericope: Hebrews 9:1–7 (The Earthly Sanctuary and Ark of the Covenant); Gospel pericope: Luke 1:39–56 (The Visitation and the Magnificat).
[^432]: Great Lent: Saturday of the Akathist: Divine Liturgy Concluded and The Akathist Hymn in Matins (§§ 52 Concluded, 53). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p745 (printed page 730) and facing English leaf p746 (printed page 731). Saturday Divine Liturgy Concluded: Paragraph 52 concludes the Divine Liturgy on Saturday of the Akathist: In place of the Megalynarion "It is truly meet", the Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Akathist Canon is chanted: "Let every earthborn mortal rejoice in spirit, bearing his lamp..." (Hapas gēgenēs). Communion Hymn [Koinōnikon]: "I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord. Alleluia!" (Ps. 115:4 LXX [Ps. 116:13 MT]). "We have seen the true Light", and the Dismissal is pronounced. The Akathist Hymn in Matins: Paragraph 53 codifies the solemn communal parish and cathedral practice of celebrating the complete Akathist Hymn integrated into Matins on Friday evening or early Saturday morning (a custom widely practiced throughout the Patriarchate and parish churches): The service opens with the priestly exclamation: "Blessed is our God, always, now and ever, and unto the ages of ages", "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", "O Heavenly King", the Trisagion Prayers, "For Thine is the kingdom...", "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "O come, let us worship" (3 times), and the Royal Psalms: Psalm 19 LXX ("The Lord hear thee in the day of affliction...") and Psalm 20 LXX ("In Thy strength the king shall rejoice, O Lord..."). The Trisagion Prayers, the Troparion of the Cross "Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance...", and the Six Psalms. After the Great Litany, "God is the Lord" (Theos Kyrios) is chanted four times: twice in a slow melody and twice in a fast melody with the designated verses. Then the festal Troparion "When the Angel perceived what had secretly been commanded..." (To prostachthen mystikōs) is sung twice slowly and a third time quickly. The Hierarch [or presiding Priest] reads the First Stasis of the Oikoi [Stanzas 1–6]. After "For many years, O Master", "To Thee, the Champion Leader" is sung slowly, and the Second Stasis [Stanzas 7–12] is read, followed by "To Thee, the Champion Leader" chanted at a quicker tempo by both choirs together. The Small Litany, Psalm 50, and the Canon "I shall open my mouth" in six Troparia with the Katavasia at the end of each Ode. After Ode 3, the Small Litany, "To Thee, the Champion Leader" quickly by both choirs, and the Third Stasis [Stanzas 13–18] is read. Again both choirs chant "To Thee, the Champion Leader", followed by the Small Litany and the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] "The great commander of the bodiless angels..." once. Odes 4, 5, and 6 of the Canon are sung, followed immediately by the Triodia for ten with the refrain "Holy Saints of God, intercede for us!". At the conclusion of Ode 6, the Heirmos "The prophet was delivered from the sea monster..." (Tou kētous ton Prophētēn), both choirs chant "To Thee, the Champion Leader", and the Fourth Stasis [Stanzas 19–24] is read. After "For many years, O Master", the Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" is read plain (chyma), followed immediately by the Menologion and the Memorial of the Triodion. Odes 7 and 8 of the Canon are chanted with the Triodia. Footnote 33: Violakis notes that when the Akathist service is sung at the First Hour of the night (7:00 P.M.), the Kathisma of the Psalter is customarily omitted on account of the length of the service and read the following morning.
[^433]: Great Lent: Fifth Sunday of Great Lent (Commemoration of Saint Mary of Egypt): Great Vespers and Sunday Matins Opening (§§ 53 Concluded, 54, 55 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p747 (printed page 732) and facing English leaf p748 (printed page 733). The Akathist Matins Concluded: Paragraph 53 concludes the Matins celebration of the Akathist: Instead of "More honorable than the Cherubim", Ode 9 of the Canon of the Theotokos is chanted with the Triodia. Then the Exaposteilarion: "The mystery hidden from all eternity is made known today..." (To ap' aiōnos mystērion) twice; the Praises [Lauds] in four Stichera: "The hidden mystery..." twice, "A glorious palace hath been prepared for Thee...", and "Gabriel the Archangel came openly unto thee..."; "Glory... Both now and ever...": "The Theotokos heard a tongue that she knew not...". The Great Doxology, the Troparion "When the Angel perceived...", the Augmented Litany [Ektenē], and the Dismissal. In the morning, the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated according to Paragraph 52. Fifth Sunday of Great Lent (Saint Mary of Egypt): Paragraph 54 codifies Great Vespers on Saturday evening: At "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), ten Stichera: six Resurrection Stichera in the occurring Tone of the week from the Octoechos and four Stichera of the Venerable Mother Mary of Egypt from the Triodion; "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 2: "The power of Thy Cross, O Christ, hath wrought wonders; for even she who formerly was a harlot chose to endure ascetic struggles..." (Ethaumatourgēse, Christe, tou Stavrou sou hē dynamis); "Both now and ever...": the first Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone from the Octoechos. At the Aposticha: the Resurrection Stichera of the Tone; "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint in Tone 2: "The snares of the soul and the passions of the body hast thou severed with the spiritual sword of abstinence..." (Ta tēs psychēs thēreumata); "Both now and ever...": "O marvelous wonder transcending all the ancient miracles! For who has ever known a mother bear without a man..." (Ō thaumatos kainou). Dismissal Troparia: the Resurrection Troparion; "Glory...": the Dismissal Troparion of the Venerable Mother in Tone 8: "In thee, O mother, was preserved uncorrupt that which is according to the divine image..." (En soi mēter akribōs diesōthē to kat' eikona); "Both now and ever...": the Dismissal Theotokion of the Tone: "Thou Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One..." (Ho di' hēmas gennētheis ek Parthenou), and Dismissal. Sunday Matins Opening: Paragraph 55 opens Matins: Following Psalm 50, the Triadic Canon and the Triadica ("It is truly meet to glorify Thee, the Word of God..."), the Dismissal Troparion of Saint Mary, and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord": the Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers; the designated Kathisma of the Psalter and the Amomos (Kathisma 17, Psalm 118 LXX); the Resurrection Kathismata, the Evlogitaria, the Hypakoe, the Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of the Tone, and the Resurrection Prokimenon. Canons: the Resurrection Canon of the Octoechos and the two Canons of the Triodion; the refrain for the first Canon is "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee", and for the second Canon "Holy Mother of God [Mary], intercede for us!". After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Venerable Mother: "Having fled from the darkness of sin, and having illumined thy heart with the light of repentance..." (Tēn hamartias omichlēn ekphygousa), with her Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] and its Theotokion. After Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Oikos of the Tone; the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion recounting the extraordinary life and repentance of Saint Mary of Egypt in the desert of Jordan. Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth...". Note [*]: If the Fifth Sunday of Lent falls on April 1 (the feast day of Saint Mary of Egypt in the Menaion), the Memorial of the Triodion is omitted in favor of the Menaion synaxarion.
[^434]: Great Lent: Fifth Sunday of Great Lent (Saint Mary of Egypt) Matins Concluded, Basil Liturgy, Sunday Vespers, Wednesday Presanctified, and Friday of the Palms Opening (§§ 55 Concluded, 56, 57, 58, 59 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p749 (printed page 734) and facing English leaf p750 (printed page 735). Fifth Sunday Matins Concluded: Paragraph 55 concludes Matins: Katavasiai: "I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit..." (Anoixō to stoma mou), and the complete order of the Resurrection Matins Gospel for Sundays of the Triodion; after "More honorable than the Cherubim", the Resurrection Exaposteilarion, the Exaposteilarion of the Venerable Mother: "Thee we have as a pattern of repentance, all-lauded Mary..." (Hypodeigma metanoias se echontes), and the Theotokion: "O sweetness of the Angels, joy of the afflicted, protectress of Christians, Virgin Mother of the Lord..." (Ho glykasmos tōn Angelōn). At the Praises [Lauds], eight Stichera: four Resurrection Stichera and four of the Saint from Vespers, preceded by Psalm verses 39:2 LXX ("I waited patiently for the Lord; and He attended unto me, and heard my supplication", Ps. 40:1 MT) and 39:3 LXX ("And He set my feet upon a rock, and ordered my goings", Ps. 40:2 MT); "Glory...": Doxastikon of the Saint in Tone 1: "The kingdom of God is not food and drink, but righteousness and ascetic discipline with holiness..." (Ouk estin hē basileia tou Theou brōsis kai posis); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..."; Great Doxology, and the Sunday Troparion "Today salvation is come unto the world" (Sēmeron sōtēria). Fifth Sunday Divine Liturgy: Paragraph 56 codifies the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great: Typika and the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] of the Tone for four, and four Troparia from Ode 6 of the Canon of the Venerable Mother from the Triodion. After the Little Entrance, the Dismissal Troparia: that of the Resurrection, that of the Venerable Mother, and that of the Patron Saint of the Church. The Kontakion "To Thee, the Champion Leader" (if the Feast of the Annunciation has already passed, "O protection of Christians that cannot be put to shame...", Prostasia tōn christianōn akataischynte, is chanted instead; see Paragraph 40 of the Protheoria). Epistle pericope: Hebrews 9:11–14 (Christ the High Priest of the Good Things to Come); Gospel pericope: Mark 10:32–45 (Christ Foretells His Passion, and the Request of the Sons of Zebedee). Communion Hymn [Koinōnikon]: "Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest. Alleluia!" (Ps. 148:1 LXX); "We have seen the true Light", and the Dismissal is given. Sunday Vespers: Paragraph 57 confirms that penitential Vespers on Sunday evening follows Paragraph 31. Sixth Wednesday Vespers: Paragraph 58 codifies the celebration of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on Wednesday of the Sixth Week: At Vespers, after the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter ("Unto the Lord in my distress I cried...", Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), the Idiomelon "Being rich in passions, I have clothed myself in the deceitful garment of hypocrisy..." (Ploutōn en pathesin) twice, the Martyrikon "Having in your souls an unquenchable love of Christ, O holy Martyrs..." (Alēkton echontes) once, three Prosomia from the Triodion, and four Prosomia from the Menaion; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Prosomion Theotokion from the Menaion. Old Testament Readings: Genesis 43:26–31, 45:1–16 LXX (Joseph reveals himself to his brothers) and Proverbs 21:23–22:4 LXX; "Let my prayer arise", and the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts in regular order. Friday of the Palms Opening: Paragraph 59 opens the eve of Lazarus Saturday (Friday before Palm Sunday), concluding the holy Forty Days of Great Lent: At Vespers, after the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter, at "Lord, I have cried", eight Stichera are set...
[^435]: Great Lent: Friday before Palm Sunday Concluded, Small Compline, and Saturday of Lazarus Matins Opening (§§ 59 Concluded, 60, 61 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p751 (printed page 736) and facing English leaf p752 (printed page 737). Friday Vespers Concluded: Paragraph 59 concludes Great Lent on the eve of Lazarus Saturday (Friday before Palm Sunday): At Vespers, after the Eighteenth Kathisma of the Psalter (Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), eight Stichera are set according to Violakis (though ancient Triodion usage doubles the first two Idiomela to reach 10 Stichera, ST p. 638, footnote 1229): the Small Idiomelon "Having accomplished the Forty Days that profit our souls, we pray to behold the Holy Week of Thy Passion..." (Tēn psychōphelē plērōsantes Tessarakostēn) twice, the Martyrikon "O Martyrs of the Lord, make supplication unto our God..." (Martyres Kyriou) once, and the five Idiomela of Lazarus ("O Lord, desiring to see the tomb of Lazarus...", "O Lord, coming to the tomb of Lazarus...", "O Lord, Thy voice destroyed the kingdom of Hades...", "O Lord, wishing to assure Thy Disciples of Thy Resurrection...", "O Lord, Thou didst come to Bethany to raise Lazarus..."). "Glory...": Doxastikon "Standing by the tomb of Lazarus, O our Savior..." (Epistas tō mnēmati Lazarou); "Both now and ever...": the Great Idiomelon "Having accomplished the Forty Days that profit our souls, let us cry aloud: Rejoice, O city of Bethany, home of Lazarus!..." (Tēn psychōphelē plērōsantes Tessarakostēn, boēsōmen: Chairois polis Bēthania). Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light" (Phōs hilaron), Old Testament Readings: Genesis 49:33–50:26 LXX (The Death and Burial of Jacob and Joseph) and Proverbs 31:8–31 LXX (The Virtuous Woman); "Let my prayer arise" (Kateuthynthētō), and the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts. Footnote 34: Violakis establishes that from this day forward neither a Martyrikon nor any hymn from the Octoechos is chanted until the Saturday of the Apodosis of Saint Thomas Sunday. Small Compline of Lazarus: Paragraph 60 details Small Compline on Friday evening: After "It is truly meet" (Axion estin), the Canon of Lazarus by Saint Cosmas the Melodist is sung with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!". After Ode 9, the Heirmos "He hath shown strength with His arm..." (Epoiēse kratos), Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion "Christ Who is the Truth and Joy of all..." (Hē pantōn chara Christos), "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", and the nocturnal prayer to the Mother of God: "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Virgin..." (Aspile, amolynte). Dismissal with the Litany of Mutual Forgiveness: "Let us pray for the peace of the world...", and "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". Saturday Matins Opening: Paragraph 61 opens Matins of Lazarus Saturday: After Psalm 50, the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 1: "In confirming the common Resurrection before Thy Passion, Thou didst raise Lazarus from the dead, O Christ God..." (Tēn koinēn Anastasin), and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparion is sung three times; then the appointed Kathismata of the Psalter: Kathisma 16 (Ps. 109 LXX) and Kathisma 17 (the Amomos, Ps. 118 LXX), followed by the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] from the Triodion: "Having compassion on the tears of Martha and Mary, O Christ God..." (Katoikteiras tēs Marthas) twice, and the Resurrection Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes...").
[^436]: Great Lent: Saturday of Lazarus: Matins Concluded and Divine Liturgy (§§ 61 Concluded, 62). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p753 (printed page 738) and facing English leaf p754 (printed page 739). Lazarus Matins Concluded: Paragraph 61 concludes Matins of Lazarus Saturday: Following the Small Litany after the Evlogitaria, the second Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] from the Triodion in Tone 5 is chanted: "The fountain of wisdom and foreknowledge..." (Hē pēgē tēs sophias) twice; and immediately "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." (Anastasin Christou theasamenoi) and Psalm 50 recited plain (chyma). Both Canons of Lazarus (the first by Saint Theophanes the Branded and the second by Saint Cosmas the Melodist) are chanted through Ode 6 with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!". After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymns from the Triodion; after Ode 5, the Four-Ode Canon [Tetraodion] of Ode 6, followed by the Kontakion in Tone 2: "Christ Who is the Truth and Joy of all..." (Hē pantōn chara) and its Oikos: "Since Thou didst bind Hades, O Immortal One...". The Menologion from the Menaion and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion detailing the raising of Lazarus four days dead as the harbinger of Christ's Resurrection and the general resurrection of mankind. Katavasiai: "Having traversed the water as if it were dry land..." (Ygran diodevsas). In place of "More honorable than the Cherubim", Ode 9 of both Canons is chanted with their appointed verses, and the Katavasia: "Confessing thee in truth to be the Mother of God..." (Kyriōs Theotokon). Then "Holy is the Lord our God" twice (the Psalm verse "Exalt ye the Lord our God..." is omitted). Exapostilaria: "By Thy word, O Word of God, Lazarus now leapeth forth..." (Logō sou Loge tou Theou) twice, and "Through Lazarus hath Christ already despoiled thee, O Death..." (Dia Lazarou se Christos) once. At the Praises [Lauds], eight Idiomela in Tone 1: 1. "Christ, the Resurrection and Life of mankind...", 2. "Thou didst raise Lazarus four days dead from Hades, O Christ...", 3. "Martha and Mary said unto the Savior...", 4. "Confirming the reality of Thy divine nature, O Christ...", 5. "After four days Thou didst raise Thy friend in Bethany, O Christ...", 6. "Martha cried aloud unto Mary: The Teacher is present!...", 7. "Lazarus that was dead four days didst Thou raise up in Bethany...", 8. "Even as Thou saidst unto Martha, O Lord: I am the Resurrection...". "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 2: "A great and marvelous wonder is fulfilled today!..." (Mega kai paradoxon thauma); "Both now and ever...": "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos...". Great Doxology, and the Sunday Troparion "Today salvation is come unto the world" (Sēmeron sōtēria). Divine Liturgy of Lazarus Saturday: Paragraph 62 codifies the Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom on Lazarus Saturday: Typika (Pss. 102 and 145 LXX) and Beatitudes [Makarismoi] with Troparia from Ode 3 of the first Canon and Ode 6 of the second Canon. After the Little Entrance, the Entrance Troparion: "O come, let us worship and fall down before Christ! Save us, O Son of God, Who didst rise from the dead, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!", the Dismissal Troparion: "In confirming the common Resurrection...", and the Kontakion: "Christ Who is the Truth and Joy of all...". In place of the Trisagion Hymn: the baptismal proclamation "As many of you as were baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia!" (Gal. 3:27), witnessing to the ancient practice of conferring Holy Baptism on Lazarus Saturday. Epistle pericope: Hebrews 12:28–13:8 LXX (Wherefore we receiving a kingdom which cannot be moved...); Gospel pericope: John 11:1–45 (The Raising of Lazarus). At "Especially [among the all-holy]...", the Heirmos of Ode 9: "With all peoples let us come and glorify the pure Theotokos..." (Tēn hagnēn endoxōs timēsōmen). Communion Hymn [Koinōnikon]: "Out of the mouths of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise. Alleluia!" (Ps. 8:2 LXX).
[^437]: Great Lent & Holy Week: Saturday of Lazarus Divine Liturgy Concluded and Palm Sunday Great Vespers (§§ 62 Concluded, 63, 64 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p755 (printed page 740) and facing English leaf p756 (printed page 741). Lazarus Liturgy Concluded: Paragraph 62 concludes the Divine Liturgy of Lazarus Saturday: In place of "We have seen the true Light", the Dismissal Troparion "In confirming the common Resurrection..." (Tēn koinēn Anastasin) is chanted; then "Blessed be the name of the Lord" (Ps. 112:2 LXX), and the solemn Dismissal: "May He Who rose from the dead, Christ our true God...". Footnote 35: Violakis strictly notes that on Lazarus Saturday, Palm Sunday, and Saint Thomas Sunday, memorial services with boiled wheat [kolyvo] are forbidden; however, the names of the departed may be commemorated at the conclusion of the Divine Liturgy during the chanting of the four mortuary Troparia: "With the souls of the righteous departed..." (Meta pneumatōn dikaiōn). Palm Sunday Great Vespers: Paragraph 63 codifies Great Vespers on the eve of Palm Sunday (The Entrance of our Lord into Jerusalem, Kyriakē tōn Baïōn): After the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the complete First Kathisma of the Psalter (Pss. 1–8 LXX: "Blessed is the man..."), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six Stichera are chanted by doubling the first of the five Idiomela: "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together..." (Sēmeron hē charis tou Hagiou Pneumatos) doubled, "Today the Word and co-eternal Son of God the Father..." (Ho echōn thronon Ouranon), "Let us also come today, all the new Israel..." (Deute kai hēmeis sēmeron), "Prefiguring for us Thy holy Resurrection, O Good One..." (Tēn septēn Anastasin), and "Six days before the Passover, Jesus came unto Bethany..." (Pro hex hēmerōn tou Pascha). Note [******] records that according to ancient Triodion practice, all five Stichera are doubled to total 10. "Glory...": "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together..."; "Both now and ever...": the same hymn is repeated (see note [*******]). Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light" (Phōs hilaron), the Saturday evening Great Prokimenon: "The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty!" (Ps. 92:1 LXX), and three Old Testament Readings: Genesis 49:1–2, 8–12 LXX (Jacob prophesies the scepter of Judah until Shiloh comes), the Prophecy of Zephaniah 3:14–19 LXX ("Sing, O daughter of Zion; shout, O Israel; be glad and rejoice with all the heart..."), and the Prophecy of Zechariah 9:9–15 LXX ("Rejoice greatly, O daughter of Zion; shout, O daughter of Jerusalem: behold, thy King cometh unto thee... lowly, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass"). At the Aposticha, three Idiomela: "Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion!..." (Chaire kai evphrainou, polis Siōn) with verse Ps. 8:2 LXX ("Out of the mouths of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise"), "Today the Savior cometh unto Jerusalem to fulfill the Scriptures..." with verse Ps. 8:1 LXX ("O Lord, our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth!"), and "O Thou Who sittest upon the Cherubim and art praised by the Seraphim!...". "Glory...": "Today the grace..."; "Both now and ever...": and again "Today the grace...". Dismissal Troparia: "In confirming the common Resurrection..." twice; "Glory... Both now and ever...": the second Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God, we have been granted immortal life by Thy Resurrection..." (Syntaphentes soi dia tou Baptismatos) once, and Dismissal. Paragraph 64 opens Palm Sunday Matins.
[^438]: Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Matins (§ 64). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p757 (printed page 742) and facing English leaf p758 (printed page 743). Palm Sunday Matins: Paragraph 64 details the solemn order of Matins on the Great Feast of Palm Sunday (The Entry of our Lord into Jerusalem): Following the nocturnal Litē, the Trisagion Prayers, the second Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God..." (Syntaphentes soi), and the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord", the Dismissal Troparia as at Vespers ("In confirming the common Resurrection..." twice; "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Buried with Thee through Baptism..." once). Psalter Kathismata: Kathisma 2 (Pss. 9–16 LXX) and Kathisma 3 (Pss. 17–23 LXX) followed by the Polyeleos (Kathisma 19, Pss. 134–135 LXX). Sessional Hymns [Kathismata] from the Triodion: after Kathisma 2, "In spirit carrying branches, with cleansed souls let us praise Christ in faith like children..." (Meta kladōn noētōs) and "Thou didst raise Lazarus from the tomb after four days, O Lord..." (Tetraēmeron ēgeiras); after Kathisma 3, "O Christ our Savior, Thou didst shed tears in secret over Thy friend Lazarus lying dead..." (Dakrya proecheas, Sōtēr) and "O ye peoples and nations, rejoice together!..." (Laoi kai ta ethnē synevphranthēte). After the Polyeleos, the festal Sessional Hymn in Tone 4: "He Who sits upon the throne of the Cherubim, for our sake sits upon a foal..." (Ho epi thronou Cheroubim) twice. The First Antiphon of the Hymns of Ascent [Anavathmoi] of Tone 4: "From my youth up many passions have warred against me..." (Ek neotētos mou). The Matins Prokimenon in Tone 4: "Out of the mouths of babes and sucklings hast Thou perfected praise" (Ps. 8:2 LXX) with verse Ps. 8:1 LXX ("O Lord, our Lord, how wonderful is Thy name in all the earth!"). "Let every breath praise the Lord" (Pasa pnoē), and the Matins Gospel pericope: Matthew 21:1–11, 15–17 (The Triumphal Entry into Jerusalem and the Hosanna of the children in the temple). The hymn "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" is omitted; Psalm 50 is read immediately plain (chyma). "Glory...": Idiomelon in Tone 2: "Today Christ entereth the city of Bethany..." (Sēmeron ho Christos eiserchetai en polei Bēthania); "Both now and ever...": the same; then the verse Ps. 50:1 LXX ("Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy"), and the Idiomelon in Tone 6: "Today the grace of the Holy Spirit hath gathered us together...". Canon of Palm Sunday: the renowned acrostic iambic and trochaic masterpiece by Saint Cosmas the Melodist in Tone 4: "The springs of the deep appeared dry..." (Ōphthēsan hai pēgai tēs abyssou) in six Troparia without biblical verses, except "Glory... Both now and ever..." at the final two Troparia. After Ode 3, the Hypakoe: "Praising with branches, first the peoples cried aloud with hymns of praise..." (Meta kladōn hymnēsantes) recited plain. After Ode 6, the Kontakion in Tone 6: "Upborne upon the throne in heaven and upon a foal on earth, O Christ God, Thou didst receive the praise of the Angels and the hymns of the children..." (Tō thronō en ouranō), the Oikos: "Since Thou didst bind Hades, O Immortal One...", the Menologion, and the Memorial of the Triodion. Katavasiai: "The springs of the deep appeared dry...". In place of "More honorable than the Cherubim", Ode 9 of the Canon is chanted in full with its Megalynaria: "Magnify, O my soul, Him Who sat upon a foal...". In place of the Exaposteilarion, "Holy is the Lord our God" is chanted three times in Tone 4. The Superior [Proestos] then solemnly reads the Prayer over the Palms and Branches: "O Lord our God, Who sittest upon the Cherubim...". Immediately the First Choir begins "Let every breath praise the Lord" (Ps. 150:6 LXX) and the Praises [Lauds]. Footnote 36: Violakis describes the ceremonial patriarchal rite in the Great Church of Constantinople during the Praises: the celebrating Hierarchs venerate the festal Icon of the Entry into Jerusalem, and then receive consecrated palms and olive branches from the Ecumenical Patriarch, kissing his right hand, followed by all court dignitaries and officials.
[^439]: Holy Week: Palm Sunday: Divine Liturgy and Sunday Evening Vespers with Compline (§§ 65, 66). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p759 (printed page 744) and facing English leaf p760 (printed page 745). Palm Sunday Praises Concluded: Paragraph 64 concludes Palm Sunday Matins at the Praises [Lauds]: Four festal Idiomela in Tone 4 are chanted: "O Lord, great multitudes spread their garments in the way..." (Pleistos ochlos, Kyrie, estrōnnyon en tē hodō ta himatia autōn), "When Thou wast about to enter the Holy City, O Lord..." (Mellontos sou eisienai, Kyrie, eis tēn hagian Polin), "Come forth, ye peoples, come forth, ye nations..." (Exelthete ethnē, exelthete kai laoi), and "Before Thy voluntary Passion, O Christ God, intending to assure all of the universal Resurrection..." (Pro hex hēmerōn tou hekousiou Pathous sou). "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Six days before the Passover, Jesus came unto Bethany..." (Pro hex hēmerōn tou Pascha). Great Doxology, and the Dismissal Troparion: "Buried with Thee through Baptism, O Christ our God...". Divine Liturgy of Palm Sunday: Paragraph 65 codifies the festive Divine Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom on Palm Sunday: The Festal Antiphons of Palm Sunday are chanted [Antiphon 1: Ps. 114 LXX with refrain "Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us"; Antiphon 2: Ps. 115 LXX with refrain "Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon the foal of an ass, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!"; Antiphon 3: Ps. 117 LXX with the Dismissal Troparion: "In confirming the common Resurrection..."]. Festal Entrance Hymn [Isodikon]: "Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord! God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us: Save us, O Son of God, Who didst sit upon the foal of an ass, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!" (Ps. 117:26–27 LXX). After the Little Entrance, the Dismissal Troparia: "In confirming the common Resurrection..." and "Buried with Thee through Baptism..."; Kontakion: "Upborne upon the throne in heaven and upon a foal on earth, O Christ God...". The Trisagion Hymn ("Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us"). Epistle pericope: Philippians 4:4–9 (Rejoice in the Lord always...); Gospel pericope: John 12:1–18 (The Anointing of Christ's feet by Mary at Bethany, and the Triumphal Entry into Jerusalem). At "Especially [among the all-holy]...", the Heirmos of Ode 9: "God the Lord hath appeared unto us; let us appoint a festival, and rejoicing come, let us magnify Christ with palms and branches..." (Theos Kyrios kai epephanen hēmin). Communion Hymn [Koinōnikon]: "Blessed is He that cometh in the name of the Lord. Alleluia!" (Ps. 117:26 LXX). In place of "We have seen the true Light", the Dismissal Troparion "In confirming the common Resurrection..." is sung; "Blessed be the name of the Lord" (Ps. 112:2 LXX), and the festal Dismissal: "May He Who did deign to sit upon the foal of an ass for our salvation, Christ our true God...". Palm Sunday Evening Vespers and Compline: Paragraph 66 details Sunday evening: At Vespers, after the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), the three Aposticha Idiomela of yesterday are doubled for six: "Rejoice and be glad, O city of Zion!..." (twice), "Today the Savior cometh unto Jerusalem..." (twice), and "O Thou Who sittest upon the Cherubim..." (twice); "Glory...": "Rejoice and be glad..."; "Both now and ever...": "O Thou Who sittest upon the Cherubim...". Little Entrance with the censer, "O Gladsome Light", and the Great Prokimenon of Holy Week in Tone 8: "Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; hear me speedily: attend unto my soul, and deliver it!" (Ps. 68:18–19 LXX) with its appointed verses. Then the three Aposticha of the sequence: "Passing from one divine feast to another divine feast, let us hasten, O believers..." with verse Ps. 122:1 LXX ("Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes..."), "It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God..." (Heb. 10:31) with verse Ps. 122:3 LXX ("Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us..."), and "O evil and adulterous generation, that hath not kept faith with its husband!..."; "Glory...": "Passing from one divine feast..."; "Both now and ever...": "It is a fearful thing to fall into the hands of the living God...". After "Lord, now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace" (Luke 2:29), the Canon of Compline on the Patriarch Joseph the All-Comely is chanted: "Emulating the chaste wisdom of Joseph..." (Iōsēph tēn sōphrosynēn zēlōsantes) with the refrain "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!". After Ode 9, the Heirmos "Childbirth without corruption is alien to mothers..." (Allotrion tōn mēterōn), the Trisagion Prayers, the Dismissal Troparion "Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos..." (Theotoke Parthene) with deep prostrations, and the rest of Small Compline as on other Sundays of Great Lent, inaugurating the austere holy atmosphere of Passion Week (The Bridegroom Services / Nymphios).
[^440]: Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday Matins Opening and Rubrics (§§ 66 Concluded, 67 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p761 (printed page 746) and facing English leaf p762 (printed page 747). Paragraph 66 Concluded: On Palm Sunday evening at the rite of Mutual Forgiveness during Compline, the Stavrotheotokion is chanted: "When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ..." (Sphagēn sou tēn adikon); Dismissal: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee... May the Lord, coming unto His voluntary Passion for our salvation, Christ our true God..." (Erchomenos ho Kyrios epi to hekousion pathos dia tēn hēmōn sōtērian), and: "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". Footnote 37 notes that this exact Dismissal formula is maintained throughout Passion Week until Great and Holy Wednesday at Matins and at the Divine Liturgy. Paragraph 67: Great and Holy Monday at Matins (The Service of the Bridegroom / Nymphios): After the initial blessing: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages", the Superior [Proestos] says: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King, Comforter...", the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest gives the exclamation: "For Thine is the kingdom...", the Reader recites "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "O come, let us worship and fall down before our King and God..." (3 times), and the Royal Psalms: Psalm 19 LXX ("The Lord hear thee in the day of trouble...") and Psalm 20 LXX ("The king shall rejoice in Thy strength, O Lord..."). While these Psalms are being read slowly, the Priest censes the people with the hand-censer [katzion]. After the Trisagion Prayers and "For Thine is the kingdom...", the Troparia: "Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance...", "Glory...": "Thou Who wast voluntarily lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ God...", "Both now and ever...": "O awesome and unashamed protection...". The Dean or presiding Reader [Proistamenos] recites the Six Psalms. After the Great Litany, the Great Alleluia is chanted with its appointed verses from Isaiah 26:9 LXX: "From night until morning my spirit seeketh Thee early, O God, for Thy commandments are a light upon the earth", twice in slow melody and twice in brief melody. Then the solemn Dismissal Troparion of the Bridegroom in Tone 8: "Behold, the Bridegroom cometh in the middle of the night, and blessed is the servant whom He shall find watching..." (Idou ho Nymphios erchetai) three times (twice slowly and once rapidly). At the conclusion of the first time we say: "...through the protection of the Bodiless Hosts, save us!" (Prostasiais tōn Asōmatōn sōson hēmas); Footnote 38 notes that on Great Tuesday this ending is "...through the intercessions of the Forerunner, save us", and on Great Wednesday "...by the power of Thy Cross, save us". At the second time we join the commemoration of the Patron Saint of the temple: "...through the intercessions of Saint [N.] (or: the Saints), save us!"; and at the third time: "...through the Theotokos, have mercy on us!". Then the Kathismata of the Psalter: Footnote 39 records that while the daily Psalter Kathismata are commonly omitted in most parish churches during these Bridegroom Matins services either for the sake of brevity or because five Kathismata are assigned each day, they are read privately in the morning by the Priest; nevertheless it is devoutly to be wished that at least a portion of the five Kathismata be read publicly for the spiritual edification of the faithful, as the Psalter is the basis of church prayer (as noted in the Introductory Chapter [Protheoria] § 3, footnote 3). Following the Psalter, the three appointed Sessional Hymns [Kathismata] of the Triodion are chanted slowly in sequence: the first two once each, and the third twice. Immediately the Priest exclaims: "And that we may be accounted worthy to hear the Holy Gospel...".
[^441]: Holy Week: Great and Holy Monday Matins Concluded (§ 67 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p763 (printed page 748) and facing English leaf p764 (printed page 749). Paragraph 67 Concluded: The Priest reads the Matins Gospel pericope: Matthew 21:18–43 (The Cursing of the Barren Fig Tree and the Parable of the Wicked Vinedressers). After the Gospel, Psalm 50 is read plain; then the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] of Great Monday by Saint Cosmas the Melodist (Odes 1, 8, and 9) in Tone 2 is chanted, with six Troparia in each Ode, followed by the Heirmos chanted slowly as Katavasia. After Ode 1, the Small Litany, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph, while that noble man was seated in a chariot and honored as a king..." (Ho Iakōb ōdyreto), the Ikos: "Let us now add our lamentations to the lamentations of Jacob...", the Menologion from the Menaion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion on the Patriarch Joseph the All-Comely and the Withered Fig Tree. Then Ode 8 with its Heirmos; in place of "Glory...", we say: "We bless the Lord: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit", and before repeating the Heirmos as Katavasia: "We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord". Then the Deacon proclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", and Ode 9 is chanted with its Heirmos. Small Litany and the solemn Exaposteilarion: "I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior, and I have no wedding garment that I may enter therein; enlighten the robe of my soul, O Giver of Light, and save me!" (Ton nymphōna sou blepō, Sōtēr mou) three times. At the Praises [Lauds], 4 verses are set, and the two Idiomela of the Praises are chanted twice each: "O Lord, coming unto Thy voluntary Passion, Thou didst say unto Thine Apostles on the way: Behold, we go up to Jerusalem..." and "O Lord, coming unto Thy Passion, Thou didst establish the hearts of Thy Disciples...". "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 5: "O Lord, coming unto Thy Passion, Thou didst take Thy Disciples aside and comfort them...". Footnote 40 details the patriarchal ceremonial during the Praises: at the singing of "Praise ye" [Aineite], the Ecumenical Patriarch or presiding Hierarch descends from his throne to venerate the holy Icons of the Passion of Christ, blesses the people while the choirs chant "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla etē), and ascends the small side-throne [parathronion]. Small Doxology: "To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God..." read plain. After the litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord", the First Choir begins the Aposticha: "O Lord, the mother of the sons of Zebedee, unable to understand the ineffable mystery of Thy dispensation, requested of Thee the honor of a temporal kingdom for her sons..." with verses from Psalm 89 LXX. Then: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord...", the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: "Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph...", "Lord, have mercy" (12 times); Footnote 41 references footnote 31 regarding the liturgical usage of this response. The Priest: "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God...", the Reader: "Confirm, O God, the holy Orthodox faith...", the Prayer: "O Heavenly King, strengthen our faithful civil authorities...", the three Great Prostrations with the Prayer of Saint Ephrem the Syrian, and the solemn Passion Week Dismissal: "May the Lord, coming unto His voluntary Passion for our salvation, Christ our true God...". Footnote 42 records that this identical order of Matins is observed on Great Tuesday and Great Wednesday, whether celebrated in the morning or in the evening as Bridegroom Matins; if celebrated in the evening, the Psalter Kathismata are postponed to the morning.
[^442]: Holy Week: Great Monday (Hours, Presanctified, Compline) and Great Tuesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 68, 69, 70, 71, 72, 73, 74 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p765 (printed page 750) and facing English leaf p766 (printed page 751). Paragraph 68: Great Monday Morning Hours: Five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 4, 5, 6, 7, and 8]; then the plain Hours (First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth) without singing, with the Dismissal Troparion: "Behold, the Bridegroom cometh..." and the Kontakion: "Jacob lamented the loss of Joseph...". At the Ninth Hour, the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] are chanted plain; following the Prayer: "All-Holy Trinity, Consubstantial Might...", the Dismissal is pronounced, and Vespers begins immediately. Paragraph 69: Great Monday Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts: After the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX) and the Eighteenth Kathisma: "Unto the Lord" (Pss. 119–133 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Ps. 140 LXX), six Stichera are chanted: the six Idiomela of the Praises and the Aposticha sung at Matins. "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "Finding the Egyptian woman another Eve, the serpent hastened through speech to trip up Joseph..." (Deuteran Evan tēn Aigyptian heurōn ho drakōn). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, "O Gladsome Light" (Phōs hilaron), and two Old Testament Readings: Exodus 1:1–20 LXX (The Oppression of the Israelites in Egypt) and Job 1:1–12 LXX (The Righteousness and Trials of Job). Then "Let my prayer arise" (Kateuthynthētō), and immediately: "Wisdom! Stand upright! Let us hear the Holy Gospel", and the Gospel pericope is proclaimed: Matthew 24:3–35 (The Discourse on the Mount of Olives and the Signs of the End of the Age); then the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts continues in its usual solemn order. Paragraph 70: Great Monday Evening Compline: Great Compline is read in accordance with the established Lenten rubrics; after the Doxology: "Glory to God in the highest...", the Second Choir begins the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] of Great Monday evening; after the Heirmos of Ode 9: "Him that was prefigured upon the mountain..." (Ton prodēlothenta en orei), the Trisagion Prayers, "O Lord of hosts, be with us..." (Kyrie tōn dynameōn), and the remaining prayers of Great Compline. At the rite of Mutual Forgiveness, the Stavrotheotokion is chanted: "When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ, she lamented: Woe is me, my Child!..." (Sphagēn sou tēn adikon Christe). Paragraph 71: Great and Holy Tuesday Matins: The service of Matins on Great Tuesday is celebrated unalterably in the exact manner set forth in Paragraph 67 for Great Monday. Paragraph 72: Great Tuesday Morning Hours: Before the initial blessing: "Blessed is our God...", the next five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 9, 10, 11, 12, and 13], and the plain Hours are read as yesterday with the Dismissal Troparion: "Behold, the Bridegroom cometh..." and the Kontakion of Great Tuesday: "Having in mind the hour of the end, O my soul, and fearing the cutting down of the fig tree..." (Tēn hōran, psychē, tou telous ennoēsasa). At the Ninth Hour the Beatitudes are chanted; following the Prayer: "All-Holy Trinity...", the Dismissal is given, and Vespers begins. Paragraph 73: Great Tuesday Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts: After Psalm 103 LXX and Kathisma 18 ("Unto the Lord"), at "Lord, I have cried", the six Idiomela of the Praises and Aposticha sung at Matins are chanted. "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 7: "Behold, the Master entrusteth thee with the talent, O my soul; receive the gift with fear..." (Idou soi to talanton ho Despotēs encheirizei). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, "O Gladsome Light", and two Old Testament Readings: Exodus 2:5–10 LXX (The Finding of Moses by Pharaoh's Daughter) and Job 1:13–22 LXX (Job Loses His Children and Wealth and Blesses the Name of the Lord). Then "Let my prayer arise", the Gospel pericope: Matthew 24:36–26:2 (The Parables of the Faithful and Unfaithful Servants, the Ten Virgins, the Talents, the Sheep and the Goats, and the Plot to Kill Jesus), and the rest of the Presanctified Liturgy. Paragraph 74: Great Tuesday Evening Compline: Great Compline begins in the evening; after the Doxology...
[^443]: Holy Week: Great Tuesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Wednesday (Matins, Hours, Presanctified, Compline Opening) (§§ 74 Concluded, 75, 76, 77 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p767 (printed page 752) and facing English leaf p768 (printed page 753). Paragraph 74 Concluded: Great Tuesday Great Compline concludes: After the Doxology: "Glory to God in the highest...", the First Choir begins the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] of Great Tuesday evening (Odes 3, 8, and 9) in Tone 2 by Saint Cosmas the Melodist; after the Heirmos of Ode 9: "With faith let us magnify her that supernaturally conceived in the flesh..." (Tēn hyperphyōs sarki), the Trisagion Prayers, "O Lord of hosts, be with us...", and the conclusion of Great Compline. At the rite of Mutual Forgiveness, the Stavrotheotokion is chanted: "When the Virgin beheld Thy unjust slaughter, O Christ...". Paragraph 75: Great and Holy Wednesday Matins and Hours: Matins is chanted unalterably according to the rubrics of Paragraph 67. Footnote 43 records that at the Aposticha of Matins on this night, the Second Choir sings slowly and with elaborate melody the renowned Doxastikon in Tone 8: "O Lord, the woman who had fallen into many sins, having recognized Thy divinity, O Lord, taking upon herself the office of a myrrh-bearer, bringeth myrrh with lamentations unto Thee before Thy burial..." (Kyrie, hē en pollais hamartiais), universally attributed to the Nun Kassiani, though an ancient Jerusalem manuscript attributes it to Patriarch Photios. In the morning, the next five Kathismata of the Psalter are read [Kathismata 14, 15, 16, 19, and 20], thereby completing the entire Psalter (Kathisma 17 is omitted because it is reserved for Great Saturday Matins, and Kathisma 18 is omitted because it is read at every Presanctified Liturgy). The plain Hours are read as on the previous days; after the Prayer: "All-Holy Trinity...", the Dismissal is given, and Vespers begins. Paragraph 76: Great Wednesday Vespers with the Presanctified Liturgy: After Psalm 103 LXX and Kathisma 18 ("Unto the Lord"), at "Lord, I have cried", 10 verses are set, and all the Stichera sung at Matins at the Praises and Aposticha are chanted. "Glory... Both now and ever...": the Doxastikon of the Nun Kassiani: "O Lord, the woman who had fallen into many sins...". Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, "O Gladsome Light", and two Old Testament Readings: Exodus 2:11–23 LXX (Moses Slays the Egyptian and Flees to Midian) and Job 2:1–10 LXX (Job's Affliction with Sores and His Integrity). Then "Let my prayer arise", and the Gospel pericope: Matthew 26:6–16 (The Anointing of Jesus at the House of Simon the Leper and Judas Bargains to Betray Him). The Presanctified Liturgy is completed, this being the final Presanctified Liturgy of Great Lent. Paragraph 77: Great Wednesday Evening Small Compline: In the evening, Small Compline is read rather than Great Compline, marking the transition away from the Great Lenten cycle. After "It is truly meet" (Axion estin), the Three-Ode Canon [Triodion] of Great Wednesday evening in Tone 4: "A guest-room furnished and ready..." (Anōgeōn estrōmenon) is chanted with the verse: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee!". After Ode 9, the Heirmos: "The childbirth of a conception without seed..." (Asporou syllēpseōs)...
[^444]: Holy Week: Great Wednesday (Compline Concluded) and Great Thursday (Matins, Vespers Opening) (§§ 77 Concluded, 78, 79 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p769 (printed page 754) and facing English leaf p770 (printed page 755). Paragraph 77 Concluded: Great Wednesday Small Compline concludes: After Ode 9 of the Triodion Canon, the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion in Tone 4: "Having transgressed more than the harlot, O Good One, I have in no wise brought forth a flood of tears for Thee; but falling down in silence before Thee, I kiss Thy most pure feet with love..." (Hyper tēn pornēn, Agathe), "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", the nocturnal prayer: "O spotless, undefiled, incorrupt, unblemished, pure Virgin, Lady Bride of God..." (Aspile, amolynte), etc., and the Dismissal. Paragraph 78: Great and Holy Thursday at Matins (The Mystical Supper): After the Six Psalms and the Great Litany, the Alleluia is chanted with its verses in Tone 8 four times, followed by the renowned Dismissal Troparion in Tone 8: "When the glorious Disciples were enlightened at the Supper during the washing of the feet, then the ungodly Judas was darkened by the disease of avarice; and to lawless judges he delivered Thee, the righteous Judge. Behold his goods, O lover of money, who for the sake thereof hanged himself! Flee from the insatiable soul that dared such things against the Master! O Lord, Who art good towards all, glory to Thee!" (Hote hoi endoxoi Mathētai) three times. Directly the Matins Gospel pericope is read: Luke 22:1–39 (The Plot of the Chief Priests, the Preparation of the Passover, the Institution of the Holy Eucharist, the Dispute about Greatness, and Christ's Agony in Gethsemane). Then Psalm 50 is read, and the acrostic Canon of Great Thursday by Saint Cosmas the Melodist in Tone 6: "The red sea was severed by the divine rod..." (Tomē tmēgetai) is chanted without biblical verses, with its Katavasia at the end of each Ode. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymns [Kathismata]: "He Who created the lakes, the springs and the seas, desiring to teach us the highest humility, girded Himself with a towel and washed the feet of the Disciples...", "Glory...": "Having humbled Thyself through compassion, Thou didst wash the feet of Thy Disciples...", "Both now and ever...": "Eating, O Master, with Thy Disciples, Thou didst mystically reveal Thy holy slaughter...". After Ode 6, the Kontakion in Tone 2: "Having taken the Bread into his hands, the traitor extends them secretly to receive the price of Him Who fashioned man with His own hands; and Judas remained incorrigible, having dipped his hand into the dish with Christ...", the Ikos, the Menologion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion on the Mystical Supper, the Washing of the Disciples' Feet, the Agony in the Garden, and the Betrayal of Judas. After the Heirmos of Ode 8 is chanted as Katavasia, the Deacon exclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", and Ode 9 is chanted with its Heirmos: "Come, O believers, let us enjoy the hospitality and immortal table of the Master in the upper chamber..." (Xenias Despotikēs). Exaposteilarion: "I see Thy bridal chamber adorned, O my Savior..." three times. At the Praises [Lauds], four Stichera are chanted: "The assembly of the Jews hastens together to deliver the Creator and Fashioner of all unto Pilate...", "Judas the transgressor, having dipped his hand into the dish with Thee at supper...", "Judas, being a treacherous servant, deceitfully devised betrayal...", and "Judas, servant and deceiver, disciple and plotter, friend and devil, was made manifest from his deeds...". "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 2: "The Lamb whom Isaiah proclaimed comes of His own will to the slaughter..." (Hon ekēryxen Amnon). The Small Doxology: "To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God..." read plain. The litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord", and the four Aposticha with verses from Psalm 40 LXX: "Today the evil Sanhedrin has assembled together against Christ..." with verse: "He that eats of my bread has lifted up his heel against me" (Ps. 40:9 LXX); "Today Judas hides his treachery under the pretense of love for the poor..." with verse: "When he went abroad, he spoke of the same" (Ps. 40:6 LXX); "Thy deceitful manner is revealed unto the end..." with verse: "An evil word have they poured out against me" (Ps. 40:8 LXX); and "O believers, let none of us who are uninitiated draw near to the Master's table...". "Glory...": "Thy deceitful manner..."; "Both now and ever...": "Initiating Thy Disciples, O Lord, Thou didst teach them, saying: O my friends, see that no fear separate you from Me...". Then: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord...", the Trisagion Prayers, the Troparion of the Prophecy: "Thou Who didst endure slaps for the human race, O Christ God, deliver us from corruption and save our souls", and the Old Testament Prophecy: Jeremiah 11:18–12:5, 9–11, 14–15 LXX (The Prophecy of the Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter). The Augmented Litany [Ektenis], and the Dismissal. Paragraph 79: Great Thursday Vespers with the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great: At Vespers, after Psalm 103 LXX, at "Lord, I have cried", five Idiomela of the Praises are chanted, repeating the first one for six. "Glory... Both now and ever...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Judas is truly the offspring of vipers, who ate the manna in the wilderness and murmured against the Nourisher..." (Gennēma echidnōn). Little Entrance with the Holy Gospel, "O Gladsome Light", and Old Testament Readings from Exodus 19:10–19 LXX, Job...
[^445]: Great and Holy Thursday: Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great Concluded and Service of the Holy Passions Opening (§§ 79 Concluded, 80 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p771 (printed page 756) and facing English leaf p772 (printed page 757). Paragraph 79 Concluded: Great Thursday Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great concludes: Following the Old Testament readings (Exodus 19:10–19 LXX, Job 38:1–21; 42:1–5 LXX, and Isaiah 50:4–11 LXX), the Small Litany is proclaimed, the Trisagion Hymn is chanted, and the Epistle (1 Corinthians 11:23–32: The Institution of the Holy Eucharist) and composite Passion Gospel (Matthew 26:2–20; John 13:3–17; Matthew 26:21–39; Luke 22:43–45; Matthew 26:40–27:2: The Composite Passion Gospel of the Mystical Supper) are proclaimed. Throughout this Liturgy, in place of the Cherubic Hymn, the Communion Hymn, and "We have seen the true light", the Troparion in Tone 6 is chanted: "Of Thy Mystical Supper, O Son of God, receive me today as a communicant; for I will not speak of the Mystery to Thine enemies, neither will I give Thee a kiss as did Judas, but like the thief do I confess Thee: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom!" (Tou deipnou sou tou mystikou). After "Blessed be the name of the Lord...", the solemn Passion Dismissal is given: "Glory to Thee, our God, our hope, glory to Thee. May He Who out of exceeding goodness showed humility to be the most excellent way when He washed the feet of the Disciples, and condescended unto the Cross and burial for our sake, Christ our true God...", and "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". Footnote 44 directs the reader to Appendix [Parartima] IV for the extensive ceremonial rubrics of the Service of the Holy Passions as celebrated in the Patriarchate of Constantinople. Paragraph 80: Great and Holy Thursday Evening (The Service of the Twelve Gospels of the Holy Passions): In the evening at the First Hour of the night (7:00 p.m.), the Priest gives the initial blessing, the Superior [Proestos] says "Glory to Thee, our God...", "O Heavenly King", the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest "For Thine is the kingdom...", the Reader "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "O come, let us worship" (3 times), and the Royal Psalms: Psalm 19 LXX ("The Lord hear Thee in the day of trouble...") and Psalm 20 LXX ("The king shall rejoice in Thy strength, O Lord..."). While these are read slowly, the Priest censes the temple and people with the hand-censer [katzion]. Trisagion Prayers, Troparia ("Save, O Lord, Thy people..."), and the Superior recites the Six Psalms. After the Great Litany, the brief Alleluia is chanted four times with its verses in Tone 8, followed by the Dismissal Troparion of Great Thursday in Tone 8: "When the glorious Disciples were enlightened at the Supper during the washing of the feet, then the ungodly Judas was darkened by the disease of avarice..." (Hote hoi endoxoi mathētai) twice slowly and once rapidly. The Archbishop or presiding Priest proclaims the First Gospel of the Testament (John 13:31–18:1: The Farewell Discourse of Christ to His Disciples). At the conclusion, the Archbishop blesses the congregation with the three-stemmed candlestick [trikirion] while the choirs sing "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla etē), and ascends the episcopal throne. The choirs then alternate in chanting the Antiphons, beginning with Antiphon 1 in Tone 8: "The rulers of the peoples took counsel together against the Lord and against His Anointed" (Archontes laōn) through Antiphon 3, concluding with the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 7: "Sustaining the disciples at the supper, Thou didst say: One of you will betray Me..." (En tō deipnō tous mathētas).
[^446]: Great and Holy Thursday Evening: Service of the Holy Passions Continued (Gospels II–IX, Antiphons IV–XV, Procession of the Holy Cross, Beatitudes, and Canon) (§ 80 Continued). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p773 (printed page 758) and facing English leaf p774 (printed page 759). Paragraph 80 Continued: The Second Gospel is proclaimed: John 18:1–28 (Jesus in Gethsemane, His Arrest, Interrogation before Annas, and Peter's Denial). At its conclusion, the Second Choir sings the universal Passion response: "Glory to Thy forbearance, O Lord, glory to Thee!" (Doxa tē makrothymia sou Kyrie doxa soi), which is repeated after every subsequent Gospel reading throughout the service. The Fourth Antiphon in Tone 5 is begun, continuing through Antiphons 5 and 6, concluding with the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 7: "What reason has made thee, O Judas, a traitor of the Savior? Did He cast thee off from the choir of the Apostles? Did He deprive thee of the gift of healings?..." (Poios se tropos Iouda). The Third Gospel is proclaimed: Matthew 26:57–75 (Jesus before Caiaphas, the False Witnesses, the Sanhedrin's Condemnation, and Peter Weeps Bitterly). The choirs chant Antiphons 7, 8, and 9, concluding with the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 8: "O how did Judas, who was once Thy disciple, devise betrayal against Thee?..." (Ō pōs Ioudas ho pote sou mathētēs). The Fourth Gospel is proclaimed: John 18:28–19:16 (Jesus before Pilate, the Crowning with Thorns, the Scourging, Ecce Homo, and the Sentence of Crucifixion). Antiphons 10, 11, and 12 are chanted, concluding with the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 8: "When Thou, O God, didst stand before Caiaphas, and wast delivered unto Pilate, the Judge of all: the powers of heaven shook with fear..."
