(Hote parestēs tō Kaiapha ho Theos). The Fifth Gospel is proclaimed: Matthew 27:3–32 (The Remorse and Death of Judas, Jesus before Pilate, Barabbas, and Simon of Cyrene Bears the Cross). The First Choir begins Antiphon 13 in Tone 6, followed by Antiphon 14. At the Fifteenth Antiphon, the solemn rite of the Procession of the Holy Cross (exodos tou Stavrou) is enacted according to universal Greek usage: the Priest, vested in phelonion and carrying the large carved wooden Crucifix upon his shoulder, preceded by processional fans [rhipidia], candles, and the hand-censer, exits the Sanctuary through the northern door and processes around the nave of the temple, chanting in a solemn recitative intonation: "Today is hung upon the Tree, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters..." (Sēmeron krematai epi xylou) three times, the choir repeating the verse after him while the faithful kneel in adoration; the Crucifix is then planted firmly in the center of the temple on the Tetrapod. The Antiphons conclude with the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 4: "Thou hast ransomed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood..." (Exēgorasas hēmas). The Sixth Gospel is proclaimed: Mark 15:16–32 (The Crucifixion at Golgotha and the Mocking by the Rulers). The First Choir chants the Beatitudes [Makarismoi] interspersed with Passion Troparia, followed by the Prokimenon in Tone 4 chanted three times: "They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots" (Ps. 21:18 LXX / Diemarisanto ta himatia mou heautois). The Seventh Gospel is proclaimed: Matthew 27:33–54 (The Crucifixion, the Darkness, the Rending of the Temple Veil, the Resuscitation of the Saints, and the Centurion's Confession). Psalm 50 LXX is recited plain, followed immediately by the Eighth Gospel: Luke 23:32–49 (The Good Thief and the Repentant Confession: "Remember me, Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom"). The First Choir begins the solemn Triodion Canon of Great Friday in Tone 6 by Saint Cosmas the Melodist: "Early in the morning my soul rises unto Thee, O Word of God..." (Pros se orthrizō), with four Troparia for each Ode without biblical verses, the Heirmos being repeated slowly at the end of each Ode as Katavasia. After Ode 5, the Small Litany, the Kontakion in Tone 8: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou dost endure the Cross, Thou art my Son and my God!" (Ton di' hēmas stavrōthenta), the Ikos, the Menologion, and the Memorial [Hypomnema] of the Triodion on the Holy and Salvific Passions. Ode 9 is chanted with the exclamation "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", followed by the Small Litany and the solemn Exaposteilarion in Tone 3: "The wise thief in a single hour Thou didst account worthy of Paradise, O Lord; enlighten me also by the wood of the Cross, and save me!" (Ton lēstēn authēmeron) three times. The Ninth Gospel is proclaimed: John 19:25–37 (Christ Entrusts His Mother to the Beloved Disciple, "It Is Finished", and the Piercing of His Sacred Side). At the Praises [Lauds], three Idiomela are chanted with four verses from Psalm 150 LXX, repeating the first: "Two evil deeds has My first-born son Israel committed..." (Ps. 150:2 LXX) and "Every member of Thy holy flesh endured dishonor for our sake..." (Ps. 150:4 LXX).
[^447]: Great and Holy Thursday Evening: Service of the Holy Passions Concluded (Gospels X–XII, Praises Concluded, Aposticha, and Dismissal) (§ 80 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p775 (printed page 760) and facing English leaf p776 (printed page 761). Paragraph 80 Concluded: The service of the Holy Passions concludes: At the Praises, after the fourth verse from Psalm 150:5–6 LXX ("Praise Him with sounding cymbals..."), the third Idiomelon is chanted: "Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, all creation trembled..." (Stavrōthenta se theōrousa). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "They stripped Me of My garments and clothed Me in a scarlet robe..." (Exedysan me ta himatia mou); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: "I gave My back to scourgings, and My cheeks to blows, and I turned not My face from the shame of spittings..." (Ton nōton mou edōka eis mastigas). The Tenth Gospel is proclaimed: Mark 15:43–47 (Joseph of Arimathea Obtains the Body of Jesus from Pilate, Wraps It in Linen, and Lays It in a Tomb Hewn from the Rock). Immediately the Small Doxology: "To Thee belongs glory, O Lord our God, and to Thee we send up glory..." (Soi doxa prepei) is read plain. The litany: "Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord" (Plērōsōmen tēn heōthinēn deēsin), followed directly by the Eleventh Gospel: John 19:38–42 (Joseph of Arimathea and Nicodemus Anoint the Body with Myrrh and Aloes and Lay Jesus in the New Garden Tomb). Immediately the Aposticha are chanted with their appointed Psalm verses: 1. "All creation was altered with fear when it beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ..." (Pasa hē ktisis ēlloiouto phobō); Verse: "They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots" (Ps. 21:18 LXX); 2. "Why does the impious and lawless people imagine vain things?..." (Laos dyssebēs kai paranomos); Verse: "They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink" (Ps. 68:21 LXX); 3. "Today, seeing Thee, O Word, hanging upon the Cross, the blameless Virgin wept with maternal sorrow..." (Sēmeron se theōrousa hē amemptos Parthenos); Verse: "God is our King before the ages; He has worked salvation in the midst of the earth" (Ps. 73:12 LXX); 4. "Beholding Thee, the Creator and God of all, lifted up upon the Tree..." (Epi xylou blepousa kremamenon). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 8: "O Lord, when Thou didst ascend the Cross, fear and trembling fell upon creation..."; "Both now and ever...": "The reed of denial has already been dipped by the unjust judges...". The Twelfth Gospel is proclaimed by the Deacon from the elevated ambo: Matthew 27:62–66 (The Chief Priests and Pharisees Seal the Tomb and Post the Roman Guard). At the conclusion of the reading, the Archbishop blesses the Deacon, and the choirs sing "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla etē). The Priest or Superior recites: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord, and to sing unto Thy name, O Most High" (Ps. 91:1 LXX), the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, and the Chanter sings the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 4: "Thou hast ransomed us from the curse of the Law by Thy precious Blood; having been nailed to the Cross and pierced with the spear, Thou didst pour forth immortality upon mankind. O our Savior, glory to Thee!" (Exēgorasas hēmas) three times. The Deacon intones the Augmented Litany: "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy...", commemorating the hierarchical authorities and all Orthodox Christians. After the exclamation "For Thou art a merciful and loving God...", the Deacon proclaims "Wisdom!", the Priest "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God...", and the Archbishop or Superior recites the prayer of confirmation: "Confirm, O Lord God, the holy Orthodox faith of pious and Orthodox Christians unto ages of ages" (Stereōsai Kyrios ho Theos). The Priest pronounces the solemn Passion Dismissal: "Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee. May Christ our true God, Who endured spittings, and scourgings, buffetings, and the Cross and Death for the salvation of the world, through the intercessions of His all-pure and blameless holy Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind" (Doxa soi ho Theos hē elpis hēmōn doxa soi; ho emptysmous kai mastigas...), and the final benediction: "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...".
[^448]: Great and Holy Friday: The Royal Hours (First and Third Hours) (§ 81). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p777 (printed page 762) and facing English leaf p778 (printed page 763). Paragraph 81: Great and Holy Friday Morning: The Royal Hours (First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth): At the First Hour of the day (around 7:00 a.m.), the Priest gives the initial blessing: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages", the Superior [Proestos] recites "O Heavenly King", the Reader the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest "For Thine is the kingdom...", the Reader "Lord, have mercy" (12 times), "Glory... Both now and ever...", "O come, let us worship" (3 times), and the three appointed Psalms of the First Hour: Psalm 5 LXX ("Give ear to my words, O Lord..."), Psalm 2 LXX ("Why do the nations rage..."), and Psalm 21 LXX ("O God, my God, attend to me; why hast Thou forsaken me?..."). Then "Glory...": Troparion in Tone 1: "When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, the tyranny was destroyed, and the power of the enemy was trampled underfoot; for neither an angel nor a man, but the Lord Himself saved us. Glory to Thee!" (Stavrōthentos sou Christe); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "What shall we call thee, O full of grace?..." (Ti se kalesōmen). The First Choir chants the first Idiomelon in Tone 8: "Today the veil of the temple is rent in twain for the rebuke of the lawless, and the sun hides its rays beholding the Master crucified" (Sēmeron tou naou to katapetasma); the Second Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 8: "As a sheep led to the slaughter wast Thou brought, O Christ King..." (Hōs probaton epi sphagēn) with verse from Psalm 2:1 LXX ("Why do the nations rage?"); the First Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Unto the transgressors who seized Thee, O Christ, Thou didst cry aloud: What harm did I do unto you, or wherein did I provoke you?..." (Tois syllabousi se paranomois). The Old Testament Prophecy is proclaimed: Zechariah 11:10–13 LXX (The Thirty Pieces of Silver Weighed as the Price of the Shepherd and Cast into the Furnace); the Epistle: Galatians 6:14–18 (Glorying only in the Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, through Whom the World is Crucified unto Me); and the Gospel: Matthew 27:1–56 (The Betrayal, the Trial before Pilate, the Mocking, the Crucifixion, the Death of Jesus, and the Graves Opened). The Reader recites: "Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me..." (Ps. 118:133 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion of Great Friday in Tone 8: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us; for Mary beheld Him upon the Tree and said: Even though Thou dost endure the Cross, Thou art my Son and my God!" (Ton di' hēmas stavrōthenta), "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", and the Superior says the prayer of Saint Basil the Great: "O Christ, the True Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that cometh into the world..." (Christe to phōs). Footnote 45 notes that during this Hour, as well as during the Third and Sixth Hours, one of the deacons according to their order of seniority, vested in his customary monastic mandya, censes the temple with the hand-censer [katzion]. The Third Hour follows immediately: Beginning with the Trisagion Prayers, the three Psalms of the Third Hour are read: Psalm 34 LXX ("Judge Thou, O Lord, them that injure me..."), Psalm 108 LXX ("O God, pass not over my praise in silence..."), and Psalm 50 LXX ("Have mercy on me, O God..."). "Glory...": Troparion in Tone 6: "O Lord, the Jews condemned Thee to death, the Life of all..." (Kyrie, katekrinan se Ioudaioi thanatō); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "O Theotokos, thou art the true vine that blossomed for us the fruit of life...". The Second Choir chants the first Idiomelon in Tone 6: "Through fear of the Jews, Peter Thy friend and companion denied Thee, O Lord..." (Dia ton phobon tōn Ioudaiōn); the First Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 6: "Before Thy precious Cross, when the soldiers mocked Thee, O Lord, the angelic hosts were struck with dread..." (Pro tou timiou sou Stavrou) with verse from Psalm 5:1 LXX ("Give ear to my words, O Lord; understand my cry"); the Second Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Drawn upon the Cross, Thou didst cry aloud, O Lord: For which deed do the Jews wish to stone Me? Because I healed their paralytics?..." (Helkomenos epi Stavrou). The Old Testament Prophecy: Isaiah 50:4–11 LXX (The Suffering Servant: "I gave my back to scourges, and my cheeks to blows"); the Epistle: Romans 5:6–10 (While We Were Still Helpless, Christ Died for the Ungodly); and the Gospel: Mark 15:16–41 (The Soldiers Mock Christ in the Praetorium, the Crucifixion at the Third Hour, the Inscription of the Accusation, and the Death of Christ). The Reader: "Blessed is the Lord God, blessed is the Lord day by day...", the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "Thou Who at all times...", the Prayer of Saint Mardarios: "O Master God, Father Almighty, Lord, Only-Begotten Son Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit, one Divinity, one Power, have mercy on me a sinner...", and the Sixth Hour begins.
[^449]: Great and Holy Friday: The Royal Hours (Sixth and Ninth Hours) (§§ 82, 83). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p779 (printed page 764) and facing English leaf p780 (printed page 765). Paragraph 82: The Royal Sixth Hour: Celebrated with the three appointed Psalms: Psalm 53 LXX ("Save me, O God, by Thy name..."), Psalm 139 LXX ("Deliver me, O Lord, from the evil man..."), and Psalm 90 LXX ("He that dwelleth in the help of the Most High..."). "Glory...": Troparion in Tone 2: "Thou hast worked salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God; Thou didst stretch out Thy most pure hands upon the Cross; Thou didst gather all the nations that cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, glory to Thee!" (Eirgasō sōtērian en mesō tēs gēs); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "Seeing that we have no boldness because of our manifold sins...". The First Choir chants the first Idiomelon in Tone 8: "Thus saith the Lord unto the Jews: My people, what have I done unto you, or wherein have I grieved you? Your blind have I given sight, your lepers have I cleansed, the man lying on his pallet have I restored. My people, what have I done unto you, and wherewith have you repaid Me? Instead of manna, gall; instead of water, vinegar; instead of loving Me, you have nailed Me to the Cross!..." (Tade legei Kyrios pros Ioudaious); the Second Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 8: "O lawgivers of Israel, Jews and Pharisees, the choir of the Apostles cries aloud unto you: Behold the Temple which you destroyed; behold the Lamb Whom you crucified and delivered unto the tomb!..." (Hoi nomothetai Israēl) with verse from Psalm 68:21 LXX ("They gave Me gall for My food, and for My thirst they gave Me vinegar to drink"); the First Choir chants "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Come, O Christ-bearing peoples, let us behold what counsel Judas the traitor took with the lawless priests against our Savior..." (Deute christophoroi laoi). The Old Testament Prophecy: Isaiah 52:13–54:1 LXX (The Fourth Servant Song: "He was wounded for our iniquities, and bruised for our transgressions; the chastisement of our peace was upon Him, and by His stripes we are healed"); the Epistle: Hebrews 2:11–18 (Christ Partook of Flesh and Blood that through Death He Might Destroy the Devil); and the Gospel: Luke 23:32–49 (The Crucifixion between Two Malefactors, "Father, forgive them", the Good Thief Confesses Christ, the Sun Darkened at the Sixth Hour, and Jesus Cries with a Loud Voice and Breathes His Last). The Reader recites: "Let Thy tender mercies speedily prevent us, O Lord, for we are brought very low..." (Ps. 78:8 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "Thou Who at all times...", the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: "O God and Lord of powers, and Maker of all creation, Who through the compassion of Thine inscrutable mercy didst send down Thine Only-Begotten Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, for the salvation of our race...", and the Ninth Hour begins. Paragraph 83: The Royal Ninth Hour: Celebrated with the three appointed Psalms: Psalm 68 LXX ("Save me, O God, for the waters are come in even unto my soul..."), Psalm 69 LXX ("O God, draw nigh unto my help; Lord, make haste to help me..."), and Psalm 85 LXX ("Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me, for I am poor and needy..."). "Glory...": Troparion in Tone 8: "When the thief beheld the Author of life hanging upon the Cross, he said: If Thou wert not God incarnate Who art crucified with us, the sun would not have hidden its rays, nor would the earth have shaken with trembling. But O Thou Who endurest all things, remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom!" (Horōn ho lēstēs ton archēgon tēs zōēs); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion: "O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst despoil death and didst manifest the Resurrection as God...". The Second Choir chants the first Idiomelon in Tone 7: "A marvelous and fearful wonder it was to behold today: the Creator of heaven and earth hangs upon the Tree; He Who suspended the earth upon the waters is transfixed with nails; the sun hides its rays at the sight of the Master dishonored..." (Thambos ēn katidein ton ouranou kai gēs Poiētēn); the First Choir chants the second Idiomelon in Tone 2: "When the transgressors nailed the Lord of glory upon the Cross, they mocked Him and said: If Thou art the Son of God, save Thyself and come down from the Cross!..." (Hote se stavrō prosēlōsan paranomoi) with verse from Psalm 21:18 LXX ("They divided My garments among them, and for My vesture did they cast lots"); the Second Choir takes up the conclusion: "...Together with the Father and the Holy Spirit!"; the First Choir chants "Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit"; the Second Choir chants "Both now and ever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". At this point, the Canonarch advances to the center of the temple before the Holy Cross and intones in the solemn recitative manner of the Epistle the renowned Idiomelon: "Today is hung upon the Tree, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters..." (Sēmeron krematai epi xylou) three times, along with its verses: "A crown of thorns crowns Him Who is the King of angels. He is wrapped in false purple, Who wrapped the heavens with clouds. He received buffetings, Who freed Adam in the Jordan. He was transfixed with nails, Who is the Bridegroom of the Church. He was pierced with a spear, Who is the Son of the Virgin. We worship Thy Passion, O Christ! (3 times). Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!", after which the same Sticheron is chanted antiphonaally by both choirs verse by verse. The Old Testament Prophecy: Jeremiah 11:18–12:5, 9–11, 14–15 LXX (The Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter: "I was like an innocent lamb led to the slaughter; I knew not that they devised an evil device against me"); the Epistle: Hebrews 10:19–31 (Having Boldness to Enter into the Holiest by the Blood of Jesus, by a New and Living Way Which He Dedicated for Us through the Veil, that is to say, His Flesh); and the Gospel: John 19:23–37 (The Soldiers Part His Garments, Jesus Commends His Mother to the Beloved Disciple, He Receives the Vinegar and Gives Up the Ghost, the Soldiers Break Not His Legs, and One Pierces His Side with a Spear). The Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), "Thou Who at all times...", the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who hast patiently endured our transgressions, and hast brought us even unto this present hour, in which, hanging upon the life-creating Tree, Thou didst open the way into Paradise unto the penitent thief, and by death didst destroy death...", and the Dismissal is given, concluding the solemn Royal Hours of Great and Holy Friday.
[^450]: Great and Holy Friday: Vespers of the Deposition from the Cross Opening (The Royal Ninth Hour Concluded and Vespers of Apokathelosis Opening) (§§ 83 Concluded, 84 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p781 (printed page 766) and facing English leaf p782 (printed page 767). Concluding rubrics of the Royal Ninth Hour: The choirs conclude the antiphonal chanting of the solemn Passion Sticheron: "Today is hung upon the Tree, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters" (Semeron krematai epi xylou), finishing with: "Show us also Thy glorious Resurrection!" (Deixon hemin kai ten endoxon sou Anastasin). The Old Testament Prophecy is proclaimed: Jeremiah 11:18–12:4, 9–10, 14–15 LXX (The Innocent Lamb Led to the Slaughter: "I was like an innocent lamb led to the slaughter; I knew not that they devised an evil device against me"); the Epistle: Hebrews 10:19–31 (A New and Living Way Opened through the Veil of His Flesh); and the Gospel: John 19:23–37 (The Parting of the Garments, "It Is Finished", and the Piercing of His Sacred Side). The Reader recites: "Deliver us not up utterly, for Thy holy Name's sake, and make not Thy covenant void..." (Me de paradoes hemas eis telos, Dan. 3:34 LXX), the Trisagion Prayers, the Kontakion of Great Friday in Tone 8: "Come, let us all hymn Him Who was crucified for us...", "Lord, have mercy" (40 times), the Prayer of the Hours: "Thou Who at all times and at every hour...", the prayer of Saint Basil the Great: "O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who hast patiently endured our transgressions...", and the Dismissal is given, concluding the Royal Hours. Paragraph 84: Great and Holy Friday: Vespers of the Taking Down of the Body of Christ from the Cross (Apokathelosis): After the Introductory Psalm (Psalm 103 LXX), at "Lord, I have cried" (Kyrie ekekraxa), the five Idiomela of the Aposticha are chanted for six verses: 1. "All creation was altered with fear when it beheld Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ..." (Pasa he ktisis elloiouto phobo) (twice); 2. "Why does the impious and lawless people imagine vain things?..." (Laos dyssebes kai paranomos); 3. "Today, seeing Thee, O Word, hanging upon the Cross, the blameless Virgin wept..." (Semeron se theorousa he amemptos Parthenos); 4. "Beholding Thee, the Creator and God of all, lifted up upon the Tree..." (Epi xylou blepousa kremamenon); 5. "Today the Master of creation stands before Pilate..." (Semeron ho Despotes tes ktiseos). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "O how did the lawless assembly condemn the King of creation to death!..." (O pos he paranomos synagoge); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 6: "A fearful and marvelous mystery is seen accomplished today: He Who is untouchable is held fast..." (Phoberon kai paradoxon mysterion). The Little Entrance is made with the Holy Gospel Book; "O Gladsome Light" (Phos hilaron) is chanted; and immediately the three Old Testament readings are proclaimed: Exodus 33:11–23 LXX (The Lord Speaks with Moses Face to Face, and Moses Beholds the Glory of God from the Cleft of the Rock); Job 42:12–17 LXX (The Lord Blesses the Latter End of Job More than His Beginning); and Isaiah 52:13–54:1 LXX (The Fourth Servant Song: "Behold, My Servant Shall Prosper"). The Epistle: 1 Corinthians 1:18–31; 2:1–2 (The Word of the Cross is Foolishness to Those Who Perish, but to Us Who Are Saved It Is the Power of God); and the Gospel: Matthew 27:1–38; Luke 23:39–43; Matthew 27:39–54; John 19:31–37; Matthew 27:55–61 (The Composite Passion Gospel of the Crucifixion, the Death, and the Deposition of the Lord) read from the elevated ambo. At the words: "Now when evening was come, there came a rich man from Arimathea, named Joseph...", the ceremonial rite of the Taking Down from the Cross (Apokathelosis) is enacted: the sculpted figure of Christ is removed from the large Crucifix, wrapped in a pure white linen shroud, and carried into the Sanctuary. The litanies follow: "Let us all say with our whole soul...", "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin...", and "Let us complete our evening supplication unto the Lord". After the exclamation: "Blessed and glorified be the might of Thy kingdom: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", the choirs gather by the northern door of the Sanctuary and begin chanting the Aposticha. Six priests with uncovered heads (without kamilavkia), vested in phelonia and epitrachilia, carrying the Epitaphios [Plashchanytsia] upon their heads with reverence, led by the Great Archimandrite carrying the Holy Gospel Book, exit from the Sanctuary preceded by candle-bearers, processional fans [hexapteryga], the chanters, the Primikirios bearing the two-branched candlestick [divamvoulon], and deacons censing; they advance to the middle of the nave, where the decorated Tomb [Kuvuklion] stands prepared.
[^451]: Great and Holy Friday: Vespers Concluded (Entombment of the Epitaphios and Dismissal) and Great and Holy Saturday: Matins Opening (§ 84 Concluded, Matins Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p783 (printed page 768) and facing English leaf p784 (printed page 769). Paragraph 84 Concluded: The solemn procession of the Epitaphios [Plashchanytsia] reaches the center of the temple where the Tomb [Sepulchre / Kuvuklion] stands decorated. The chanters take their stations and continue chanting the Aposticha. The six priests bearing the Epitaphios upon their heads make three circuits around the Tomb, then place the Epitaphios inside it; the Great Ecclesiarch sprinkles the Epitaphios with sweet-smelling flowers. The Patriarch descends from the throne, advances to the Tomb, venerates the Holy Gospel Book and the Epitaphios, and blesses the people while the choirs sing: "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla ete); he then returns to the throne. The concelebrating Hierarchs approach two by two, venerate the Epitaphios, and receive flowers from the Patriarch, followed in turn by all the ecclesiastical officials. In the meantime, the Second Choir slowly and melodiously chants the renowned Doxastikon in Tone 5: "Glory... Both now and ever...": "Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, when Joseph with Nicodemus took Thee down from the Tree, beholding Thee dead, stripped, and unburied, he wept in sorrow and mourning, and said: Woe is me, most sweet Jesus!..." (Se ton anaballomenon to phos). Then the prayer of Saint Symeon: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word..." (Luke 2:29–32); the Reader recites the Trisagion Prayers; and the Dismissal Troparia in Tone 2 are chanted: 1. "The noble Joseph, taking down Thine undefiled Body from the Tree, wrapped It in a clean linen shroud and with sweet spices, and laid It for burial in a new tomb" (Ho euschemon Joseph) down to "and laid It for burial" (kedeusas apetheto); and 2. "Unto the myrrh-bearing women the angel stood by the tomb and cried aloud: Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ has shown Himself foreign to corruption" (Tais myrophorois gynaixi) down to "foreign to corruption" (diaphthoras edeichthe allotrios). The solemn Dismissal of Great Friday is given: "Wisdom!"; "Blessed is He Who is, Christ our God, always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen."; "Glory to Thee, O God, our hope, glory to Thee. May Christ our true God, Who for us men and for our salvation condescended to the terrible Passions and the life-giving Cross and voluntary entombment in the flesh, through the intercessions of His all-pure and blameless holy Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind", and the final benediction: "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...". Paragraph 85 Opening / Footnote 46: Great and Holy Saturday Matins (The Service of the Lamentations / Epitaphios Threnos): Heading and historical-canonical footnote. Footnote 46 records that because many faithful in urban settings experienced difficulty leaving their homes around midnight to attend the Matins service at the Seventh Hour of the night (1:00 a.m.), pastoral economy and dispensation [oikonomia] established the custom of anticipating this Matins service to the First Hour of the night (7:00 p.m. on Friday evening), coinciding with the traditional hour of the Bridegroom Matins services earlier in Holy Week. The Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is postponed until the following morning (Holy Saturday); thus the immense length of a combined nocturnal vigil is divided into two manageable liturgical celebrations.
[^452]: Great and Holy Saturday: Matins of the Lamentations Opening (The Amomos, Canon of the Holy Sabbath, and Stasis I of the Enkomia) (§ 85 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p785 (printed page 770) and facing English leaf p786 (printed page 771). Paragraph 85 Opening: Great and Holy Saturday Matins (The Lamentations at the Tomb / Epitaphios Threnos): When celebrated according to ancient monastic observance at the Seventh Hour of the night (1:00 a.m.), the Amomos (Psalm 118 LXX) and the Old Testament readings omitted from the Vespers of Great Saturday are read here (Footnote 47 notes that if Matins is anticipated at 7:00 p.m., the Amomos and readings are read in the morning before Vespers). The Priest gives the blessing: "Blessed is our God always, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages"; the Superior [Proestos] says: "Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King...", the Reader the Trisagion Prayers, the Priest "For Thine is the kingdom...", the Reader the Troparia ("Save, O Lord, Thy people..."), and the Superior recites the Six Psalms. At "God is the Lord" (Theos Kyrios), the Dismissal Troparia in Tone 2: "The noble Joseph..." until "and laid It for burial"; "Glory...": Tone 2: "When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal, then didst Thou slay Hades with the brilliance of Thy Divinity..." (Hote katelthes pros ton thanaton) in its entirety; "Both now and ever...": Tone 2: "Unto the myrrh-bearing women..." until "foreign to corruption". Small Litany, followed by the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 1: "In a clean shroud and with spices, the noble Joseph, taking down Thine all-pure Body from the Tree, wrapped It and laid It in a new tomb; and so early in the morning the myrrh-bearing women cried aloud: Show us, O Christ, Thy Resurrection!" (Sindoni kathara) once, slowly; "Glory... Both now and ever...": Tone 1: "The angelic choirs were astounded at the sight of Him Who rests in the bosom of the Father being laid in a tomb as a mortal..." (Exestesan choroi) likewise slowly. Footnote 48 notes that during this hymn the Great Archdeacon and deacons receive the blessing for the time [kairos], enter the Sanctuary, vest in their sacred vestments, and emerge holding the two-branched and three-branched candlesticks [dikirotrikira]. Psalm 50 LXX, followed by the monumental Canon of Great Saturday in Tone 6: "Of old the surge of the sea covered the pursuing tyrant beneath the waves; but the children of those who were saved bury beneath the earth Him Who was hidden: let us sing unto the Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!" (Kymati thalasses) by Mark the Monk and Saint Cosmas the Melodist for six Troparia, the Heirmos being repeated slowly at the end of each Ode as Katavasia. After Ode 3, the Sessional Hymn [Kathisma] in Tone 1: "Thy tomb, O Savior, the soldiers guarding became as dead men..." (Ton taphon sou Soter) once, slowly; Footnote 49 notes that at Ode 4 the Patriarch enters the Sanctuary and vests in complete hierarchical vestments with the concelebrating Hierarchs. After Ode 6, the Small Litany, the Kontakion in Tone 2: "He Who closed the abyss is beheld dead, and wrapped in fine linen and myrrh, the Immortal One is laid in a tomb as a mortal; and the women came to anoint Him with myrrh, weeping bitterly and crying aloud: This Sabbath is most blessed, in which Christ, having fallen asleep, shall rise again on the third day!" (Ten abysson ho kleisas); the Ikos; the Menologion; and the Triodion Memorial on the bodily burial of the Lord and His descent into Hades. Odes 7 and 8 of the Canon follow. The Deacon exclaims: "The Theotokos and Mother of the Light let us magnify in hymns!", and Ode 9 is chanted ("Weep not for Me, O Mother, beholding in the tomb the Son Whom without seed thou didst conceive in thy womb..."). At the conclusion of Ode 9, the Patriarch in full hierarchical vestments emerges from the Sanctuary preceded by the concelebrating Hierarchs; chanting the opening verse of the First Stasis of the Lamentations [Enkomia]: "Life in the tomb art Thou laid, O Christ, and the angelic hosts were astounded, glorifying Thy condescension" (He zoe en tapho), he advances toward the Epitaphios and censes it cross-wise, and afterward censes all the people. The Hierarchs, standing in choir formation, chant the verses of the First Stasis until the Patriarch returns to the throne; the opening Troparion is repeated. Small Litany with exclamation by the Patriarch: "For blessed is Thy Name, and glorified is Thy kingdom: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", and he begins the Second Stasis.
[^453]: Great and Holy Saturday: Matins of the Lamentations Continued (Stases II and III, Evlogitaria of the Resurrection, and Exaposteilarion) (§ 85 Continued). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p787 (printed page 772) and facing English leaf p788 (printed page 773). Paragraph 85 Continued: Great and Holy Saturday Matins: The Second Stasis of the Lamentations is chanted by the Patriarch and concelebrating Hierarchs: "It is right to magnify Thee, the Giver of life, Who wast lifted up upon the Cross and didst shatter the power of the enemy" (Axion esti megalynein se ton Zoodoten); at its conclusion, the first Troparion of this Stasis is repeated. The Small Litany follows, with the exclamation by the Patriarch: "For Holy art Thou, O our God, Who sittest upon the throne of glory of the Cherubim, and unto Thee we send up glory: together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most holy, good, and life-giving Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". The Patriarch and Hierarchs begin chanting the Third Stasis: "All generations offer a hymn unto Thy burial, O my Christ" (Hai geneai pasai hymnon te taphe sou prospherousi Christe mou). At the verse: "The myrrh-bearing women came early in the morning and sprinkled myrrh upon Thy tomb" (Erranan ton taphon hai Myrophoroi myra, lian proi elthousai), the Patriarch descends from the throne, receives a sprinkling vessel [kanion] from the Ecclesiarch, and singing this same Troparion (which is repeated three times), sprinkles the Epitaphios [Plashchanytsia] and the Tomb with rose-water [rhodostagma]. Upon the completion of the Third Stasis, its opening Troparion is repeated. The Small Litany follows, with the exclamation by the Patriarch: "For Thou art the King of peace, and the Savior of our souls, O Christ our God, and unto Thee we send up glory: together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine All-Holy, Good, and Life-giving Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". The Patriarch and concelebrating Hierarchs immediately begin chanting the Evlogitaria of the Resurrection in Tone 5: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach me Thy statutes! The assembly of the angels was amazed, beholding Thee numbered among the dead, yet destroying the power of death, O Savior, and with Thyself raising up Adam, and freeing all from Hades!..." (Eulogetos ei, Kyrie... Ton Angelon ho demos kateplage). When the Evlogitaria are completed, the Small Litany follows, with the exclamation by the Patriarch: "For all the powers of heaven praise Thee, and unto Thee they send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". Directly the Exaposteilarion in Tone 2 is chanted: "Holy is the Lord our God" (Hagios Kyrios ho Theos hemon) three times without additional Psalm verses. "Let every breath praise the Lord" (Psalm 150:6 LXX) and the Praises [Lauds] begin. Footnote 50 (noting Violakis's errant repetition of numeral 49) records the ceremonial action during the Praises: at the verse "Praise Him, all His angels; praise Him, all His hosts! To Thee, O God, is due a hymn!", the Patriarch descends from the throne, venerates the Epitaphios, blesses the congregation, and returns to the throne; while the remaining verses of the Praises are chanted, the concelebrating Hierarchs advance two by two, venerate the Epitaphios, and return to their stalls.
[^454]: Great and Holy Saturday: Matins Concluded (Praises Concluded, Solemn Epitaphios Procession, Prophecy, Epistle, Gospel, and Dismissal) (§ 85 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p789 (printed page 774) and facing English leaf p790 (printed page 775). Paragraph 85 Concluded: The service of Great Saturday Matins concludes: At the Praises, four Stichera Idiomela are chanted: 1. "Today the tomb holds Him Who holds creation in the palm of His hand; a stone covers Him Who covered the heavens with virtue; Life sleeps and Hades trembles, and Adam is loosed from his bonds..." (Semeron synechei taphos); 2. "What is this sight that we behold? What is this present rest? The King of the ages, having accomplished the dispensation through His Passion, keeps Sabbath in the tomb..." (Ti to horomenon theama); 3. "Come, let us behold our Life lying in the tomb, that He may give life unto those lying in the tombs..." (Deute idomen ten zoen hemon); 4. "Joseph asked for the Body of Jesus, and laid It in his own new tomb; for it was fitting that He should come forth from a tomb as from a bridal chamber..." (Etesato Joseph to soma tou Iesou). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 2: "This present day Moses mystically prefigured, saying: And God blessed the seventh day; for this is the blessed Sabbath, this is the day of rest, whereon the Only-Begotten Son of God rested from all His works..." (Ten semeron mystikos ho megas Moyses prodietypouto legon); "Both now and ever...": Theotokion in Tone 2: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos, for through Him Who became incarnate of thee, Hades is bound, Adam is recalled, the curse is annulled, Eve is set free, death is put to death, and we are given life..." (Hypereulogemene hyparcheis). The Great Doxology is chanted; at its conclusion, the slow melismatic Asmatikon: "Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us" (Hagios ho Theos) is sung by the choirs as the solemn Procession of the Epitaphios [Plashchanytsia] departs. Order of procession: the priests lead the way, followed by the chanters, and after them the Patriarch and concelebrating Hierarchs bearing the Epitaphios upon their heads; the procession exits through the western doors of the church and circles around the perimeter before returning. Upon re-entering the temple, the Patriarch enters the Sanctuary and proclaims: "Let us attend! Peace be unto all! Wisdom!". He chants the Dismissal Troparion in Tone 2: "When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal...", censing around the Holy Table processing before the Epitaphios; then the Troparia: "Unto the myrrh-bearing women..." and "The noble Joseph..."; the priests then reverently lay the Epitaphios upon the Holy Altar Table (Footnote 51* notes that ancient Typika prescribed only "The noble Joseph", while later custom prefixed the two Resurrectional Troparia). The Reader outside the iconostasis recites the Troparion of the Prophecy in Tone 2: "O Christ, Who holdest fast the ends of the earth, Thou didst deign to be held fast in a narrow tomb, that Thou mightest deliver mankind from the fall of Hades, and raising us up, immortalize us as the immortal God!" (Ho synechon ta perata); the Prokimenon in Tone 4: "Arise, O Lord, help us, and redeem us for Thy Name's sake!" (Ps. 43:26 LXX); the Prophecy: Ezekiel 37:1–14 LXX (The Vision of the Valley of Dry Bones: "Thus saith the Lord God: Behold, I will open your graves, O My people, and cause you to come up out of your graves, and bring you into the land of Israel"); the Epistle: 1 Corinthians 5:6–8 and Galatians 3:13–14 (Purging out the Old Leaven, Christ Our Passover Sacrificed for Us, and Redemption from the Curse of the Law); and the Gospel: Matthew 27:62–66 (The Chief Priests and Pharisees Seal the Sepulchre and Set the Guard) read by the Patriarch before the Royal Doors. After "Many years to you, Master!" (Eis polla ete), the Patriarch unvests from his hierarchical vestments (Footnote 52* notes that if Matins was anticipated at 7:00 p.m., the Patriarch does not unvest, but remains in the Sanctuary until the end to pronounce the Dismissal of the section), puts on his monastic mandya, takes his pastoral staff, and ascends the throne. The Archdeacon recites the Augmented Litany ("Let us all say with our whole soul...") and the Morning Litany ("Let us complete our morning prayer unto the Lord"). The Priest exclaims: "For Thine it is to show mercy and to save us, O Christ our God, and unto Thee we send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.", and gives the Dismissal: "May Christ, our true God, Who for us men and for our salvation voluntarily endured His terrible Passions, the Cross, and voluntary entombment in the flesh, through the intercessions of His all-pure and blameless holy Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind", followed by "Through the prayers of our holy fathers...".
[^455]: Great and Holy Saturday: Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great Opening (§ 86 Opening). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p791 (printed page 776) and facing English leaf p792 (printed page 777). Rubrics of Paragraph 86: The Archdeacon exclaims: "Bless, Master!" (Eulogeson Despota); the Priest intones the Kingdom Doxology: "Blessed is the kingdom of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen." (Eulogemene he basileia tou Patros); and the Patriarch recites the Introductory Psalm [Psalm 103 LXX: "Bless the Lord, O my soul..."] plainly. At "Lord, I have cried" (Kyrie ekekraxa), eight stichoi are set, and the choirs chant four Resurrectional Stichera in Tone 1 and four Idiomela of the day in Tone 8: 1. Tone 1: "Accept our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins, for Thou alone art He Who hast manifested the Resurrection unto the world" (Tas hesperinas hemon euchas); 2. Tone 1: "Surround Zion, O ye peoples, and encompass her, and give glory within her unto Him Who is risen from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered us from our iniquities" (Kyklosate laoi Sion); 3. Tone 1: "Come, ye peoples, let us hymn and worship Christ, glorifying His Resurrection from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered the world from the error of the enemy" (Deute laoi hymnesomen); 4. Tone 1: "By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions, and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption: O Lord, glory to Thee!" (To pathei sou Christe); 5. & 6. Tone 8: "Today Hades groaning cries aloud: Woe is me, if only I had not received Him Who was born of Mary; for coming upon me He has dissolved my power, and broken the gates of bronze; as God, He has raised up the souls that I held fast of old! Glory to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection, O Lord!" (Semeron ho hades stenon boa) (twice); 7. Tone 8: "Today Hades groaning cries aloud: My power is dissolved; I received a dead Man as one of the dead, but could not hold Him at all, for with Him I lose those over whom I had ruled; all those whom I held fast for ages I have vomited forth! But He Who was crucified has raised up the dead! Glory to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection, O Lord!" (Semeron ho hades stenon boa: Katelythe mou he exousia); 8. Tone 8: "Today Hades groaning cries aloud: My dominion is swallowed up; the Shepherd has been crucified, and has raised up Adam; those over whom I reigned are taken from me; whom I swallowed down in my might, I have surrendered utterly; the Crucified One has emptied the graves; the might of death has no power! Glory to Thy Cross and Thy Resurrection, O Lord!" (Semeron ho hades stenon boa: Katepothe mou to kratos). "Glory...": Doxastikon in Tone 6: "Moses the great mystically prefigured this present day, saying: And God blessed the seventh day; for this is the blessed Sabbath, this is the day of rest, whereon the Only-Begotten Son of God rested from all His works..." (Ten semeron mystikos ho megas Moyses); "Both now and ever...": Dogmatic Theotokion in Tone 1: "Let us praise the Virgin Mary, the glory of the whole world, who sprang forth from mankind and gave birth to the Master, the gate of heaven..." (Ten pankosmion doxan). The Entrance is made with the Holy Gospel Book; "O Gladsome Light" (Phos hilaron) is chanted; there is no Prokimenon, but directly the three Old Testament Readings are proclaimed: 1. Genesis 1:1–13 LXX (The Creation of the World: Heaven and Earth, the Light, the Firmament, the Earth, the Seas, and Plant Life); 2. Jonah 1:1–4:11 LXX (The Entire Book of the Prophet Jonah: The Three Days and Three Nights Prefiguring the Burial and Resurrection of the Lord); 3. Daniel 3:1–23 LXX (The Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian Furnace of Fire), followed by the Prayer of Azariah [Daniel 3:26–45 LXX / Apocrypha]: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy Name unto the ages...", and the Hymn of the Three Youths [Daniel 3:52–90 LXX / Song of the Three Holy Children] through verse 56: "Blessed art Thou in the firmament of heaven, and praised and glorified unto the ages" (Eulogemenos ei en to stereomati tou ouranou). At this point, all stand and begin chanting in Tone 1 the remainder of the Hymn of the Three Youths [from verse 57]: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; hymn and exalt Him above all forever!" (Ton Kyrion hymneite kai hyperypsoute eis pantas tous aionas), with the Canonarch intoning the intermittent verses through verse 88: "Bless the Lord, Hananiah, Azariah, and Mishael; hymn and exalt Him above all forever!", the choir repeating after each verse the refrain: "Hymn and exalt Him above all forever!" (Hyperypsoute eis pantas tous aionas), concluding with the verses for the Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, and the final Doxology: "We bless the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit: the Lord!" (Eulogoumen Patera, Yion kai Hagion Pneuma, ton Kyrion).
[^456]: Great and Holy Saturday: Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great Continued (§ 86 Continued). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p793 (printed page 778) and facing English leaf p794 (printed page 779). Concluding cadences of the Hymn of the Three Youths: "We hymn, we bless, and we worship the Lord! Hymn and exalt Him above all forever!". The Little Litany is recited by the Deacon, with the Priest's exclamation: "For Holy art Thou, our God, and unto Thee we send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and forever, and unto the ages of ages. Amen.". Instead of the Trisagion Hymn, the choirs chant the Baptismal Hymn: "As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia" (Hosoi eis Christon ebaptisthete, Christon enedysasthe. Allelouia, Gal. 3:27). The Epistle of the day is proclaimed: Romans 6:3–11 (Baptized into Christ's Death, Buried with Him through Baptism, That as Christ was Raised from the Dead We Also Should Walk in Newness of Life). At the conclusion of the Epistle, "Alleluia" is omitted. Instead, the Priest emerges from within the Sanctuary and intones in Tone 7 (Grave Tone) the triumphant Verse: "Arise, O God, judge the earth; for Thou shalt inherit among all the nations!" (Anasta ho Theos krinon ten gen, hoti sy katakleronomeseis en pasi tois ethnesi, Ps. 81:8 LXX), while proceeding down the solea and nave, scattering fragrant laurel leaves (bay leaves) throughout the entire temple as an epigraphic symbol of the Lord's victory over death and the shattering of Hades. The choirs repeat this verse after each of the six stichoi of Psalm 81 LXX intoned by the Canonarch: 1. "God standeth in the assembly of gods; in the midst He judgeth gods" (Ps. 81:1 LXX); 2. "How long will ye judge unjustly, and accept the persons of the wicked?" (Ps. 81:2 LXX); 3. "Judge the orphan and the poor; do justice to the humble and needy" (Ps. 81:3 LXX); 4. "Rescue the poor and needy; deliver them out of the hand of the sinner" (Ps. 81:4 LXX); 5. "They have not known, neither have they understood; they walk on in darkness; let all the foundations of the earth be shaken" (Ps. 81:5 LXX); 6. "I said: Ye are gods, and all of you the children of the Most High; but ye die as men, and fall as one of the princes" (Ps. 81:6–7 LXX). After the final stichos, "Arise, O God" is chanted slowly and majestically. The Holy Gospel: Matthew 28:1–20 (The Resurrection of Christ, the appearance of the angel to the women, and the Great Commission: "In the end of the Sabbath, as it began to dawn toward the first day of the week..." until "even unto the end of the world") is proclaimed from the ambo. The Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great continues in its regular order. Instead of the customary Cherubic Hymn, the ancient Liturgical Troparion of the Liturgy of Saint James in Tone 5 is chanted: "Let all mortal flesh keep silence, and stand with fear and trembling, and ponder nothing earthly within itself; for the King of kings and Lord of lords cometh forth to be slain, and to be given as food to the faithful. Before Him go the choirs of angels with every principality and power," [during which the solemn Great Entrance is made with the Holy Gifts] "the many-eyed Cherubim and the six-winged Seraphim, covering their faces and singing the hymn: Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia!" (Sigasato pasa sarx broteia).
[^457]: Great and Holy Saturday: Vespers with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great Concluded (§ 86 Concluded). Detailed epigraphic synthesis of physical leaf p795 (printed page 780) and facing English leaf p796 (printed page 781). Concluding cadences of the Cherubic Troparion "Let all mortal flesh keep silence": "...covering their faces and crying aloud the hymn: Alleluia, Alleluia, Alleluia!" [thrice]. The Communion Hymn is chanted: "The Lord awoke as if from sleep, and is risen saving us. Alleluia" (Exegethe hos ho hypnon Kyrios, kai aneste sozon hemas, Allelouia, Ps. 77:65 LXX). Instead of "We have seen the true light" (Eidomen to phos), the choirs chant the Troparion in Tone 2 in a heirmologic melody: "Remember us also, O compassionate One, even as Thou didst remember the thief in the Kingdom of Heaven" (Mnesthiti eusplanchnos kai hemon, kathos emnemoneusas tou Lestou en te Basileia ton Ouranon). The Thanksgiving Hymn: "Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth and forevermore" (Eie to onoma Kyriou) is sung three times. The solemn Dismissal of the Divine Liturgy is given with the Resurrectional incipit: "May He Who rose from the dead, Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His all-pure and blameless holy Mother, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, for He is good and loves mankind" (Ho anastas ek nekron, Christos ho alethinos Theos hemon...), because the Vespers of Great and Holy Sunday [Pascha] has already been celebrated in anticipation prior to the Liturgy. Violakis notes the foundational canonical reference to General Provisions [Protheoria], Paragraph 16, and the official Report of the Typikon Committee published in the Preface of the 1888 Typikon.
